\displaystyle=\mathbb{E}r(V_{\mathit{1}},X)\mu_{\mathcal{X}}(V_{\mathit{1}},X)-\mathbb{E}r(V_{\mathit{1}},X)\mu_{\mathcal{X}}(V_{\mathit{1}},X)=0,

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}\mathbbm{1}_{V_{\mathit{2}}=c}(g(V,X)-\gamma_{\mathcal{V}}(c))=\mathbb{E}\left[\mathbbm{1}_{V_{\mathit{2}}=c}g(V,X)\right]-p_{V_{\mathit{2}}}(c)\gamma_{\mathcal{V}}(c)

\displaystyle=p_{V_{\mathit{2}}}(c)\mathbb{E}\left[\left.g(V,X)\,\right|\,V_{\mathit{2}}=c\right]-p_{V_{\mathit{2}}}(c)\gamma_{\mathcal{V}}(c)=0,

because
V_{\mathit{2}}
is distributed on a finite set. Hence, by the definition of
\chi(P_{VX})
in (
T
),
\mathbb{E}\tilde{\chi}=0
. Because
r\in L_{2}(P_{V_{\mathit{1}}X})
, (
C.I
) implies that
\tilde{\chi}
is in the tangent set
L_{2}^{0}(P_{VX})
of the nonparametric model.

Consider now a regular submodel
t\mapsto P_{VX,t}
for
t\in\real
in the neighbourhood of zero
with
P_{VX,0}=P_{VX}
and the property that
\frac{\mathrm{d}}{\mathrm{d}t}\big|_{t=0}P_{VX,t}(\mathrm{d}v,\mathrm{d}x)=s(v,x)P_{VX}(\mathrm{d}v,\mathrm{d}x)
for the score function
s
running through the tangent set
L_{2}^{0}(P_{VX})
; for example
\mathrm{d}P_{VX}=\exp(ts)(P_{VX}\exp(ts))^{-1}\mathrm{d}P_{VX}
as in
39
. See
10
for regular submodels. For (
4
), it suffices to show
\frac{\mathrm{d}}{\mathrm{d}t}\big|_{t=0}\chi(P_{VX,t})=\mathbb{E}\tilde{\chi}s
. Assuming that differentiation and expectation commutes, we have

\displaystyle\frac{\mathrm{d}}{\mathrm{d}t}\big|_{t=0}\chi(P_{VX,t})=

\displaystyle\,\frac{\mathrm{d}}{\mathrm{d}t}\big|_{t=0}\int_{\mathfrak{V}\times\mathfrak{X}}f(v,x,\mu_{\mathcal{X},t},\gamma_{\mathcal{V},t}(c))\,\mathrm{d}P_{VX,t}(v,x)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\,\int_{\mathfrak{V}\times\mathfrak{X}}\frac{\mathrm{d}}{\mathrm{d}t}\big|_{t=0}f(v,x,\mu_{\mathcal{X},t},\gamma_{\mathcal{V},t}(c))\,\mathrm{d}P_{VX}(v,x)

\displaystyle+\mathbb{E}f(V,X,\mu_{\mathcal{X}},\gamma_{\mathcal{V}}(c))s(V,X).

Here,

\displaystyle\int_{\mathfrak{V}\times\mathfrak{X}}\frac{\mathrm{d}}{\mathrm{d}t}\big|_{t=0}f(v,x,\mu_{\mathcal{X},t},\gamma_{\mathcal{V},t}(c))\,\mathrm{d}P_{VX}(v,x)

\displaystyle=\frac{\mathrm{d}}{\mathrm{d}t}\big|_{t=0}\mathbb{E}f(V,X,\mu_{\mathcal{X},t},\gamma_{\mathcal{V}}(c))+\frac{\mathrm{d}}{\mathrm{d}t}\big|_{t=0}\mathbb{E}f(V,X,\mu_{\mathcal{X}},\gamma_{\mathcal{V},t}(c)).

By (
3
),
\mathbb{E}f(V,X,\mu_{\mathcal{X},t},\gamma_{\mathcal{V}}(c))=\mathbb{E}r(V_{\mathit{1}},X)\mu_{\mathcal{X},t}(V_{\mathit{1}},X)
. Taking derivatives, the properties of (conditional) expectation give

\displaystyle\begin{aligned} \frac{\mathrm{d}}{\mathrm{d}t}\big|_{t=0}\mu_{\mathcal{X},t}(v_{\mathit{1}},x)&=\mathbb{E}\left[\left.(m(V,X)-\mu_{\mathcal{X}}(v_{\mathit{1}},x))s(V,X)\,\right|\,V_{\mathit{1}}=v_{\mathit{1}},X=x\right],\\
\frac{\mathrm{d}}{\mathrm{d}t}\big|_{t=0}\gamma_{\mathcal{V},t}(c)&=\mathbb{E}\left[\left.(g(V,X)-\gamma_{\mathcal{V}}(c))s(V,X)\,\right|\,V_{\mathit{2}}=c\right]\\
&=\mathbb{E}\frac{\mathbbm{1}_{V_{\mathit{2}}=c}}{p_{V_{\mathit{2}}}(c)}(g(V,X)-\gamma_{\mathcal{V}}(c))s(V,X).\end{aligned}

(55)

This can be seen in a few steps. First, given a collection of coordinates
V_{\mathit{j}}
of
V
, we can assume without loss of generality that
V=(V_{\mathit{j}},V_{-\mathit{j}})
for another collection of coordinates
V_{-\mathit{j}}
of
V
. Second,
P_{V_{-\mathit{j}}{|\,}V_{\mathit{j}}X}(B{|\,}v_{\mathit{j}},x)=\frac{\mathrm{d}P_{V_{-\mathit{j}}V_{\mathit{j}}X}(B,\cdot,\cdot)}{\mathrm{d}P_{V_{\mathit{j}}X}}(v_{\mathit{j}},x)
can be verified to be the conditional distribution of
V_{-\mathit{j}}
given
(V_{\mathit{j}},X)=(v_{\mathit{j}},x)
as in the proof of
Lemma

4
, noting that
P_{V_{-\mathit{j}}V_{\mathit{j}}X}(B,\cdot,\cdot)\ll P_{V_{\mathit{j}}X}
for all
B\in\mathscr{F}_{{\mathfrak{V}}_{\mathit{j}}}
. Third, if
\pi_{t}\coloneqq\frac{\mathrm{d}\lambda_{t}}{\mathrm{d}\nu_{t}}
for valid submodels
t\mapsto(\lambda_{t},\nu_{t})
of measures, then
\partial_{t}\pi_{t}=\frac{\mathrm{d}\partial_{t}\lambda_{t}}{\mathrm{d}\nu_{t}}-(\frac{\mathrm{d}\lambda_{t}}{\mathrm{d}\nu_{t}})(\frac{\mathrm{d}\partial_{t}\nu_{t}}{\mathrm{d}\nu_{t}})
provided the densities on the right are well defined. Fourth, the given submodel
t\mapsto P_{VX,t}
induces the marginal
\mathrm{d}P_{V_{\mathit{j}}X,t}(v_{\mathit{j}},x)=\int_{\mathfrak{V}_{\mathit{j}}}s(v_{-\mathit{j}},v_{\mathit{j}},x)P_{V_{-\mathit{j}}V_{\mathit{j}}X}(\mathrm{d}v_{-\mathit{j}},\mathrm{d}v_{\mathit{j}},\mathrm{d}x)
.
Finally, apply these steps to obtain

\displaystyle\frac{\mathrm{d}\partial_{t}P_{V_{-\mathit{j}},V_{\mathit{j}},X,t}(\mathrm{d}v_{-\mathit{j}},\cdot,\cdot)\big|_{t=0}}{\mathrm{d}P_{V_{\mathit{j}}X}}(v_{\mathit{j}},x)=s(v_{-\mathit{j}},v_{\mathit{j}},x)P_{V_{-\mathit{j}}{|\,}V_{\mathit{j}}X}(\mathrm{d}v_{-\mathit{j}}{|\,}v_{\mathit{j}},x),

\displaystyle\frac{\mathrm{d}\partial_{t}P_{V_{\mathit{j}}X,t}\big|_{t=0}}{\mathrm{d}P_{V_{\mathit{j}X}}}(v_{\mathit{j}},x)=\int s(v_{-\mathit{j}},v_{\mathit{j}},x)P_{V_{-\mathit{j}}{|\,}V_{\mathit{j}}X}(\mathrm{d}v_{-\mathit{j}}{|\,}v_{\mathit{j}},x).

The display (
55
) then implies

\displaystyle\frac{\mathrm{d}}{\mathrm{d}t}\big|_{t=0}\mathbb{E}r(V_{\mathit{1}},X)\mu_{\mathcal{X},t}(V_{\mathit{1}},X)

\displaystyle=\mathbb{E}\left[r(V_{\mathit{1}},X)\mathbb{E}\left[\left.\left(m(V,X)-\mu_{\mathcal{X}}(V_{\mathit{1}},X)\right)s(V,X)\,\right|\,V_{\mathit{1}},X\right]\right]

\displaystyle=\mathbb{E}r(V_{\mathit{1}},X)\left(m(V,X)-\mu_{\mathcal{X}}(V_{\mathit{1}},X)\right)s(V,X),

\displaystyle\frac{\mathrm{d}}{\mathrm{d}t}\big|_{t=0}\mathbb{E}f(V,X,\mu_{\mathcal{X}},\gamma_{\mathcal{V},t}(c))=\mathbb{E}\left[\partial_{\gamma}f(V,X,\mu_{\mathcal{X}},\gamma_{\mathcal{V}}(c))\frac{\mathrm{d}}{\mathrm{d}t}\big|_{t=0}\gamma_{\mathcal{V},t}(c)\right]

\displaystyle=\mathbb{E}\left[\partial_{\gamma}f(V,X,\mu_{\mathcal{X}},\gamma_{\mathcal{V}}(c))\mathbb{E}\left[\frac{\mathbbm{1}_{V_{\mathit{2}}=c}}{p_{V_{\mathit{2}}}(c)}(g(V,X)-\gamma_{\mathcal{V}}(c))s(V,X)\right]\right]

\displaystyle=\mathbb{E}\left[\mathbb{E}\left[\partial_{\gamma}f(V,X,\mu_{\mathcal{X}},\gamma_{\mathcal{V}}(c))\right]\frac{\mathbbm{1}_{V_{\mathit{2}}=c}}{p_{V_{\mathit{2}}}(c)}(g(V,X)-\gamma_{\mathcal{V}}(c))s(V,X)\right].

Hence,

\displaystyle\frac{\mathrm{d}}{\mathrm{d}t}\big|_{t=0}\chi(P_{VX,t})=\frac{\mathrm{d}}{\mathrm{d}t}\big|_{t=0}\mathbb{E}f(V,X,\mu_{\mathcal{X},t},\gamma_{\mathcal{V}}(c))+\frac{\mathrm{d}}{\mathrm{d}t}\big|_{t=0}\mathbb{E}f(V,X,\mu_{\mathcal{X}},\gamma_{\mathcal{V},t}(c))

\displaystyle+\mathbb{E}\left[f(V,X,\mu_{\mathcal{X}},\gamma_{\mathcal{V}}(c))s(V,X)\right]

\displaystyle=\mathbb{E}r(V_{\mathit{1}},X)\left(m(V,X)-\mu_{\mathcal{X}}(V_{\mathit{1}},X)\right)s(V,X)

\displaystyle+\mathbb{E}\left[\mathbb{E}\left[\partial_{\gamma}f(V,X,\mu_{\mathcal{X}},\gamma_{\mathcal{V}}(c))\right]\frac{\mathbbm{1}_{V_{\mathit{2}}=c}}{p_{V_{\mathit{2}}}(c)}(g(V,X)-\gamma_{\mathcal{V}}(c))s(V,X)\right]

\displaystyle+\mathbb{E}\left[f(V,X,\mu_{\mathcal{X}},\gamma_{\mathcal{V}}(c))s(V,X)\right]

\displaystyle=\mathbb{E}\tilde{\chi}(V,X)s(V,X),

where the last equality follows from
\mathbb{E}s(V,X)=0
.
∎

Proof of
Theorem

1
.

First,

\displaystyle P_{VX}\big[-r(V_{\mathit{1}},X)h(V_{\mathit{1}},X)+f(V,X,h,\gamma_{\mathcal{V}}(c))\big]

\displaystyle=0,

(56)

\displaystyle P_{VX}\big[(m(V,X)-\mu_{\mathcal{X}}(V_{\mathit{1}},X))h(V_{\mathit{1}},X)\big]

\displaystyle=0

(57)

for all
h\in L_{2}(P_{V_{\mathit{1}}X})
, where the first equality is by (
3
) and the second is by the definition of
\mu_{\mathcal{X}}
and the tower property of expectation. Then, because
\tilde{\chi}
is an influence function satisfying
P_{VX}\tilde{\chi}=0
, and
\tilde{\chi},\chi_{0}
— not depending on
(v,x)
— are constants with respect to
P_{VX}
-integration,

\displaystyle\chi^{\prime}-\chi_{0}+P_{VX}\tilde{\chi}^{\prime}=P_{VX}\left[\chi^{\prime}+\tilde{\chi}^{\prime}\right]-P_{VX}\left[\chi_{0}+\tilde{\chi}\right]

\displaystyle=P_{VX}\left\{r^{\prime}(V_{\mathit{1}},X)(m(V,X)-\mu_{\mathcal{X}}^{\prime}(V_{\mathit{1}},X))+\frac{\mathbbm{1}_{V_{\mathit{2}}=c}}{p_{V_{\mathit{2}}}^{\prime}(c)}(g(V,X)-\gamma_{\mathcal{V}}^{\prime}(c))e^{\prime\prime}\right.

\displaystyle+\left.\vphantom{\frac{\mathbbm{1}_{V_{\mathit{2}}=c}}{p_{V_{\mathit{2}}}^{\prime}(c)}}f(V,X,\mu_{\mathcal{X}}^{\prime},\gamma_{\mathcal{V}}^{\prime}(c))\right\}

\displaystyle-P_{VX}\left\{r(V_{\mathit{1}},X)(m(V,X)-\mu_{\mathcal{X}}(V_{\mathit{1}},X))\vphantom{\frac{\mathbbm{1}_{V_{\mathit{2}}=c}}{p_{V_{\mathit{2}}}(c)}}\right.

\displaystyle+\left.\frac{\mathbbm{1}_{V_{\mathit{2}}=c}}{p_{V_{\mathit{2}}}(c)}(g(V,X)-\gamma_{\mathcal{V}}(c))P_{VX}\partial_{\gamma}f(V,X,\mu_{\mathcal{X}},\gamma_{\mathcal{V}}(c))+\vphantom{\frac{\mathbbm{1}_{V_{\mathit{2}}=c}}{p_{V_{\mathit{2}}}(c)}}f(V,X,\mu_{\mathcal{X}},\gamma_{\mathcal{V}}(c))\right\}

\displaystyle-P_{VX}\left\{-r(V_{\mathit{1}},X)(\mu_{\mathcal{X}}^{\prime}(V_{\mathit{1}},X)-\mu_{\mathcal{X}}(V_{\mathit{1}},X))\right.

\displaystyle\left.+f(V,X,\mu_{\mathcal{X}}^{\prime},\gamma_{\mathcal{V}}(c))-f(V,X,\mu_{\mathcal{X}},\gamma_{\mathcal{V}}(c))\right\}

\displaystyle-P_{VX}\left\{\left[m(V,X)-\mu_{\mathcal{X}}(V_{\mathit{1}},X)\right]\left[r^{\prime}(V_{\mathit{1}},X)-r(V_{\mathit{1}},X)\right]\right\},

where the last two expectations are zero: we apply (
56
) and (
57
) choosing
h
to be
\mu_{\mathcal{X}}^{\prime}-\mu_{\mathcal{X}}
and
r^{\prime}-r
, respectively, and use the linearity of
f
in (
C.L
). As
P_{VX}\frac{\mathbbm{1}_{V_{\mathit{2}}=c}}{p_{V_{\mathit{2}}}(c)}(g(V,X)-\gamma_{\mathcal{V}}(c))=0
by the tower property and the definition of
\gamma_{\mathcal{V}}
,

\displaystyle\chi^{\prime}-\chi_{0}+P_{VX}\tilde{\chi}^{\prime}=-P_{VX}(r-r^{\prime})(\mu_{\mathcal{X}}-\mu_{\mathcal{X}}^{\prime})

\displaystyle+P_{VX}\left\{\frac{\mathbbm{1}_{V_{\mathit{2}}=c}}{p_{V_{\mathit{2}}}^{\prime}(c)}(g(V,X)-\gamma_{\mathcal{V}}^{\prime}(c))e^{\prime\prime}\right.

\displaystyle\left.\phantom{\frac{\mathbbm{1}_{V_{\mathit{2}}=c}}{p_{V_{\mathit{2}}}^{\prime}(c)}}+f(V,X,\mu_{\mathcal{X}}^{\prime},\gamma_{\mathcal{V}}^{\prime}(c))-f(V,X,\mu_{\mathcal{X}}^{\prime},\gamma_{\mathcal{V}}(c))\right\}.

Next, a Taylor-approximation of
\gamma\mapsto f(V,X,\mu_{\mathcal{X}}^{\prime},\gamma)
by (
C.D
) with a mean-value representation of the the remainder term gives

\displaystyle f(V,X,\mu_{\mathcal{X}}^{\prime},\gamma_{\mathcal{V}}(c))=

\displaystyle\,f(V,X,\mu_{\mathcal{X}}^{\prime},\gamma_{\mathcal{V}}^{\prime}(c))+\partial_{\gamma}f(V,X,\mu_{\mathcal{X}}^{\prime},\gamma_{\mathcal{V}}^{\prime}(c))\big[\gamma_{\mathcal{V}}(c)-\gamma_{\mathcal{V}}^{\prime}(c)\big]

\displaystyle+\frac{1}{2}\partial_{\gamma}^{2}f(V,X,\mu_{\mathcal{X}}^{\prime},\widetilde{\gamma_{\mathcal{V}}(c)})\big[\gamma_{\mathcal{V}}(c)-\gamma_{\mathcal{V}}^{\prime}(c)\big]^{2}.

As
P_{VX}\mathbbm{1}_{V_{\mathit{2}}=c}(g(V,X)-\gamma_{\mathcal{V}}^{\prime}(c))=p_{V_{\mathit{2}}}(c)(\gamma_{\mathcal{V}}(c)-\gamma_{\mathcal{V}}^{\prime}(c))
,

\displaystyle P_{VX}\left\{\frac{\mathbbm{1}_{V_{\mathit{2}}=c}}{p_{V_{\mathit{2}}}^{\prime}(c)}(g(V,X)-\gamma_{\mathcal{V}}^{\prime}(c))e^{\prime\prime}+f(V,X,\mu_{\mathcal{X}}^{\prime},\gamma_{\mathcal{V}}^{\prime}(c))-f(V,X,\mu_{\mathcal{X}}^{\prime},\gamma_{\mathcal{V}}(c))\right\}

\displaystyle=\frac{p_{V_{\mathit{2}}}(c)}{p_{V_{\mathit{2}}}^{\prime}(c)}(\gamma_{\mathcal{V}}(c)-\gamma_{\mathcal{V}}^{\prime}(c))e^{\prime\prime}-P_{VX}\partial_{\gamma}f(V,X,\mu_{\mathcal{X}}^{\prime},\gamma_{\mathcal{V}}^{\prime}(c))\big[\gamma_{\mathcal{V}}(c)-\gamma_{\mathcal{V}}^{\prime}(c)\big]

\displaystyle-\frac{1}{2}P_{VX}\partial_{\gamma}^{2}f(V,X,\mu_{\mathcal{X}}^{\prime},\widetilde{\gamma_{\mathcal{V}}(c)})\big[\gamma_{\mathcal{V}}(c)-\gamma_{\mathcal{V}}^{\prime}(c)\big]^{2},

which yields the assertion by the definition of
e^{\prime}
.
∎

Appendix C
Privacy

This section contains complementary results to and proofs of the claims in
Section

4
and
Section

5
.
Section

C.1
complements

Section

4
by deriving the tangent set of the private model, and proves
Theorem

2
.
Section

C.2
proves
Section

5
’s
Corollary

1
. Our proofs rest on the auxiliary results
Lemmas

3
,
4
and
5
in
Section

C.3
, proven there.

Finally, in
Section

C.4
, the relation between total-variation and differential privacy is quantified, while
Section

C.5
discusses alternative choices for invertible privacy mechanisms in the sense of (
9
).

C.1.  Private Inferential Properties

This section complements
Section

4
by deriving the tangent set of the private model, and proves
Theorem

2
.

Lemma

1
shows that the tangent set of the model
\mathcal{P}_{VZ}(Q,\mathcal{P}_{VX})
is

\mathcal{T}_{VZ}(Q,\mathcal{P}_{VX})=Q_{\mathcal{X}}^{*}\mathcal{T}_{VX}=\left\{(v,z)\mapsto\mathbb{E}\left[\left.s(V,X)\,\right|\,V=v,Z=z\right]:s\in\mathcal{T}_{VX}\right\},

which is typical of mixture models such as
P_{VZ}
in (
7
) (e.g.
48
), and is also in agreement with
46
.

Lemma

1
also shows that
\mathcal{P}_{VZ}(Q,\mathcal{P}_{\mathfrak{V}\mathfrak{X}})
remains nonparametric if
Q_{\mathcal{X}}
is invertible, and when
X
is discrete with
|\mathfrak{X}|=|\mathfrak{Z}|
, this holds with “if and only if.”

Lemma 1

(Private Semiparametric Properties)
.

Let
\mathcal{Q}_{J},\mathcal{Q}^{\mathrm{I}}_{J},\mathcal{Q}_{\delta}
be the sets of mechanism defined in (
13
), (
14
), (
16
), respectively. Then the tangent set
\mathcal{T}_{VZ}(Q,\mathcal{P}_{VX})
at
P_{VZ}
in the model (
8
) is as follows.

(i)

Let
Q\in\mathcal{Q}(\mathfrak{X}\to\mathfrak{Z})
be arbitrary. Then
\mathcal{T}_{VZ}(Q,\mathcal{P}_{VX})=\left\{Q_{\mathcal{X}}^{*}s:s\in\mathcal{T}_{VX}\right\}
for the tangent set
\mathcal{T}_{VX}\subset L_{2}^{0}(P_{VX})
at
P_{VX}
in any model
\mathcal{P}_{VX}
, and
Q_{\mathcal{X}}^{*}
in
Lemma

3
.

(ii)

Suppose that
\mathcal{T}_{VX}=L_{2}^{0}(P_{VX})
and
Q\in\mathcal{Q}_{J}
. Then
\mathcal{T}_{VZ}(Q,\mathcal{P}_{\mathfrak{V}\mathfrak{X}})=L_{2}^{0}(P_{VZ})
if and only if
Q\in\mathcal{Q}^{\mathrm{I}}_{J}
.

(iii)

Suppose that
\mathcal{T}_{VX}=L_{2}^{0}(P_{VX})
and
Q\in\mathcal{Q}_{\delta}
. Then the closure of
\mathcal{T}_{VZ}(Q,\mathcal{P}_{\mathfrak{V}\mathfrak{X}})
in
L_{2}(P_{VZ})
is
L_{2}^{0}(P_{VZ})
.

Proof of
Lemma

1
.

Assertion
(i)
. Consider the submodel
t\mapsto\mathrm{d}P_{VX,t}=e^{ts}(P_{VX}e^{ts})^{-1}\mathrm{d}P_{VX}
for
s\in\mathcal{T}_{VX}\subset L_{2}^{0}(P_{VX})
(e.g.
39
). Clearly,
P_{VX,t}\ll P_{VX}
for all
t
. This submodel is differentiable in the quadratic mean with score
s
(
10
): under
P_{VX,t}\ll P_{VX}
,

\displaystyle\int\left[\frac{1}{t}\left(\sqrt{\frac{\mathrm{d}P_{VX,t}}{{\mathrm{d}P_{VX}}}}-1\right)-\frac{1}{2}s\right]^{2}\,\mathrm{d}P_{VX}\to 0\,\text{ as }t\to 0.

(58)

The submodel
P_{VX,t}
induces the submodel
P_{VZ,t}\coloneqq\int_{(\cdot)\times\mathfrak{X}}Q(\cdot{|\,}x)\,\mathrm{d}P_{VX,t}(v,x)
for
P_{VZ}=\int_{(\cdot)\times\mathfrak{X}}Q(\cdot{|\,}x)\,\mathrm{d}P_{VX}(v,x)
because
Q
is known, following the construction (
7
). Thus,
P_{VZ,t}\ll P_{VZ}
for all
t
. For the assertion, it is then necessary and sufficient that
P_{VZ,t}
be differentiable in quadratic mean with score
Q_{\mathcal{X}}^{*}s
:

\displaystyle\int\left[\frac{1}{t}\left(\sqrt{\frac{\mathrm{d}P_{VZ,t}}{{\mathrm{d}P_{VZ}}}}-1\right)-\frac{1}{2}Q_{\mathcal{X}}^{*}s\right]^{2}\,\mathrm{d}P_{VZ}\to 0\,\text{ as }t\to 0.

(59)

To show this, we follow
38
. Note that
\frac{\mathrm{d}P_{VZ,t}}{{\mathrm{d}P_{VZ}}}=Q_{\mathcal{X}}^{*}\frac{\mathrm{d}P_{VX,t}}{{\mathrm{d}P_{VX}}}
, because one can verify (as in the proof of
Lemma

4
) that the conditional distribution on the right in
Q_{\mathcal{X}}^{*}
is
P_{X{|\,}VZ}(B{|\,}v,z)=\frac{\mathrm{d}\int_{(\cdot)\times B}Q(\cdot{|\,}x)\mathrm{d}P_{VX}(\tilde{v},x)}{\mathrm{d}P_{VZ}}(v,z)
. Further,

\displaystyle\frac{\mathrm{d}P_{VZ,t}}{{\mathrm{d}P_{VZ}}}=Q_{\mathcal{X}}^{*}\frac{\mathrm{d}P_{VX,t}}{{\mathrm{d}P_{VX}}}=Q_{\mathcal{X}}^{*}\left(\sqrt{\frac{\mathrm{d}P_{VX,t}}{{\mathrm{d}P_{VX}}}}-Q_{\mathcal{X}}^{*}\sqrt{\frac{\mathrm{d}P_{VX,t}}{{\mathrm{d}P_{VX}}}}+Q_{\mathcal{X}}^{*}\sqrt{\frac{\mathrm{d}P_{VX,t}}{{\mathrm{d}P_{VX}}}}\right)^{2}

\displaystyle=Q_{\mathcal{X}}^{*}\left(\sqrt{\frac{\mathrm{d}P_{VX,t}}{{\mathrm{d}P_{VX}}}}-Q_{\mathcal{X}}^{*}\sqrt{\frac{\mathrm{d}P_{VX,t}}{{\mathrm{d}P_{VX}}}}\right)^{2}+\left(Q_{\mathcal{X}}^{*}\sqrt{\frac{\mathrm{d}P_{VX,t}}{{\mathrm{d}P_{VX}}}}\right)^{2}\eqqcolon\sigma_{t}^{2}+\left(Q_{\mathcal{X}}^{*}\sqrt{\frac{\mathrm{d}P_{VX,t}}{{\mathrm{d}P_{VX}}}}\right)^{2},

(60)

where the last equality follows from
Q_{\mathcal{X}}^{*}
being the conditional expectation given
(V,Z)
, and we introduced
\sigma_{t}^{2}\in L_{2}(P_{VZ})
. It follows that

\displaystyle L_{2}(P_{VZ})\ni\delta_{t}\coloneqq\sqrt{\frac{\mathrm{d}P_{VZ,t}}{{\mathrm{d}P_{VZ}}}}-Q_{\mathcal{X}}^{*}\sqrt{\frac{\mathrm{d}P_{VX,t}}{{\mathrm{d}P_{VX}}}}\geq 0.

(61)

Write

\displaystyle r_{t}\coloneqq\sqrt{\frac{\mathrm{d}P_{VX,t}}{{\mathrm{d}P_{VX}}}}-1-\frac{1}{2}ts,\quad\bar{r}_{t}\coloneqq\sqrt{\frac{\mathrm{d}P_{VZ,t}}{{\mathrm{d}P_{VZ}}}}-1-\frac{1}{2}tQ_{\mathcal{X}}^{*}s

(62)

for the residuals
r_{t}\in L_{2}(P_{VX})
and
\bar{r}_{t}\in L_{2}(P_{VZ})
. By (
58
),
P_{VX}r_{t}^{2}=o\left(t^{2}\right)
as
t\to 0
. To show (
59
), we show
P_{VZ}\bar{r}_{t}^{2}=o\left(t^{2}\right)
as
t\to 0
. Using that
(x+y)^{2}\leq 2(x^{2}+y^{2})
for all
x,y\in\real
, expand

\displaystyle P_{VX}\bar{r}_{t}^{2}\leq 2P_{VZ}(Q_{\mathcal{X}}^{*}r_{t})^{2}+2P_{VZ}(\bar{r}_{t}-Q_{\mathcal{X}}^{*}r_{t})^{2}.

Here, the first term
P_{VZ}(Q_{\mathcal{X}}^{*}r_{t})^{2}\leq

P_{VZ}Q_{\mathcal{X}}^{*}r_{t}^{2}=P_{VX}r_{t}^{2}=o\left(t^{2}\right)
, where the first inequality is Jensen’s, conditional on
(V,Z)
; the second term

\displaystyle P_{VZ}(\bar{r}_{t}-Q_{\mathcal{X}}^{*}r_{t})^{2}=P_{VZ}\left(\sqrt{\frac{\mathrm{d}P_{VZ,t}}{{\mathrm{d}P_{VZ}}}}-Q_{\mathcal{X}}^{*}\sqrt{\frac{\mathrm{d}P_{VX,t}}{{\mathrm{d}P_{VX}}}}\right)^{2}=P_{VZ}\delta_{t}^{2}.

By definitions (
60
) and (
61
),
\left(\delta_{t}+Q_{\mathcal{X}}^{*}\sqrt{\frac{\mathrm{d}P_{VX,t}}{{\mathrm{d}P_{VX}}}}\right)^{2}=\frac{\mathrm{d}P_{VZ,t}}{{\mathrm{d}P_{VZ}}}=\sigma_{t}^{2}+\left(Q_{\mathcal{X}}^{*}\sqrt{\frac{\mathrm{d}P_{VX,t}}{{\mathrm{d}P_{VX}}}}\right)^{2}
, yielding the algebraic identity

\displaystyle\delta_{t}^{2}=\sigma_{t}^{2}-2\delta_{t}Q_{\mathcal{X}}^{*}\sqrt{\frac{\mathrm{d}P_{VX,t}}{{\mathrm{d}P_{VX}}}}.

(63)

But
\delta_{t}\geq 0
by (
61
), and so is
Q_{\mathcal{X}}^{*}\sqrt{\frac{\mathrm{d}P_{VX,t}}{{\mathrm{d}P_{VX}}}}\geq 0
as a density is nonnegative; hence
\delta_{t}^{2}\leq\sigma_{t}^{2}
. Using (
62
),

\displaystyle\begin{aligned} \sigma_{t}^{2}=Q_{\mathcal{X}}^{*}\left(\frac{1}{2}t(s-Q_{\mathcal{X}}^{*}s)+r_{t}-Q_{\mathcal{X}}^{*}r_{t}\right)^{2}\leq\frac{1}{2}t^{2}Q_{\mathcal{X}}^{*}(s-Q_{\mathcal{X}}^{*}s)^{2}+2Q_{\mathcal{X}}^{*}(r_{t}-Q_{\mathcal{X}}^{*}r_{t})^{2}\\
\leq\frac{t^{2}}{2}Q_{\mathcal{X}}^{*}s^{2}+2Q_{\mathcal{X}}^{*}r_{t},\end{aligned}

(64)

because the projection
Q_{\mathcal{X}}^{*}
decreases the conditional variance. Here, by the tower property of expectations,
P_{VZ}Q_{\mathcal{X}}^{*}s^{2}=P_{VX}s^{2}=O\left(1\right)
as
s\in L_{2}(P_{VX})
since it is a score, and
P_{VZ}Q_{\mathcal{X}}^{*}r_{t}^{2}=P_{VX}r_{t}^{2}=o\left(t^{2}\right)
by (
58
). Hence,
P_{VZ}\sigma_{t}^{2}\to 0
as
t\to 0
.

For a fixed, strictly positive
\beta\in\real
, define the set of
(V,Z)
,

A_{t,\beta}\coloneqq\left\{Q_{\mathcal{X}}^{*}\sqrt{\frac{\mathrm{d}P_{VX,t}}{{\mathrm{d}P_{VX}}}}\geq\frac{1}{2},\sigma_{t}^{2}\leq\beta\right\}.

On
A_{t,\beta}
, squaring (
63
) gives

\delta_{t}^{2}=\frac{\sigma_{t}^{4}-\delta_{t}^{4}-2\delta_{t}^{3}Q_{\mathcal{X}}^{*}\sqrt{\frac{\mathrm{d}P_{VX,t}}{{\mathrm{d}P_{VX}}}}}{4\left(Q_{\mathcal{X}}^{*}\sqrt{\frac{\mathrm{d}P_{VX,t}}{{\mathrm{d}P_{VX}}}}\right)^{2}}\leq\sigma_{t}^{4}\leq\beta\sigma_{t}^{2}

since
\delta_{t}\geq 0
; while on its complement
A_{t,\beta}^{\mathrm{c}}
, like
P_{VZ}
-everywhere,
\delta_{t}^{2}\leq\sigma_{t}^{2}
as seen above. From (
64
) and (
58
),

\displaystyle P_{VZ}\delta_{t}^{2}=P_{VZ}\mathbbm{1}_{A_{t,\beta}}\delta_{t}^{2}+P_{VZ}\mathbbm{1}_{A_{t\beta}^{\mathrm{c}}}\delta_{t}^{2}\leq\beta P_{VZ}\sigma_{t}^{2}+P_{VZ}\mathbbm{1}_{A_{t,\beta}^{\mathrm{c}}}\sigma_{t}^{2}

\displaystyle\leq\frac{\beta t^{2}}{2}P_{VZ}Q_{\mathcal{X}}^{*}s^{2}+o\left(t^{2}\right)+\frac{t^{2}}{2}P_{VZ}\mathbbm{1}_{A_{t,\beta}^{\mathrm{c}}}Q_{\mathcal{X}}^{*}s^{2}+o\left(t^{2}\right).

Since
P_{VZ}(A_{t,\beta}^{\mathrm{c}})\to 0
as
t\to 0
by the previous paragraph, a small enough choice of
\beta
shows the right side
o\left(t^{2}\right)

Conclude that
P_{VZ}\bar{r}_{t}^{2}\leq P_{VZ}\delta_{t}^{2}=o\left(t^{2}\right)
, whereby (
59
) holds.

Assertions
(ii)
and
(iii)
. We build on
48
. By
(i)
,
\mathcal{T}_{VZ}=\left\{Q_{\mathcal{X}}^{*}s:s\in L_{2}^{0}(P_{VX})\right\}
, which is the range
R(Q_{\mathcal{X}}^{*})
of
Q_{\mathcal{X}}^{*}
. It follows from the defining relation between
Q_{\mathcal{X}}
and
Q_{\mathcal{X}}^{*}
that
R(Q_{\mathcal{X}}^{*})^{\perp}=N((Q_{\mathcal{X}}^{*})^{*})
, where
(Q_{\mathcal{X}}^{*})^{*}=Q_{\mathcal{X}}
in Hilbert spaces
L_{2}(P_{VX})
and
L_{2}(P_{VZ})
, and

R(Q_{\mathcal{X}}^{*})^{\perp}\coloneqq\left\{k\in L_{2}(P_{VZ}):P_{VZ}k\kappa=0\text{ holds for all }\kappa\in R(Q_{\mathcal{X}}^{*})\right\}

is the orthocomplement of
R(Q_{\mathcal{X}}^{*})
, and
N(Q_{\mathcal{X}})\coloneqq\left\{k\in L_{2}(P_{VZ}):Q_{\mathcal{X}}k=0\right\}
is the kernel of
Q_{\mathcal{X}}
. By properties of Hilbert spaces, it follows that
\overline{R(Q_{\mathcal{X}}^{*})}=N(Q_{\mathcal{X}})^{\perp}
, where
\overline{R(Q_{\mathcal{X}}^{*})}
is the closure of
R(Q_{\mathcal{X}}^{*})
in
L_{2}(P_{VZ})
. Studying the kernel
N(Q_{\mathcal{X}})
, the relation

\displaystyle 0=(Q_{\mathcal{X}}k)(v,x)\,\text{ for all }(v,x)\in\mathfrak{V}\times\mathfrak{X},

for
(ii)
is equivalent to

\displaystyle 0_{J}=Q^{\intercal}\begin{bmatrix}k(v,z_{1})\\
k(v,z_{2})\\
\vdots\\
k(v,z_{J})\end{bmatrix}\,\text{ for all }v\in\mathfrak{V}\quad

\displaystyle\iff(Q^{\intercal})^{-1}0_{J}=0_{J}=\begin{bmatrix}k(v,z_{1})\\
k(v,z_{2})\\
\vdots\\
k(v,z_{J})\end{bmatrix}\,\text{ for all }v\in\mathfrak{V},

by invertibility of
Q
. Hence,
k=0
, and thus
\overline{R(Q_{\mathcal{X}}^{*})}=N(Q_{\mathcal{X}})^{\perp}=L_{2}(P_{VZ})
so that the model remains nonparametric. For
(iii)
,
Q_{\mathcal{X}}^{-1}0=0
with
Q_{\mathcal{X}}^{-1}
in
Lemma

3

(vi)
, yields the same conclusion.
∎

Proof of
Theorem

2
.

We follow
48
. First, we address the nonparametric model
\mathcal{P}_{VX}=\mathcal{P}_{\mathfrak{V}\mathfrak{X}}
with tangent set
\mathcal{T}_{VX}=L_{2}^{0}(P_{VX})
and efficient influence function
\tilde{\chi}
in (
4
). Consider the submodel
t\mapsto P_{VX,t}
of
Lemma

1
with scores
s\in\mathcal{T}_{VX}=L_{2}^{0}(P_{VX})
. Because
Q\in\mathcal{Q}_{\psi}
, these submodels induce submodel
t\mapsto P_{VZ,t}
and the tangent set
\mathcal{T}_{VZ}(Q,\bar{P}_{VX})=\left\{Q_{\mathcal{X}}^{*}s:s\in L_{2}^{0}(P_{VX})\right\}
by
Lemma

1
. By construction, since
Q\in\mathcal{Q}_{\psi}
,
\psi(P_{VZ,t})=\chi(P_{VX,t})
for all
t\in\real
.

The efficient influence function
\tilde{\psi}
of
\psi(P_{VZ})
exists if and only if

\frac{\mathrm{d}}{\mathrm{d}t}\big|_{t=0}\psi(P_{VZ,t})=P_{VZ}[\tilde{\psi}(Q_{\mathcal{X}}^{*}s)]

for all regular submodels
P_{VZ,t}
with score
Q_{\mathcal{X}}^{*}s\in\left\{Q_{\mathcal{X}}^{*}s:s\in L_{2}^{0}(P_{VX})\right\}
. But
\frac{\mathrm{d}}{\mathrm{d}t}\big|_{t=0}\psi(P_{VZ,t})=\frac{\mathrm{d}}{\mathrm{d}t}\big|_{t=0}\chi(P_{VX,t})
by the previous paragraph. Since
\tilde{\chi}
is the efficient influence function of
\chi(P_{VX})
by assumption, we must also have that

\frac{\mathrm{d}}{\mathrm{d}t}\big|_{t=0}\chi(P_{VX,t})=P_{VX}[s\tilde{\chi}].

Thus, for all
s\in L_{2}^{0}(P_{VX})
,

\displaystyle P_{VZ}[\tilde{\psi}(Q_{\mathcal{X}}^{*}s)]=\frac{\mathrm{d}}{\mathrm{d}t}\big|_{t=0}\psi(P_{VZ,t})=\frac{\mathrm{d}}{\mathrm{d}t}\big|_{t=0}\chi(P_{VX,t})=P_{VX}[s\tilde{\chi}].

By the definition of the adjoint
(Q_{\mathcal{X}}^{*})^{*}=Q_{\mathcal{X}}
, the inner product
P_{VZ}[\tilde{\psi}(Q_{\mathcal{X}}^{*}s)]
is equal to
P_{VZ}[\tilde{\psi}(Q_{\mathcal{X}}^{*}s)]=P_{VX}[(((Q_{\mathcal{X}}^{*})^{*})\tilde{\psi})s]=P_{VX}[(Q_{\mathcal{X}}\tilde{\psi})s]
, which by the last display is equal to
P_{VX}[s\tilde{\chi}]
for all
s\in L_{2}^{0}(P_{VX})
. Hence
P_{VX}[(Q_{\mathcal{X}}\tilde{\psi})s]=P_{VX}[s\tilde{\chi}]
for all
s\in L_{2}^{0}(P_{VX})
, or, by rearrangement,
P_{VX}[(Q_{\mathcal{X}}\tilde{\psi}-\tilde{\chi})s]=0
for all
s\in L_{2}^{0}(P_{VX})
. Equivalently,
Q_{\mathcal{X}}\tilde{\psi}-\tilde{\chi}
must be in the orthocomplement of
L_{2}^{0}(P_{VX})\subset L_{2}(P_{VX})
, which, as we show below, is

L_{2}^{0}(P_{VX})^{\perp}=\left\{f\in L_{2}(P_{VX}):f-P_{VX}f=0\,P_{VX}\text{-a.s.}\right\}.

Now,
\tilde{\chi}
is the efficient influence function for
\chi(P_{VX})
, so
P_{VX}\tilde{\chi}=0
. For
\tilde{\psi}
to be an influence function for
\psi(P_{VZ})
, we must have
P_{VZ}\tilde{\psi}=0
, but by
Lemma

3
(iii)
,
P_{VZ}\tilde{\psi}=P_{VX}Q_{\mathcal{X}}\tilde{\psi}
. Hence,
\tilde{\chi},Q_{\mathcal{X}}\tilde{\psi}\in L_{2}^{0}(P_{VX})
and
Q_{\mathcal{X}}\tilde{\psi}-\tilde{\chi}\in L_{2}^{0}(P_{VX})
. But
Q_{\mathcal{X}}\tilde{\psi}-\tilde{\chi}\in L_{2}^{0}(P_{VX})^{\perp}
too as we showed above. Since
L_{2}^{0}(P_{VX})\cap L_{2}^{0}(P_{VX})^{\perp}=\left\{f:f=0\,P_{VX}\text{-a.s.}\right\}
, we must have
Q_{\mathcal{X}}\tilde{\psi}=\tilde{\chi}

P_{VX}
-a.s.. Because
Q\in\mathcal{Q}_{\psi}
,
Q_{\mathcal{X}}^{-1}
exists, giving
\tilde{\psi}=Q_{\mathcal{X}}^{-1}\tilde{\chi}
.

To see that the orthocomplement
L_{2}^{0}(P_{VX})^{\perp}
of
L_{2}^{0}(P_{VX})
in
L_{2}(P_{VX})
is

\left\{f\in L_{2}(P_{VX}):f-P_{VX}f=0\,P_{VX}\text{-a.s.}\right\},

take some
f\in L_{2}^{0}(P_{VX})^{\perp}
. Because
f\in L_{2}^{0}(P_{VX})^{\perp}
and
f-P_{VX}f\in L_{2}^{0}(P_{VX})
, we must have
P_{VX}[f(f-P_{VX}f)]=0
. Because
P_{VX}[f(f-P_{VX}f)]=P_{VX}[(f-P_{VX}f)(f-P_{VX}f)]
, we must have
f-P_{VX}f=0

P_{VX}
-a.s.. Because
f\in L_{2}^{0}(P_{VX})^{\perp}
was chosen arbitrarily, the assertion follows.

Second, we address an arbitrary model
\mathcal{P}_{VX}\subset\mathcal{P}_{\mathfrak{V}\mathfrak{X}}
with
\mathcal{T}_{VX}\subset L_{2}^{0}(P_{VX})
and efficient influence function
\varphi\in Q_{\mathcal{X}}Q_{\mathcal{X}}^{*}\mathcal{T}_{VX}
. Let
\tilde{\Psi}
be the efficient influence function of
\psi(P_{VZ})
in the model
\mathcal{P}_{VZ}(Q,\mathcal{P}_{VX})
of (
8
). We have
P_{VX}[(Q_{\mathcal{X}}\tilde{\Psi}-\varphi)s]=0
for all
s\in\mathcal{T}_{VX}
by the arguments above, that is,

Q_{\mathcal{X}}\tilde{\Psi}-\varphi\in\mathcal{T}_{VX}^{\perp}\coloneqq\left\{f\in L_{2}(P_{VX}):P_{VX}fs=0\text{ for all }s\in\mathcal{T}_{VX}\right\}.

If
Q_{\mathcal{X}}\tilde{\Psi}-\varphi\in\mathcal{T}_{VX}
also, then
Q_{\mathcal{X}}\tilde{\Psi}-\varphi=0
a.s. must be, from where the assertion follows by the invertibility of
Q_{\mathcal{X}}
. From the previous display,
\tilde{\Psi}=Q_{\mathcal{X}}^{-1}(f_{0}+\varphi)
for some
f_{0}\in\mathcal{T}_{VX}^{\perp}.
Since
\tilde{\Psi}\in\mathcal{T}_{VZ}(Q,\mathcal{P}_{VX})
, which set is
\left\{Q_{\mathcal{X}}^{*}s:s\in\mathcal{T}_{VX}\right\}
by
Lemma

1
, we also have that
(Q_{\mathcal{X}}^{*})^{-1}\tilde{\Psi}\in\mathcal{T}_{VX}
.
But then
P_{VX}f_{0}(Q_{\mathcal{X}}^{*})^{-1}\tilde{\Psi}=0
because
f_{0}\in\mathcal{T}_{VX}^{\perp}
. Whence,
P_{VX}f_{0}(Q_{\mathcal{X}}^{*})^{-1}Q_{\mathcal{X}}^{-1}(f_{0}+\varphi)=0
, or, equivalently,

P_{VX}f_{0}(Q_{\mathcal{X}}^{*})^{-1}Q_{\mathcal{X}}^{-1}f_{0}=-P_{VX}f_{0}(Q_{\mathcal{X}}^{*})^{-1}Q_{\mathcal{X}}^{-1}\varphi.

By the definition of
Q_{\mathcal{X}}
and
Q_{\mathcal{X}}^{*}
and by the invertibility of
Q_{\mathcal{X}}
, one can verify with the tower property of expectations that
(Q_{\mathcal{X}}^{*})^{-1}Q_{\mathcal{X}}^{-1}=(Q_{\mathcal{X}}Q_{\mathcal{X}}^{*})^{-1}
is a positive definite operator, so that the left side of the previous display is zero if and only if
f_{0}=0
a.s.. But the right side is zero because
f_{0}\in\mathcal{T}_{VX}^{\perp}
, and
(Q_{\mathcal{X}}Q_{\mathcal{X}}^{*})^{-1}\varphi\in\mathcal{T}_{VX}
by the assumption
\varphi\in Q_{\mathcal{X}}Q_{\mathcal{X}}^{*}\mathcal{T}_{VX}
. Hence,
f_{0}=0
a.s., so
\tilde{\Psi}=Q_{\mathcal{X}}^{-1}\varphi
a.s..

∎

C.2.  Private Estimation

This section proves the main result
Corollary

1
of
Section

5
via
Lemma

2
.

Lemma

5
in
Section

C.3
establishes some technical results concerning norms under
P_{VX}
and
P_{VZ}
and the continuity of the operators
Q_{\mathcal{X}},Q_{\mathcal{X}}^{-1}
. In the light of these technical results, under
2
,
Lemma

2
shows that the empirical process term in (
31
) vanishes as
o_{P_{VZ}}\left(1\right)
. It rests on the same arguments as its nonprivate counterpart,
Lemma

9
, but it relies on the continuity of the operator
Q_{\mathcal{X}}^{-1}
. It is solely because of this that
2
is more involved than its nonprivate counterpart,
4
, and that it requires that the whole
(V,X)
be distributed on a finite set, as opposed to only
X
be finitely distributed. For the proof, note that

\displaystyle\partial_{\gamma}\bar{f}(v,z,\mu,\bar{\gamma})\coloneqq\frac{\partial\bar{f}}{\partial\gamma}(v,z,\mu,\bar{\gamma})=\frac{\partial}{\partial\gamma}(Q_{\mathcal{X}}^{-1}(v,x)\mapsto f(v,x,\mu,\bar{\gamma}))(v,z)

\displaystyle=(Q_{\mathcal{X}}^{-1}(v,x)\mapsto\partial_{\gamma}f(v,x,\mu,\bar{\gamma}))(v,z),\quad(v,z,\mu,\bar{\gamma})\in\mathfrak{V}\times\mathfrak{Z}\times L_{2}(P_{V_{\mathit{1}}X})\times\Gamma.

Lemma 2

(Vanishing Empirical Process Term — Private Estimators)
.

Assume that
\eta
is estimated by (
25
) and
2
holds.
Then
\check{e}-e=o_{P_{VZ}}\left(1\right)
and
(\bar{\mathbb{P}}_{n}-P_{VZ})(\hat{\tilde{\psi}}-\tilde{\psi})=o_{P_{VZ}}\left(n^{-1/2}\right)
.

Proof of
Lemma

2
.

As in the proof of
Lemma

9
, we apply that if a random function
\hat{q}\in L_{2}(P_{VZ})
is independent of the random sample generating the process
\bar{\mathbb{P}}_{n}
, then

\displaystyle\begin{aligned} \int(\hat{q}(v,z)-q(v,z))^{2}\,\mathrm{d}P_{VZ}(v,z)=o_{P_{VZ}}\left(1\right)\text{ implies }\\
\sqrt{n}(\bar{\mathbb{P}}_{n}-P_{VZ})(\hat{q}-q)=o_{P_{VZ}}\left(1\right).\end{aligned}

(65)

In particular, we shall combine this with
Lemma

5
, establishing the boundedness of
Q_{\mathcal{X}}^{-1}
for
\left\lVert{\cdot}\right\rVert_{\mathrm{L}_{2}}
, to show the convergence of

\displaystyle\left\lVert{Q_{\mathcal{X}}^{-1}T}\right\rVert_{L_{2}(P_{VZ})}\lesssim\left\lVert{T}\right\rVert_{\mathrm{L}_{2}}

(66)

to zero in
P_{VZ}
-probability for some
T\in\mathrm{L}_{2}
.

By the linearity of
Q_{\mathcal{X}}^{-1}
,
\hat{\tilde{\psi}}-\tilde{\psi}=Q_{\mathcal{X}}^{-1}(\check{\tilde{\chi}}-\tilde{\chi})
. By the definitions (
4
) and (
24
),

\displaystyle\check{\tilde{\chi}}(v,x)-\tilde{\chi}(v,x)=

\displaystyle\,\bar{T}_{1}(v,x)+\bar{T}_{2}(v,x)+\bar{T}_{3}(v,x)+\bar{T}_{4},

(67)

\displaystyle\bar{T}_{1}(v,x)\coloneqq

\displaystyle\,\check{r}(v_{\mathit{1}},x)(m(v,x)-{\check{\mu}_{\mathcal{X}}}(v_{\mathit{1}},x))

\displaystyle-r(v_{\mathit{1}},x)(m(v,x)-\mu_{\mathcal{X}}(v_{\mathit{1}},x)),

\displaystyle\bar{T}_{2}(v,x)\coloneqq

\displaystyle\,\frac{\mathbbm{1}_{v_{\mathit{2}}=c}}{\check{p}_{V_{\mathit{2}}}(c)}(g(v,x)-\check{\gamma}_{\mathcal{V}}(c))\check{e}-\frac{\mathbbm{1}_{v_{\mathit{2}}=c}}{p_{V_{\mathit{2}}}(c)}(g(v,x)-\gamma_{\mathcal{V}}(c))e,

\displaystyle\bar{T}_{3}(v,x)\coloneqq

\displaystyle\,f(v,x,{\check{\mu}_{\mathcal{X}}},\check{\gamma}_{\mathcal{V}}(c))-f(v,x,\mu_{\mathcal{X}},\gamma_{\mathcal{V}}(c)),

\displaystyle\bar{T}_{4}\coloneqq

\displaystyle\,-\psi(\hat{P}_{VZ})+\psi(P_{VZ}).

As
\bar{T}_{4}
is constant, not depending on
(v,x)
,
Q_{\mathcal{X}}^{-1}\bar{T}_{4}=\bar{T}_{4}
and
(\bar{\mathbb{P}}_{n}-P_{VZ})Q_{\mathcal{X}}^{-1}\bar{T}_{4}=0
. It remains to show
(\bar{\mathbb{P}}_{n}-P_{VZ})Q_{\mathcal{X}}^{-1}\bar{T}_{j}=o_{P_{VZ}}\left(n^{-1/2}\right)
for
j=1,2,3
by the linearity of the process
\bar{\mathbb{P}}_{n}-P_{VZ}
.

Term
\bar{T}_{1}.\,\,
In the light of (
66
),
(\bar{\mathbb{P}}_{n}-P_{VZ})Q_{\mathcal{X}}^{-1}\bar{T}_{1}=o_{P_{VZ}}\left(n^{-1/2}\right)
can be established along the same steps as that of
T_{1}
in the proof of
Lemma

9
. In particular,
\left\lVert{Q_{\mathcal{X}}^{-1}\bar{T}_{1}}\right\rVert_{L_{2}(P_{VZ})}\lesssim\left\lVert{\bar{T}_{1}}\right\rVert_{\mathrm{L}_{2}}
. Namely, suppressing the arguments, write

\displaystyle\bar{T}_{1}=\check{r}(m-{\check{\mu}_{\mathcal{X}}})-r(m-\mu_{\mathcal{X}})

\displaystyle=(\check{r}-r+r)(m-{\check{\mu}_{\mathcal{X}}})-r(m-\mu_{\mathcal{X}})

\displaystyle=(\check{r}-r)(m-{\check{\mu}_{\mathcal{X}}})+r(\mu_{\mathcal{X}}-{\check{\mu}_{\mathcal{X}}}).

By
2
,
\left\lVert{m-{\check{\mu}_{\mathcal{X}}}}\right\rVert_{\infty}=O_{{P_{VZ}}}\left(1\right)
; either directly by
(
49
), or by (
47
) and (
48
), noting that
\left\lVert{m-{\check{\mu}_{\mathcal{X}}}}\right\rVert_{\infty}\leq\left\lVert{m-\mu_{\mathcal{X}}}\right\rVert_{\infty}+\left\lVert{\mu_{\mathcal{X}}-{\check{\mu}_{\mathcal{X}}}}\right\rVert_{\infty}=O\left(1\right)+o_{P_{VZ}}\left(1\right)=O_{{P_{VZ}}}\left(1\right)
.
Then the convergence (
44
) of
r
implies that
(\bar{\mathbb{P}}_{n}-P_{VZ})Q_{\mathcal{X}}^{-1}((\check{r}-r)(m-{\check{\mu}_{\mathcal{X}}}))=o_{P_{VZ}}\left(n^{-1/2}\right)
by (
66
) as

\displaystyle\left\lVert{(\check{r}-r)(m-{\check{\mu}_{\mathcal{X}}})}\right\rVert_{\mathrm{L}_{2}}\leq\left\lVert{m-{\check{\mu}_{\mathcal{X}}}}\right\rVert_{\infty}\left\lVert{\check{r}-r}\right\rVert_{\mathrm{L}_{2}}=O_{{P_{VZ}}}\left(1\right)o_{P_{VZ}}\left(1\right)=o_{P_{VZ}}\left(1\right)

since
\left\lVert{|q|^{2}}\right\rVert_{\infty}=\left\lVert{q}\right\rVert_{\infty}^{2}
.

By
2
, either (
48
), or (
50
) and (
51
). In the former case,

\displaystyle\left\lVert{r(\mu_{\mathcal{X}}-{\check{\mu}_{\mathcal{X}}})}\right\rVert_{\mathrm{L}_{2}}\leq\left\lVert{\mu_{\mathcal{X}}-{\check{\mu}_{\mathcal{X}}}}\right\rVert_{\infty}\left\lVert{r}\right\rVert_{\mathrm{L}_{2}}=o_{P_{VZ}}\left(1\right),

because
r\in\mathrm{L}_{2}
. In the latter case,

\displaystyle\left\lVert{r(\mu_{\mathcal{X}}-{\check{\mu}_{\mathcal{X}}})}\right\rVert_{\mathrm{L}_{2}}\leq\bar{R}\left\lVert{\mu_{\mathcal{X}}-{\check{\mu}_{\mathcal{X}}}}\right\rVert_{\mathrm{L}_{2}}=o_{P_{VZ}}\left(1\right),

since (
51
) bounds
r
and
{\check{\mu}_{\mathcal{X}}}
is convergent by (
50
). Thus,
(\bar{\mathbb{P}}_{n}-P_{VZ})Q_{\mathcal{X}}^{-1}

(r(\mu_{\mathcal{X}}-{\check{\mu}_{\mathcal{X}}}))=o_{P_{VZ}}\left(n^{-1/2}\right)
by (
65
). Conclude that
(\bar{\mathbb{P}}_{n}-P_{VZ})Q_{\mathcal{X}}^{-1}\bar{T}_{1}=o_{P_{VZ}}\left(n^{-1/2}\right)
.

Term
\bar{T}_{2}.\,\,
By the mean-value theorem there exists
(\tilde{\gamma}_{\mathcal{V}}(c),\tilde{p}_{V_{\mathit{2}}}(c),\tilde{e})
between
(\gamma_{\mathcal{V}}(c),p_{V_{\mathit{2}}}(c),e)
and
(\check{\gamma}_{\mathcal{V}}(c),\check{p}_{V_{\mathit{2}}}(c),\check{e})
such that

\displaystyle\bar{T}_{2}(v,x)=

\displaystyle\,\frac{\mathbbm{1}_{v_{\mathit{2}}=c}}{\check{p}_{V_{\mathit{2}}}(c)}(g(v,x)-\check{\gamma}_{\mathcal{V}}(c))\check{e}-\frac{\mathbbm{1}_{v_{\mathit{2}}=c}}{p_{V_{\mathit{2}}}(c)}(g(v,x)-\gamma_{\mathcal{V}}(c))e

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\,-\frac{\mathbbm{1}_{v_{\mathit{2}}=c}}{\tilde{p}_{V_{\mathit{2}}}(c)}\tilde{e}(\check{\gamma}_{\mathcal{V}}(c)-\gamma_{\mathcal{V}}(c))

\displaystyle-\frac{\mathbbm{1}_{v_{\mathit{2}}=c}}{\tilde{p}_{V_{\mathit{2}}}(c)^{2}}(g(v,x)-\tilde{\gamma}_{\mathcal{V}}(c))\tilde{e}(\check{p}_{V_{\mathit{2}}}(c)-p_{V_{\mathit{2}}}(c))

\displaystyle+\frac{\mathbbm{1}_{v_{\mathit{2}}=c}}{\tilde{p}_{V_{\mathit{2}}}(c)}(g(v,x)-\tilde{\gamma}_{\mathcal{V}}(c))(\check{e}-e).

The standard central limit theorem applies to the i.i.d. sequence

\bigg((Q_{\mathcal{X}}^{-1}(v,x)\mapsto\mathbbm{1}_{v_{\mathit{2}}=c})(V_{i},Z_{i})\bigg)_{i\in[n]},

hence
\sqrt{n}(\bar{\mathbb{P}}_{n}-P_{VZ})((Q_{\mathcal{X}}^{-1}(v,x)\mapsto\mathbbm{1}_{v_{\mathit{2}}=c})(V,Z))=O_{{P_{VZ}}}\left(1\right)
. By the linearity of the process
\sqrt{n}(\bar{\mathbb{P}}_{n}-P_{VZ})Q_{\mathcal{X}}^{-1}
,

\displaystyle\sqrt{n}(\bar{\mathbb{P}}_{n}-P_{VZ})\big\{\big[Q_{\mathcal{X}}^{-1}(v,x)\mapsto\mathbbm{1}_{v_{\mathit{2}}=c}g(v,x)-\mathbbm{1}_{v_{\mathit{2}}=c}\tilde{\gamma}_{\mathcal{V}}(c)\big](V,Z)\big\}

\displaystyle=\sqrt{n}(\bar{\mathbb{P}}_{n}-P_{VZ})\big\{\big[Q_{\mathcal{X}}^{-1}(v,x)\mapsto\mathbbm{1}_{v_{\mathit{2}}=c}g(v,x)\big](V,Z)\big\}

\displaystyle-\tilde{\gamma}_{\mathcal{V}}(c)\sqrt{n}(\bar{\mathbb{P}}_{n}-P_{VZ})\big\{\big[Q_{\mathcal{X}}^{-1}(v,x)\mapsto\mathbbm{1}_{v_{\mathit{2}}=c}\big](V,Z)\big\}

\displaystyle=(1-\tilde{\gamma}_{\mathcal{V}}(c))O_{{P_{VZ}}}\left(1\right)=O_{{P_{VZ}}}\left(1\right)

again by the standard central limit theorem and (
45
). Suppose that
\check{e}-e=o_{P_{VZ}}\left(1\right)
, which we show later. Then by (
45
) and (
46
),
(\bar{\mathbb{P}}_{n}-P_{VZ})Q_{\mathcal{X}}^{-1}\bar{T}_{2}=o_{P_{VZ}}\left(n^{-1/2}\right)
.

Term
\bar{T}_{3}.\,\,
Recall that
\bar{T}_{3}(v,x)=f(v,x,{\check{\mu}_{\mathcal{X}}},\check{\gamma}_{\mathcal{V}}(c))-f(v,x,\mu_{\mathcal{X}},\gamma_{\mathcal{V}}(c))
. By the consistency of
\check{\gamma}_{\mathcal{V}}
and
{\check{\mu}_{\mathcal{X}}}
((
45
) and (
48
) or (
50
)), we have
\left\lVert{\bar{T}_{3}}\right\rVert_{\mathrm{L}_{2}}=o_{P_{VZ}}\left(1\right)
by the continuous mapping theorem and (
42
). Conclude by (
65
) and (
66
) that
(\bar{\mathbb{P}}_{n}-P_{VZ})Q_{\mathcal{X}}^{-1}\bar{T}_{3}=o_{P_{VZ}}\left(n^{-1/2}\right)
.

Consistency of
\check{e}.\,\,
By the definition of
e,\check{e}
,

\displaystyle\check{e}-e=

\displaystyle\,\bar{\mathbb{P}}_{n}^{\prime\prime}\partial_{\gamma}\bar{f}(V,Z,{\check{\mu}_{\mathcal{X}}},\check{\gamma}_{\mathcal{V}}(c))-P_{VZ}\partial_{\gamma}\bar{f}(V,Z,\mu_{\mathcal{X}},\gamma_{\mathcal{V}}(c))

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\,\bar{\mathbb{P}}_{n}^{\prime\prime}\partial_{\gamma}\bar{f}(V,Z,{\check{\mu}_{\mathcal{X}}},\check{\gamma}_{\mathcal{V}}(c))-P_{VZ}\partial_{\gamma}\bar{f}(V,Z,{\check{\mu}_{\mathcal{X}}},\check{\gamma}_{\mathcal{V}}(c))

\displaystyle+P_{VZ}\partial_{\gamma}\bar{f}(V,Z,{\check{\mu}_{\mathcal{X}}},\check{\gamma}_{\mathcal{V}}(c))-P_{VZ}\partial_{\gamma}f(V,Z,\mu_{\mathcal{X}},\gamma_{\mathcal{V}}(c))

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\,(\bar{\mathbb{P}}_{n}^{\prime\prime}-P_{VZ})\partial_{\gamma}\bar{f}(V,Z,{\check{\mu}_{\mathcal{X}}},\check{\gamma}_{\mathcal{V}}(c))

\displaystyle+P_{VZ}\big[\partial_{\gamma}\bar{f}(V,Z,{\check{\mu}_{\mathcal{X}}},\check{\gamma}_{\mathcal{V}}(c))-\partial_{\gamma}\bar{f}(V,Z,\mu_{\mathcal{X}},\gamma_{\mathcal{V}}(c))\big]

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\,(\bar{\mathbb{P}}_{n}^{\prime\prime}-P_{VZ})\partial_{\gamma}\bar{f}(V,Z,\mu_{\mathcal{X}},\gamma_{\mathcal{V}}(c))

\displaystyle+(\bar{\mathbb{P}}_{n}^{\prime\prime}-P_{VZ})\big[\partial_{\gamma}\bar{f}(V,Z,{\check{\mu}_{\mathcal{X}}},\check{\gamma}_{\mathcal{V}}(c))-\partial_{\gamma}\bar{f}(V,Z,\mu_{\mathcal{X}},\gamma_{\mathcal{V}}(c))\big]

\displaystyle+P_{VZ}\big[\partial_{\gamma}\bar{f}(V,Z,{\check{\mu}_{\mathcal{X}}},\check{\gamma}_{\mathcal{V}}(c))-\partial_{\gamma}\bar{f}(V,Z,\mu_{\mathcal{X}},\gamma_{\mathcal{V}}(c))\big]

Here, the first term is
O_{{P_{VZ}}}\left(n^{-1/2}\right)=o_{P_{VZ}}\left(1\right)
by the standard central limit theorem, and the second and third term are
o_{P_{VZ}}\left(1\right)
by the continuity (
43
) of
\partial_{\gamma}f
using that
\partial_{\gamma}\bar{f}=Q_{\mathcal{X}}^{-1}\partial_{\gamma}f
along the same arguments concerning
\bar{T}_{3}
above.
∎

Proof of
Corollary

1
.

Follows from
Lemmas

2
and
1
, noting that, for
\bar{e}^{\prime}
in (
34
),

\check{e}-\bar{e}^{\prime}=(\bar{\mathbb{P}}_{n}^{\prime\prime}-P_{VZ})\partial_{\gamma}\bar{f}(V,Z,{\hat{\mu}_{\mathcal{X}}},\hat{\gamma}_{\mathcal{V}}(c))

is
o_{P_{VZ}}\left(1\right)
by the consistency proof of
\check{e}
in
Lemma

2
.
∎

C.3.  Auxiliary Results

In this section, auxiliary results underpinning the proofs in
Appendix

C
are derived: in
Lemma

3
, the properties of the linear operator
Q_{\mathcal{X}}
; in
Lemma

4
, the distributions of the (partly) unobserved data
(V,X,Z)
under mechanism (
16
); in
Lemma

5
, norms under
P_{VX}
and
P_{VZ}
, and consequent continuity of
Q_{\mathcal{X}},Q_{\mathcal{X}}^{-1}
.

The following properties of
Q_{\mathcal{X}}
play an essential role in the derivation of the tangent set and the efficient influence function.

Lemma 3

(Properties of
Q_{\mathcal{X}}
in (
18
))
.

(i)

The operator
Q_{\mathcal{X}}
is the conditional expectation operator
(Q_{\mathcal{X}}k)(v,x)=\mathbb{E}\left[\left.k(V,Z)\,\right|\,V=v,X=x\right]
,
(v,x)\in\mathfrak{V}\times\mathfrak{X}
.

(ii)

The operator
Q_{\mathcal{X}}
has adjoint
Q_{\mathcal{X}}^{*}:L_{2}(P_{VX})\to L_{2}(P_{VZ})
,

(Q_{\mathcal{X}}^{*}h)(v,z)=\mathbb{E}\left[\left.h(V,X)\,\right|\,V=v,Z=z\right]
,
(v,z)\in\mathfrak{V}\times\mathfrak{Z}
.

(iii)

Change of measure:

P_{VZ}k=P_{VX}Q_{\mathcal{X}}k
for all
k\in L_{2}(P_{VZ})
. In particular, if
Q_{\mathcal{X}}:L_{2}(P_{VZ})\to S\subset L_{2}(P_{VX})
has an inverse
Q_{\mathcal{X}}^{-1}:S\to L_{2}(P_{VZ})
so that
Q_{\mathcal{X}}Q_{\mathcal{X}}^{-1}h=h
for all
h\in S
, then
k\coloneqq Q_{\mathcal{X}}^{-1}h
yields
P_{VZ}Q_{\mathcal{X}}^{-1}h=P_{VX}Q_{\mathcal{X}}Q_{\mathcal{X}}^{-1}h=P_{VX}h.

(iv)

If
X
is distributed on a finite set with
|\mathfrak{Z}|=|\mathfrak{X}|=J
, then the operator
Q_{\mathcal{X}}:L_{2}(P_{VZ})\to L_{2}(P_{VX})
of (
18
) can be represented in the matrix notation (
12
) as, for all
v\in\mathfrak{V}
,

\displaystyle\begin{bmatrix}(Q_{\mathcal{X}}k)(v,x_{1})\\
(Q_{\mathcal{X}}k)(v,x_{2})\\
\vdots\\
(Q_{\mathcal{X}}k)(v,x_{J})\end{bmatrix}=Q^{\intercal}\begin{bmatrix}k(v,z_{1})\\
k(v,z_{2})\\
\vdots\\
k(v,z_{J})\end{bmatrix},

and it has inverse
Q_{\mathcal{X}}^{-1}:L_{2}(P_{VX})\to L_{2}(P_{VZ})
if and only if
Q
is invertible, given by, for all
v\in\mathfrak{V}
,

\displaystyle\begin{bmatrix}k(v,z_{1})\\
k(v,z_{2})\\
\vdots\\
k(v,z_{J})\end{bmatrix}=(Q^{\intercal})^{-1}\begin{bmatrix}h(v,x_{1})\\
h(v,x_{2})\\
\vdots\\
h(v,x_{J})\end{bmatrix}

with
(Q^{\intercal})^{-1}=(Q^{-1})^{\intercal}
.

(v)

Let
Q\in\mathcal{Q}_{\delta}
in (
16
). Then
(Q_{\mathcal{X}}k)(v,x)=\alpha k(v,x)+(1-\alpha)\int_{\mathcal{X}}k(v,z)\bar{Q}(\mathrm{d}z)
; moreover,
Q_{\mathcal{X}}:L_{2}(P_{VZ})\to L_{2}(P_{VX})\cap L_{2}(P_{V}\otimes\bar{Q})
is a bounded, hence continuous, linear operator for the norm

\displaystyle\left\lVert{h}\right\rVert_{L_{2}(P_{VX})\cap L_{2}(P_{V}\otimes\bar{Q})}\coloneqq\left\lVert{h}\right\rVert_{L_{2}(P_{VX})}+\left\lVert{h}\right\rVert_{L_{2}(P_{V}\otimes\bar{Q})}

(68)

on
L_{2}(P_{VX})\cap L_{2}(P_{V}\otimes\bar{Q})
, where
P_{V}\otimes\bar{Q}
is the distribution of a random element
(V,\bar{Z})
with independent coordinates
V\sim P_{V}
and
\bar{Z}\sim\bar{Q}
.

(vi)

Let
Q\in\mathcal{Q}_{\delta}
in (
16
). The inverse of
Q_{\mathcal{X}}:L_{2}(P_{VZ})\to L_{2}(P_{VX})\cap L_{2}(P_{V}\otimes\bar{Q})
exists, and is, as in
37
,

(Q_{\mathcal{X}}^{-1}h)(v,z)=\frac{1}{\alpha}h(v,z)-\frac{1-\alpha}{\alpha}\int_{\mathfrak{X}}h(v,x)\bar{Q}(\mathrm{d}x)

for all
h\in L_{2}(P_{VX})\cap L_{2}(P_{V}\otimes\bar{Q}).

That is,
Q_{\mathcal{X}}Q_{\mathcal{X}}^{-1}h=h
and
Q_{\mathcal{X}}^{-1}Q_{\mathcal{X}}k

=k
for all
h\in L_{2}(P_{VX})\cap L_{2}(P_{V}\otimes\bar{Q})
and all
k\in L_{2}(P_{VZ})
. Moreover
Q_{\mathcal{X}}^{-1}:L_{2}(P_{VX})\cap L_{2}(P_{V}\otimes\bar{Q})\to L_{2}(P_{VZ})
is a bounded, hence continuous, linear operator for the norm (
68
).

(vii)

Let
Q\in\mathcal{Q}_{\delta}
in (
16
). Then for all
h\in L_{2}(P_{VX})\cap L_{2}(P_{V}\otimes\bar{Q})
,

\displaystyle P_{VX}h^{2}+\frac{1-\alpha}{\alpha}\left((P_{V}\otimes\bar{Q})h^{2}-P_{V}\left(\int h(V,x)\bar{Q}(\mathrm{d}x)\right)^{2}\right)\leq P_{VZ}(Q_{\mathcal{X}}^{-1}h)^{2}

\displaystyle\leq\frac{2-\alpha}{\alpha}P_{VX}\tilde{\chi}^{2}+2\frac{(1-\alpha)(2-\alpha)}{\alpha^{2}}(P_{V}\otimes\bar{Q})h^{2}.

The second term in the lower bound is nonnegative due to Jensen’s inequality.

Proof of
Lemma

3
.

Assertion
(i)
. Follows directly from
Z\mid(V,X)\sim Q(\cdot{|\,}X)
.

Assertion
(ii)
. By definition, the adjoint
Q_{\mathcal{X}}^{*}
of
Q_{\mathcal{X}}
satisfies
P_{VX}[(Q_{\mathcal{X}}k)h]=P_{VZ}[kQ_{\mathcal{X}}^{*}h]
. By the tower property of expectation we can verify that, for
Q_{\mathcal{X}}^{*}
given in
(ii)
,

\displaystyle P_{VX}[(Q_{\mathcal{X}}k)h]=\mathbb{E}\left[\mathbb{E}\left[\left.k(V,Z)\,\right|\,V,X\right]h(V,X)\right]=\mathbb{E}\left[\mathbb{E}\left[\left.k(V,Z)h(V,X)\,\right|\,V,X\right]\right]

\displaystyle=\mathbb{E}\left[\mathbb{E}\left[\left.k(V,Z)h(V,X)\,\right|\,V,Z\right]\right]=\mathbb{E}\left[k(V,Z)\mathbb{E}\left[\left.h(V,X)\,\right|\,V,Z\right]\right]=P_{VZ}[kQ_{\mathcal{X}}^{*}h].

Assertion
(iii)
. By the tower property of expectation,

P_{VZ}k=\mathbb{E}k(V,Z)=\mathbb{E}\mathbb{E}\left[\left.k(V,Z)\,\right|\,V,X\right]=P_{VX}Q_{\mathcal{X}}k.

Assertion
(iv)
. Under the discrete model for
X
,

\int_{\mathfrak{X}}k(v,z)Q(\mathrm{d}z{|\,}x)=\sum_{z\in\mathfrak{X}}k(v,z)Q(\left\{z\right\}{|\,}x),

from which the assertion directly follows.

Assertion
(v)
. Under the mechanism
Q
in (
16
), we have

\displaystyle(Q_{\mathcal{X}}k)(v,x)=\int_{\mathfrak{X}}k(v,z)Q(\mathrm{d}z{|\,}x)=\int_{\mathfrak{X}}k(v,z)\left[\alpha\delta_{x}(\mathrm{d}z)+(1-\alpha)\bar{Q}(\mathrm{d}z)\right]

\displaystyle=\alpha k(v,x)+(1-\alpha)\int_{\mathfrak{X}}k(v,z)\bar{Q}(\mathrm{d}z).

Next we show that
Q_{\mathcal{X}}:L_{2}(P_{VZ})\to L_{2}(P_{VX})\cap L_{2}(P_{V}\otimes\bar{Q})
is bounded. Take some
k\in L_{2}(P_{VZ})
, and let
(Q_{\mathcal{X}}k)(v,x)=\alpha k(v,x)+(1-\alpha)\int_{\mathfrak{X}}k(v,z)\bar{Q}(\mathrm{d}z)\eqqcolon\phi_{1}(v,x)+\phi_{2}(v)
. By Minkowski’s inequality,

\left\lVert{Q_{\mathcal{X}}k}\right\rVert_{L_{2}(P_{VX})\cap L_{2}(P_{V}\otimes\bar{Q})}\leq\left\lVert{\phi_{1}}\right\rVert_{L_{2}(P_{VX})\cap L_{2}(P_{V}\otimes\bar{Q})}+\left\lVert{\phi_{2}}\right\rVert_{L_{2}(P_{VX})\cap L_{2}(P_{V}\otimes\bar{Q})},

where, by definition (
68
),

\left\lVert{\phi_{1}}\right\rVert_{L_{2}(P_{VX})\cap L_{2}(P_{V}\otimes\bar{Q})}=\left\lVert{\phi_{1}}\right\rVert_{L_{2}(P_{VX})}+\left\lVert{\phi_{1}}\right\rVert_{L_{2}(P_{V}\otimes\bar{Q})}

with
\left\lVert{\phi_{1}}\right\rVert_{L_{2}(P_{VX})}=\alpha\left\lVert{k}\right\rVert_{L_{2}(P_{VX})}\leq\sqrt{\alpha}\left\lVert{k}\right\rVert_{L_{2}(P_{VZ})}
by
Lemma

4
(iv)
, and

\left\lVert{\phi_{2}}\right\rVert_{L_{2}(P_{VX})\cap L_{2}(P_{V}\otimes\bar{Q})}=2\left\lVert{\phi_{2}}\right\rVert_{L_{2}(P_{V})}.

By Jensen’s inequality,

\left(\int_{\mathfrak{X}}k(v,z)\bar{Q}(\mathrm{d}z)\right)^{2}\leq\int_{\mathfrak{X}}k(v,z)^{2}\bar{Q}(\mathrm{d}z),

thus

\displaystyle\left\lVert{\phi_{2}}\right\rVert_{L_{2}(P_{V})}\leq(1-\alpha)\sqrt{\int_{\mathfrak{V}}\int_{\mathfrak{X}}k(v,z)^{2}\bar{Q}(\mathrm{d}z)P_{V}(\mathrm{d}v)}

\displaystyle=(1-\alpha)\sqrt{\int_{\mathfrak{V}}\int_{\mathfrak{X}}k(v,z)^{2}\bar{q}(z)\nu_{X}(\mathrm{d}z)p_{V}(v)\nu_{V}(\mathrm{d}v)}

\displaystyle\leq\sqrt{(1-\alpha)}\sqrt{\int_{\mathfrak{V}}\int_{\mathfrak{X}}k(v,z)^{2}p_{VZ}(v,z)\nu_{X}(\mathrm{d}z)\nu_{V}(\mathrm{d}v)}=\sqrt{(1-\alpha)}\left\lVert{k}\right\rVert_{L_{2}(P_{VZ})},

where we used that by
Lemma

4

(iii)
,

\bar{q}(z)p_{V}(v)=\frac{1}{1-\alpha}p_{VZ}(v,z)-\frac{\alpha}{1-\alpha}p_{VX}(v,z)\leq\frac{1}{1-\alpha}p_{VZ}(v,z)

for
\alpha\in(0,1)
. Finally, by the previous display,
\left\lVert{\phi_{1}}\right\rVert_{L_{2}(P_{V}\otimes\bar{Q})}\leq\sqrt{\frac{1}{1-\alpha}}\left\lVert{\phi_{1}}\right\rVert_{L_{2}(P_{VZ})}
, yielding

\displaystyle\left\lVert{Q_{\mathcal{X}}k}\right\rVert_{L_{2}(P_{VX})\cap L_{2}(P_{V}\otimes\bar{Q})}

\displaystyle\leq\sqrt{\alpha}\left\lVert{k}\right\rVert_{L_{2}(P_{VZ})}+\frac{\alpha}{\sqrt{1-\alpha}}\left\lVert{k}\right\rVert_{L_{2}(P_{VZ})}+2\sqrt{(1-\alpha)}\left\lVert{k}\right\rVert_{L_{2}(P_{VZ})}.

Assertion
(vi)
. It suffices to show
Q_{\mathcal{X}}(Q_{\mathcal{X}}^{-1}h)=h
for all
h\in L_{2}(P_{VX})\cap L_{2}(P_{V}\otimes\bar{Q})
. Under (
16
),

\displaystyle(Q_{\mathcal{X}}(Q_{\mathcal{X}}^{-1}h))(v,x)=\alpha(Q_{\mathcal{X}}^{-1}h)(v,x)+(1-\alpha)\int_{\mathfrak{X}}(Q_{\mathcal{X}}^{-1}h)(v,z)\bar{Q}(\mathrm{d}z).

Here, the first term is

\displaystyle\alpha(Q_{\mathcal{X}}^{-1}h)(v,x)

\displaystyle=\alpha\left\{\frac{1}{\alpha}h(v,x)-\frac{1-\alpha}{\alpha}\int_{\mathfrak{X}}h(v,t)\bar{Q}(\mathrm{d}t)\right\}

\displaystyle=h(v,x)-(1-\alpha)\int_{\mathfrak{X}}h(v,t)\bar{Q}(\mathrm{d}t),

while the second term is

\displaystyle(1-\alpha)\int_{\mathfrak{X}}(Q_{\mathcal{X}}^{-1}h)(v,z)\bar{Q}(\mathrm{d}z)

\displaystyle=(1-\alpha)\int_{\mathfrak{X}}\left\{\frac{1}{\alpha}h(v,z)-\frac{1-\alpha}{\alpha}\int_{\mathfrak{X}}h(v,t)\bar{Q}(\mathrm{d}t)\right\}\bar{Q}(\mathrm{d}z)

\displaystyle=(1-\alpha)\left\{\frac{1}{\alpha}\int_{\mathfrak{X}}h(v,z)\bar{Q}(\mathrm{d}z)-\frac{1-\alpha}{\alpha}\int_{\mathfrak{X}}h(v,t)\bar{Q}(\mathrm{d}t)\right\}

\displaystyle=(1-\alpha)\int_{\mathfrak{X}}h(v,t)\bar{Q}(\mathrm{d}t),

where we used that
\bar{Q}
is a probability measure with
\int_{\mathfrak{X}}\bar{Q}(\mathrm{d}z)=1
. Collecting terms, conclude that
Q_{\mathcal{X}}(Q_{\mathcal{X}}^{-1}h)=h
.
We showed that
Q_{\mathcal{X}}^{-1}
is the inverse of
Q_{\mathcal{X}}
, which is by
(v)
a bounded operator.

To see that
Q_{\mathcal{X}}^{-1}:L_{2}(P_{VX})\cap L_{2}(P_{V}\otimes\bar{Q})\to L_{2}(P_{VZ})
is bounded, take some
h\in L_{2}(P_{VX})\cap L_{2}(P_{V}\otimes\bar{Q})
. As
(Q_{\mathcal{X}}^{-1}h)(v,z)=\frac{1}{\alpha}h(v,z)-\frac{1-\alpha}{\alpha}\int_{\mathfrak{X}}h(v,x)\bar{Q}(\mathrm{d}x)
, we have

\displaystyle\left\lVert{Q_{\mathcal{X}}^{-1}h}\right\rVert_{L_{2}(P_{VZ})}\leq\frac{1}{\alpha}\left\lVert{h}\right\rVert_{L_{2}(P_{VZ})}+\frac{1-\alpha}{\alpha}\left\lVert{\int_{\mathfrak{X}}h(\cdot,x)\bar{Q}(\mathrm{d}x)}\right\rVert_{L_{2}(P_{V})},

where, by
Lemma

4

(ii)
,

\displaystyle\left\lVert{h}\right\rVert_{L_{2}(P_{VZ})}=\sqrt{\int h^{2}\,\mathrm{d}[\alpha P_{VX}+(1-\alpha)P_{V}\otimes\bar{Q}]}

\displaystyle\leq\sqrt{\left\lVert{h}\right\rVert_{L_{2}(P_{VX})}^{2}+\left\lVert{h}\right\rVert_{L_{2}(P_{V}\otimes\bar{Q})}^{2}}\leq\sqrt{(\left\lVert{h}\right\rVert_{L_{2}(P_{VX})}+\left\lVert{h}\right\rVert_{L_{2}(P_{V}\otimes\bar{Q})})^{2}}

\displaystyle\leq\left\lVert{h}\right\rVert_{L_{2}(P_{VX})}+\left\lVert{h}\right\rVert_{L_{2}(P_{V}\otimes\bar{Q})}=\left\lVert{h}\right\rVert_{L_{2}(P_{VX})\cap L_{2}(P_{V}\otimes\bar{Q})}.

By Jensen’s inequality,

\displaystyle\left\lVert{\int_{\mathfrak{X}}h(\cdot,x)\bar{Q}(\mathrm{d}x)}\right\rVert_{L_{2}(P_{V})}

\displaystyle\leq\sqrt{\int h^{2}\,\mathrm{d}P_{V}\otimes\bar{Q}}=\left\lVert{h}\right\rVert_{L_{2}(P_{V}\otimes\bar{Q})}\leq\left\lVert{h}\right\rVert_{L_{2}(P_{VX})\cap L_{2}(P_{V}\otimes\bar{Q})},

whereby

\displaystyle\left\lVert{Q_{\mathcal{X}}^{-1}h}\right\rVert_{L_{2}(P_{VZ})}\leq\frac{1}{\alpha}\left\lVert{h}\right\rVert_{L_{2}(P_{VX})\cap L_{2}(P_{V}\otimes\bar{Q})}+\frac{1-\alpha}{\alpha}\left\lVert{h}\right\rVert_{L_{2}(P_{VX})\cap L_{2}(P_{V}\otimes\bar{Q})}.

Assertion
(vii)
. By
(vi)
,

\displaystyle(Q_{\mathcal{X}}^{-1}h)^{2}(v,z)=\frac{1}{\alpha^{2}}h^{2}(v,z)-2\frac{1-\alpha}{\alpha^{2}}h(v,z)\int h(v,x)\bar{Q}(\mathrm{d}x)+\left(\frac{1-\alpha}{\alpha}\int h(v,x)\bar{Q}(\mathrm{d}x)\right)^{2}.

(69)

Here, because
xy\leq(x^{2}+y^{2})/2
for all
x,y\in\real
,

\displaystyle\bigg|h(v,z)\int h(v,x)\bar{Q}(\mathrm{d}x)\bigg|\leq\frac{1}{2}\left(h^{2}(v,z)+\left(\int h(v,x)\bar{Q}(\mathrm{d}x)\right)^{2}\right).

(70)

Then (
69
) yields the lower bound

\displaystyle P_{VZ}(Q_{\mathcal{X}}^{-1}h)^{2}\geq\frac{1}{\alpha^{2}}P_{VZ}h^{2}-\frac{1-\alpha}{\alpha^{2}}\left(P_{VZ}h^{2}+P_{V}\left(\int h(V,x)\bar{Q}(\mathrm{d}x)\right)^{2}\right)

\displaystyle+P_{V}\left(\frac{1-\alpha}{\alpha}\int h(V,x)\bar{Q}(\mathrm{d}x)\right)^{2}=\frac{1}{\alpha}P_{VZ}h^{2}-\frac{1-\alpha}{\alpha}P_{V}\left(\int h(V,x)\bar{Q}(\mathrm{d}x)\right)^{2}

Here,
P_{VZ}=\alpha P_{VX}+(1-\alpha)P_{V}\otimes\bar{Q}
by
Lemma

4
, which proves the lower bound. For the upper bound, (
69
) and (
70
) give

\displaystyle P_{VZ}(Q_{\mathcal{X}}^{-1}h)^{2}\leq\frac{1}{\alpha^{2}}P_{VZ}h^{2}+\frac{1-\alpha}{\alpha^{2}}\left(P_{VZ}h^{2}+P_{V}\left(\int h(V,x)\bar{Q}(\mathrm{d}x)\right)^{2}\right)

\displaystyle+P_{V}\left(\frac{1-\alpha}{\alpha}\int h(V,x)\bar{Q}(\mathrm{d}x)\right)^{2}=\frac{2-\alpha}{\alpha^{2}}P_{VZ}h^{2}+\frac{(2-\alpha)(1-\alpha)}{\alpha^{2}}P_{V}\left(\int h(V,x)\bar{Q}(\mathrm{d}x)\right)^{2}.

Then
P_{VZ}=\alpha P_{VX}+(1-\alpha)P_{V}\otimes\bar{Q}
and Jensen’s inequality
P_{V}\left(\int h(v,x)\bar{Q}(\mathrm{d}x)\right)^{2}\leq(P_{V}\otimes\bar{Q})h^{2}
prove the upper bound.
∎

By the construction of
Z_{i}
in
Section

4.1
, the sequence
((V_{i},X_{i},Z_{i}))_{i\in[n]}
is an i.i.d. sample from the distribution of the partly unobserved data
(V,X,Z)
,

\displaystyle P_{VXZ}(B_{\mathsf{v}},B_{\mathsf{x}},B_{\mathsf{z}})

\displaystyle=\int\mathbbm{1}_{(v,x)\in B_{\mathsf{v}}\times B_{\mathsf{x}}}Q(B_{\mathsf{z}}{|\,}x)\,\mathrm{d}P_{VX}(v,x)

(71)

for
B_{\mathsf{v}}\in\mathscr{F}_{\mathfrak{V}},B_{\mathsf{x}}\in\mathscr{F}_{\mathfrak{X}},B_{\mathsf{z}}\in\mathscr{F}_{\mathfrak{Z}}
. When
Q\in\mathcal{Q}_{\delta}
in (
16
), this distribution is as follows.

Lemma 4

(Distributions under (
16
))
.

If the mechanism
Q\in\mathcal{Q}_{\delta}
in (
16
), then:

(i)

The joint distribution of
(V,X,Z)
is
P_{VXZ}(B_{\mathsf{v}},B_{\mathsf{x}},B_{\mathsf{z}})=\alpha P_{VX}(B_{\mathsf{v}},B_{\mathsf{x}}\cap B_{\mathsf{z}})+(1-\alpha)P_{VX}(B_{\mathsf{v}},B_{\mathsf{x}})\bar{Q}(B_{\mathsf{z}})
for
B_{\mathsf{v}}\in\mathscr{F}_{\mathfrak{V}},B_{\mathsf{x}}\in\mathscr{F}_{\mathfrak{X}},B_{\mathsf{z}}\in\mathscr{F}_{\mathfrak{Z}}
.

(ii)

The joint distribution of
(V,Z)
is
P_{VZ}(B_{\mathsf{v}},B_{\mathsf{z}})=\alpha P_{VX}(B_{\mathsf{v}},B_{\mathsf{z}})+(1-\alpha)P_{V}(B_{\mathsf{v}})\bar{Q}(B_{\mathsf{z}})
for
B_{\mathsf{v}}\in\mathscr{F}_{\mathfrak{V}},B_{\mathsf{z}}\in\mathscr{F}_{\mathfrak{Z}}
. Hence, we have the absolute-continuity relations
P_{VX}\ll P_{VZ}
, so
P_{X}\ll P_{Z}
, and
\bar{Q}\ll P_{Z}
.

(iii)

If
P_{VX}
has density
p_{VX}
with respect to some measure
\nu_{V}\times\nu_{X}
and
\bar{Q}
has density
\bar{q}
with respect to
\nu_{X}
, then
P_{VZ}
has density
p_{VZ}(v,z)\coloneqq\alpha p_{VX}(v,z)+(1-\alpha)p_{V}(v)\bar{q}(z)
for
(v,z)\in\mathfrak{V}\times\mathfrak{X}
with respect to
\nu_{V}\times\nu_{X}
.

(iv)

For all
h:\mathfrak{V}\times\mathfrak{X}\to\real
and all
p\in[1,\infty]
,
\left\lVert{h}\right\rVert_{L_{p}(P_{VX})}\leq\alpha^{-1/p}\left\lVert{h}\right\rVert_{L_{p}(P_{VZ})}
.

(v)

The Markov kernel

\displaystyle P_{X\mid Z}(B{|\,}z)

\displaystyle\coloneqq\alpha\frac{\mathrm{d}P_{X}}{\mathrm{d}P_{Z}}(z)\delta_{z}(B)+(1-\alpha)P_{X}(B)\frac{\mathrm{d}\bar{Q}}{\mathrm{d}P_{Z}}(z),\quad B\in\mathscr{F}_{\mathfrak{X}},z\in\mathfrak{X},

is the conditional distribution of
X
given
Z=z
, where the Radom-Nykodým derivatives
\frac{\mathrm{d}P_{X}}{\mathrm{d}P_{Z}}
,
\frac{\mathrm{d}\bar{Q}}{\mathrm{d}P_{Z}}
exist by
(ii)
. If
P_{VX}
and
\bar{Q}
have densities
p_{VX}
and
\bar{q}
with respect to
\nu_{V}\times\nu_{X}
and
\nu_{X}
, respectively, then for
p_{Z}
induced by
(ii)
, the last display is equal to

\displaystyle P_{X\mid Z}(B{|\,}z)=\alpha\frac{p_{X}(z)}{p_{Z}(z)}\delta_{z}(B)+(1-\alpha)\frac{\bar{q}(z)}{p_{Z}(z)}P_{X}(B),\quad B\in\mathscr{F}_{\mathfrak{X}},z\in\mathfrak{X}.

(vi)

Suppose that
X
given
V=v
,
v\in\mathfrak{V}
, admits a conditional distribution
P_{X{|\,}V}(\cdot{|\,}v)
. Then the Markov kernel

\displaystyle P_{Z{|\,}V}(B{|\,}v)\coloneqq\alpha P_{X{|\,}V}(B{|\,}v)+(1-\alpha)\bar{Q}(B),\quad B\in\mathscr{F}_{\mathfrak{X}},v\in\mathfrak{V},

is the conditional distribution of
Z
given
V=v
. Hence,
\bar{Q}\ll P_{Z{|\,}V}(\cdot{|\,}v)
for any
v\in\mathfrak{V}
.

(vii)

Suppose that
X
given
V=v
,
v\in\mathfrak{V}
, admits a conditional distribution
P_{X{|\,}V}(\cdot{|\,}v)
. For each
v\in\mathfrak{V}
, let
\bar{q}(z{|\,}v)\coloneqq\frac{\mathrm{d}\bar{Q}}{\mathrm{d}P_{Z{|\,}V}(\cdot{|\,}v)}(z)
be the Radom-Nykodým derivative of
\bar{Q}
with respect to
P_{Z{|\,}V}(\cdot{|\,}v)
, which exists by
(vi)
. Then the Markov kernel

\displaystyle P_{X{|\,}VZ}(B{|\,}v,z)\coloneqq\alpha\frac{\mathrm{d}P_{VX}}{\mathrm{d}P_{VZ}}(v,z)\delta_{z}(B)+(1-\alpha)\bar{q}(z{|\,}v)P_{X{|\,}V}(B{|\,}v),

for
B\in\mathscr{F}_{\mathfrak{X}},(v,z)\in\mathfrak{V}\times\mathfrak{X}
, is the conditional distribution of
X
given
(V,Z)=(v,z)
.

Proof of
Lemma

4
.

Assertion
(i)
. Plug (
16
) into (
71
), using the definition of the Dirac measure
\delta_{x}(B)=\mathbbm{1}_{x\in B}
, to find

\displaystyle P_{VXZ}(B_{\mathsf{v}},B_{\mathsf{x}},B_{\mathsf{z}})=

\displaystyle\ \alpha\int\mathbbm{1}_{(v,x)\in B_{\mathsf{v}}\times B_{\mathsf{x}}}\mathbbm{1}_{x\in B_{\mathsf{z}}}\,\mathrm{d}P_{VX}(v,x)

\displaystyle+(1-\alpha)\bar{Q}(B_{\mathsf{z}})\int\mathbbm{1}_{(v,x)\in B_{\mathsf{v}}\times B_{\mathsf{x}}}\,\mathrm{d}P_{VX}(v,x)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\,\alpha\int\mathbbm{1}_{(v,x)\in B_{\mathsf{v}}\times(B_{\mathsf{x}}\cap B_{\mathsf{z}})}\,\mathrm{d}P_{VX}(v,x)

\displaystyle+(1-\alpha)\bar{Q}(B_{\mathsf{z}})\int\mathbbm{1}_{(v,x)\in B_{\mathsf{v}}\times B_{\mathsf{x}}}\,\mathrm{d}P_{VX}(v,x)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\,\alpha P_{VX}(B_{\mathsf{v}},B_{\mathsf{x}}\cap B_{\mathsf{z}})+(1-\alpha)\bar{Q}(B_{\mathsf{z}})P_{VX}(B_{\mathsf{v}},B_{\mathsf{x}}).

Assertion
(ii)
. Follows from
(i)
as the marginal distribution by setting
B_{\mathsf{x}}\coloneqq\mathfrak{X}
.

Assertion
(iii)
. A convex combination of two densities,
p_{VZ}
is nonnegative. From
(ii)
, write
P_{VZ}(B_{\mathsf{v}},B_{\mathsf{z}})
as

\displaystyle\alpha\int_{B_{\mathsf{v}}}\int_{B_{\mathsf{z}}}p_{VX}(v,x)\,\mathrm{d}\nu_{X}(x)\,\mathrm{d}\nu_{V}(v)+(1-\alpha)\int_{B_{\mathsf{z}}}\bar{q}(z)\,\mathrm{d}\nu_{X}(x)\int_{B_{\mathsf{v}}}p_{V}(v)\,\mathrm{d}\nu_{V}(v)

\displaystyle=\int_{B_{\mathsf{v}}}\int_{B_{\mathsf{z}}}\big\{\alpha p_{VX}(v,x)+(1-\alpha)\bar{q}(x)p_{V}(v)\big\}\,\mathrm{d}\nu_{X}(x)\,\mathrm{d}\nu_{V}(v).

Assertion
(iv)
. By
(ii)
,
P_{VX}\leq\alpha^{-1}P_{VZ}
. Hence,

\displaystyle\left\lVert{h}\right\rVert_{L_{p}(P_{VX})}=\left(\int_{\mathfrak{X}}\int_{\mathfrak{V}}|h(v,x)|^{p}\,\mathrm{d}P_{VX}(v,x)\right)^{1/p}\leq\alpha^{-1/p}\left\lVert{h}\right\rVert_{L_{p}(P_{VZ})}.

Assertion
(v)
. It is sufficient and necessary to verify that for the
P_{X|Z}
given in
(v)
,
P_{XZ}(B_{\mathsf{x}},B_{\mathsf{z}})=\int_{B_{\mathsf{z}}}P_{X|Z}(B_{\mathsf{x}}{|\,}z)\,\mathrm{d}P_{Z}(z)
. From the right,

\displaystyle\int_{B_{\mathsf{z}}}P_{X|Z}(B_{\mathsf{x}}{|\,}z)\,\mathrm{d}P_{Z}(z)

\displaystyle=\int_{B_{\mathsf{z}}}\bigg\{\alpha\frac{\mathrm{d}P_{X}}{\mathrm{d}P_{Z}}(z)\delta_{z}(B_{\mathsf{x}})+(1-\alpha)P_{X}(B_{\mathsf{x}})\frac{\mathrm{d}\bar{Q}}{\mathrm{d}P_{Z}}(z)\bigg\}\,\mathrm{d}P_{Z}(z)

\displaystyle=\alpha\int_{B_{\mathsf{z}}\cap B_{\mathsf{x}}}\frac{\mathrm{d}P_{X}}{\mathrm{d}P_{Z}}(z)\,\mathrm{d}P_{Z}(z)+(1-\alpha)P_{X}(B_{\mathsf{x}})\int_{B_{\mathsf{z}}}\frac{\mathrm{d}\bar{Q}}{\mathrm{d}P_{Z}}(z)\,\mathrm{d}P_{Z}(z)

\displaystyle=\alpha P_{X}(B_{\mathsf{x}}\cap B_{\mathsf{z}})+(1-\alpha)P_{X}(B_{\mathsf{x}})\bar{Q}(B_{\mathsf{z}}),

in which we recognise
P_{XZ}(B_{\mathsf{x}},B_{\mathsf{z}})
by
(i)
.

Assertion
(vii)
. Follows from the same arguments as
(v)
.

Assertion
(vii)
. Follows from the same arguments as
(v)
, by verifying that for the
P_{X{|\,}VZ}
given in the assertion, we have
P_{VXZ}(B_{\mathsf{v}},B,B_{\mathsf{z}})=\int_{B_{\mathsf{v}}\times B_{\mathsf{z}}}P_{X{|\,}VZ}(B{|\,}v,z)\,\mathrm{d}P_{VZ}(v,z)
for
P_{VXZ}
in
(i)
and for all
B_{\mathsf{v}}\in\mathscr{F}_{\mathfrak{V}}
,
B\in\mathscr{F}_{\mathfrak{X}}
,
B_{\mathsf{z}}\in\mathscr{F}_{\mathfrak{X}}
. Specifically, noting that
\mathrm{d}P_{VZ}(v,z)=P_{Z{|\,}V}(\mathrm{d}z{|\,}v)\mathrm{d}P_{V}(v)
by definition of the conditional distribution
P_{Z{|\,}V}
, we have from the right,

\displaystyle\int_{B_{\mathsf{v}}\times B_{\mathsf{z}}}\left\{\alpha\frac{\mathrm{d}P_{VX}}{\mathrm{d}P_{VZ}}(v,z)\delta_{z}(B)+(1-\alpha)\bar{q}(z{|\,}v)P_{X{|\,}V}(B{|\,}v)\right\}\,\mathrm{d}P_{VZ}(v,z)

\displaystyle=\alpha\int_{B_{\mathsf{v}}\times(B_{\mathsf{z}}\cap B)}\frac{\mathrm{d}P_{VX}}{\mathrm{d}P_{VZ}}(v,z)\,\mathrm{d}P_{VZ}(v,z)

\displaystyle+(1-\alpha)\int_{B_{\mathsf{v}}}P_{X{|\,}V}(B{|\,}v)\int_{B_{\mathsf{z}}}\frac{\mathrm{d}\bar{Q}}{\mathrm{d}P_{Z{|\,}V}(\cdot{|\,}v)}(z)P_{Z{|\,}V}(\mathrm{d}z{|\,}v)\mathrm{d}P_{V}(v)

\displaystyle=\alpha P_{VX}(B_{\mathsf{v}},B_{\mathsf{z}}\cap B)+(1-\alpha)\bar{Q}(B_{\mathsf{z}})\int_{B_{\mathsf{v}}}P_{X{|\,}V}(B{|\,}v)\mathrm{d}P_{V}(v)

\displaystyle=\alpha P_{VX}(B_{\mathsf{v}},B_{\mathsf{z}}\cap B)+(1-\alpha)\bar{Q}(B_{\mathsf{z}})P_{VX}(B_{\mathsf{v}},B),

in which we recognise
P_{VXZ}(B_{\mathsf{v}},B,B_{\mathsf{z}})
in
(i)
.
∎

Lemma

5
is key in proving
Lemma

2
.

Lemma 5

(Norms and continuity of
Q_{\mathcal{X}},Q_{\mathcal{X}}^{-1}
)
.

With
c_{Q}
, we denote strictly positive constants which depend only on
Q
and whose value may differ in every display in this lemma.

If
Q\in\mathcal{Q}_{\delta}
in (
16
), or
Q\in\mathcal{Q}_{J}
in (
13
) with
\min_{(z,x)\in\mathfrak{X}^{2}}Q(\left\{z\right\}{|\,}x)>0
, then for all
h:\mathfrak{V}\times\mathfrak{X}\to\real
and all
p\in[1,\infty]
,

\displaystyle\left\lVert{h}\right\rVert_{L_{p}(P_{VX})}\leq c_{Q}\left\lVert{h}\right\rVert_{L_{p}(P_{VZ})}.

(72)

If
Q\in\mathcal{Q}^{\mathrm{I}}_{J}
in (
14
) and
0<\inf_{(v,x)\in\mathfrak{V}\times\mathfrak{X}}p_{VX}(v,x)\leq\sup_{(v,x)\in\mathfrak{V}\times\mathfrak{X}}p_{VX}(v,x)<\infty
, then for all
h\in L_{2}(P_{VX})
,

\displaystyle\left\lVert{Q_{\mathcal{X}}^{-1}h}\right\rVert_{L_{2}(P_{VZ})}

\displaystyle\leq c_{Q}\left\lVert{h}\right\rVert_{L_{2}(P_{VX})}.

(73)

If
Q\in\mathcal{Q}_{\delta}
in (
16
), we have for all
h\in L_{2}(P_{VX})\cap L_{2}(P_{V}\otimes\bar{Q})
,

\displaystyle\left\lVert{Q_{\mathcal{X}}^{-1}h}\right\rVert_{L_{2}(P_{VZ})}

\displaystyle\leq c_{Q}\left\lVert{h}\right\rVert_{L_{2}(P_{VX})\cap L_{2}(P_{V}\otimes\bar{Q})}.

(74)

For any
Q\in\mathcal{Q}(\mathfrak{X}\to\mathfrak{Z})
, we have for all
k\in L_{2}(P_{VZ})
,

\displaystyle\left\lVert{Q_{\mathcal{X}}k}\right\rVert_{L_{2}(P_{VX})}

\displaystyle\leq\left\lVert{k}\right\rVert_{L_{2}(P_{VZ})}.

(75)

Proof of
Lemma

5
.

Suppose that
Q\in\mathcal{Q}_{\delta}
. Then (
72
) is
Lemma

4

(iv)
; (
74
) is
Lemma

3

(vi)
.

Suppose now that
X
is distributed on a finite set
\mathfrak{X}=\mathfrak{Z}
and
P_{VX}
has density
p_{VX}
. First, we show (
72
). We have, for any
c>0
,

\displaystyle\left\lVert{h}\right\rVert_{L_{p}(P_{VZ})}^{p}-\frac{1}{c}\left\lVert{h}\right\rVert_{L_{p}(P_{VX})}^{p}

\displaystyle=\int_{\mathfrak{V}}\sum_{x\in\mathfrak{X}}|h(v,x)|^{p}\left(p_{VZ}(v,x)-\frac{1}{c}p_{VX}(v,x)\right)\,\mathrm{d}\nu_{V}(v).

By assumption,

\underline{q}\coloneqq\min_{(x,\bar{x})\in\mathcal{X}^{2}}Q(\left\{x\right\}{|\,}\bar{x})>0.

Since
p_{VZ}(v,x)=\sum_{\bar{x}\in\mathfrak{X}}Q(\left\{x\right\}{|\,}\bar{x})p_{VX}(v,\bar{x})
, we have

\displaystyle p_{VZ}(v,x)-\frac{1}{c}p_{VX}(v,x)\geq\underline{q}\sum_{\bar{x}\in\mathfrak{X}}p_{VX}(v,\bar{x})-\frac{1}{c}p_{VX}(v,x)

\displaystyle=\underline{q}\sum_{\bar{x}\in\mathfrak{X}:\bar{x}\neq x}p_{VX}(v,\bar{x})+\left(\underline{q}-\frac{1}{c}\right)p_{VX}(v,x).

Hence, setting
c\coloneqq 1/\underline{q}
implies
p_{VZ}(v,x)-\frac{1}{c}p_{VX}(v,x)\geq 0
. Thus

\left\lVert{h}\right\rVert_{L_{p}(P_{VZ})}^{p}-\frac{1}{c}\left\lVert{h}\right\rVert_{L_{p}(P_{VX})}^{p}=\left\lVert{h}\right\rVert_{L_{p}(P_{VZ})}^{p}-\underline{q}\left\lVert{h}\right\rVert_{L_{p}(P_{VX})}^{p}\geq 0.

Second, we show (
73
). Consider the matrix representation of
Q_{\mathcal{X}}^{-1}
in
Lemma

3

(iv)
. The linear operator (matrix)
Q:\real^{J\times J}\to\real^{J\times 1}
has inverse
Q^{-1}
because
Q\in\mathcal{Q}_{\psi}
. As
(Q^{-1})^{\intercal}:\real^{J\times J}\to\real^{J\times 1}
is a linear operator on a finite-dimensional space, it is a continuous and bounded linear operator (e.g.
28
). Whence,
\left\lVert{(Q^{-1})^{\intercal}t}\right\rVert_{2}\lesssim\left\lVert{t}\right\rVert_{2}
for all
t\in\real^{J\times 1}
, where
\left\lVert{.}\right\rVert_{2}
is the Euclidean norm on
J×1
. The assertion follows by

\displaystyle\left\lVert{Q_{\mathcal{X}}^{-1}h}\right\rVert_{L_{2}(P_{VZ})}^{2}=\int_{\mathfrak{V}}\sum_{z\in\mathfrak{X}}\left[(Q_{\mathcal{X}}^{-1}h)(v,z)\right]^{2}p_{VZ}(v,z)\,\mathrm{d}\nu_{V}(v)

\displaystyle\leq\left\lVert{p_{VZ}}\right\rVert_{\infty}\int_{\mathfrak{V}}\sum_{z\in\mathfrak{X}}\left[(Q_{\mathcal{X}}^{-1}h)(v,z)\right]^{2}\,\mathrm{d}\nu_{V}(v)\lesssim\int_{\mathfrak{V}}\sum_{z\in\mathfrak{X}}\left[\sum_{x\in\mathfrak{X}}((Q^{-1})^{\intercal})_{z,x}h(v,x)\right]^{2}\,\mathrm{d}\nu_{V}(v)

\displaystyle=\int_{\mathfrak{V}}\left\lVert{(Q^{-1})^{\intercal}(h(v,x))_{x\in\mathcal{X}}}\right\rVert_{2}^{2}\,\mathrm{d}\nu_{V}(v)\lesssim\int_{\mathfrak{V}}\left\lVert{(h(v,x))_{x\in\mathcal{X}}}\right\rVert_{2}^{2}\,\mathrm{d}\nu_{V}(v)

\displaystyle=\int_{\mathfrak{V}}\sum_{x\in\mathfrak{X}}h(v,x)^{2}\,\mathrm{d}\nu_{V}(v)\simeq\int_{\mathfrak{V}}\sum_{x\in\mathfrak{X}}h(v,x)^{2}p_{VX}(v,x)\,\mathrm{d}\nu_{V}(v)=\left\lVert{h}\right\rVert_{L_{2}(P_{VX})},

since
\left\lVert{p_{VZ}}\right\rVert_{\infty}<\infty
by the assumption
\left\lVert{p_{VX}}\right\rVert_{\infty}<\infty
, and
\inf_{(v,x)\in\mathfrak{V}\times\mathfrak{X}}p_{VX}(v,x)>0
by assumption.

Finally, we show (
75
). By
Lemma

3

(i)
and Jensen’s inequality,

((Q_{\mathcal{X}}k)(v,x))^{2}\leq\mathbb{E}\left[\left.k(V,Z)^{2}\,\right|\,V=v,X=x\right].

But then

\displaystyle\left\lVert{Q_{\mathcal{X}}k}\right\rVert_{L_{2}(P_{VX})}^{2}=\mathbb{E}\left[((Q_{\mathcal{X}}k)(V,X))^{2}\right]

\displaystyle\leq\mathbb{E}\left[\mathbb{E}\left[\left.k(V,Z)^{2}\,\right|\,V,X\right]\right]=\mathbb{E}k(V,Z)^{2}=\left\lVert{k}\right\rVert_{L_{2}(P_{VZ})}^{2}.

∎

C.4.  Total-Variational and Differential Privacy

Recall the definition of differential privacy, which is one of the most common privacy guarantees.

Definition 2

(Local
(\alpha,\delta)
-Differential Privacy:
(\alpha,\delta)
-LDP
(
18
)
)
.

For
\alpha,\delta\geq 0
, the privacy mechanism
Q\in\mathcal{Q}(\mathfrak{X}\to\mathfrak{Z})
is locally
(\alpha,\delta)
-differentially private if
Q(B{|\,}x)\leq e^{\alpha}Q(B{|\,}x^{\prime})+\delta
for all
B\in\mathscr{F}_{\mathfrak{Z}}
and for all
x,x^{\prime}\in\mathfrak{X}
.

Lemma

6
shows how
\alpha
-LTVP in
Definition

1
and
(\alpha,\delta)
-LDP are related. An
\alpha
-LTVP is always an
(.,\delta=\alpha)
-LDP, which provides weaker privacy guarantee than an
(\alpha,\delta=0)
-LDP. Conversely, when
(\alpha,\delta)
is small enough — so that privacy is strict —,
(\alpha,\delta)
-LDP is an
(e^{\alpha}-1+\delta)
-LTVP. For example, an
(\alpha,\delta=0)
-LDP is also an
(e^{\alpha}-1)
-LTVP for
\alpha\leq\log(2)\approx 0.69
.

Lemma 6

(
(\alpha,\delta)
-LDP and
\alpha
-LTVP)
.

For all
0\leq\alpha\leq 1
, every
\alpha
-LTVP mechanism is
(\tilde{\alpha},\alpha)
-LDP for any
\tilde{\alpha}\geq 0
.
For all
\alpha,\delta\geq 0
such that
e^{\alpha}-1+\delta\leq 1
, every
(\alpha,\delta)
-LDP mechanism is
(e^{\alpha}-1+\delta)
-LTVP.

Proof.

Let
Q
be
\alpha
-LTVP. As
|Q|=Q
, we have for all
B\in\mathscr{F}_{\mathfrak{Z}}
,

\displaystyle Q(B{|\,}x)=|Q(B{|\,}x)|=|Q(B{|\,}x)-Q(B{|\,}x^{\prime})+Q(B{|\,}x^{\prime})|

\displaystyle\leq|Q(B{|\,}x^{\prime})|+|Q(B{|\,}x)-Q(B{|\,}x^{\prime})|\leq Q(B{|\,}x^{\prime})+\alpha\leq e^{\tilde{\alpha}}Q(B{|\,}x^{\prime})+\alpha

for all
x,x^{\prime}\in\mathfrak{X}
and for any
\tilde{\alpha}\geq 0
. Hence,
Q
is
(\tilde{\alpha},\alpha)
-LDP.

Now let
Q
be an
(\alpha,\delta)
-LDP mechanism. Then for all
x,x^{\prime}\in\mathfrak{X}
and
B\in\mathscr{F}_{\mathfrak{Z}}
,

\displaystyle|Q(B{|\,}x)-Q(B{|\,}x^{\prime})|=\begin{cases}Q(B{|\,}x)-Q(B{|\,}x^{\prime})\quad&\text{ if }\,Q(B{|\,}x)-Q(B{|\,}x^{\prime})\geq 0\\
Q(B{|\,}x^{\prime})-Q(B{|\,}x)\quad&\text{ if }\,Q(B{|\,}x)-Q(B{|\,}x^{\prime})<0\end{cases}

\displaystyle\leq\begin{cases}e^{\alpha}Q(B{|\,}x^{\prime})+\delta-Q(B{|\,}x^{\prime})\quad&\text{ if }\,Q(B{|\,}x)-Q(B{|\,}x^{\prime})\geq 0\\
e^{\alpha}Q(B{|\,}x)+\delta-Q(B{|\,}x)\quad&\text{ if }\,Q(B{|\,}x)-Q(B{|\,}x^{\prime})<0\end{cases}

\displaystyle=\begin{cases}(e^{\alpha}-1)Q(B{|\,}x^{\prime})+\delta\quad&\text{ if }\,Q(B{|\,}x)-Q(B{|\,}x^{\prime})\geq 0\\
(e^{\alpha}-1)Q(B{|\,}x)+\delta\quad&\text{ if }\,Q(B{|\,}x)-Q(B{|\,}x^{\prime})<0\end{cases}

\displaystyle\leq e^{\alpha}-1+\delta,

because
Q
maps to
[0,1]
and
e^{\alpha}-1\geq 0
for all
\alpha\geq 0
. Hence, if
e^{\alpha}-1+\delta\leq 1
,
Q
is
(e^{\alpha}-1+\delta)
-LTVP.
∎

C.5.  Invertible Privacy Mechanisms

In
Section

4.1
, we saw that for a generic
X
, a mechanism
Q\in\mathcal{Q}_{\delta}
implies the existence of
L_{Q}
. In some special cases, choices different from
\mathcal{Q}_{\delta}
also imply the existence of
L_{Q}
in (
9
).

The special cases arise when
Q(\cdot{|\,}x)=\bar{Q}_{\mathrm{c}}(\cdot{|\,}x)
, where
\bar{Q}_{\mathrm{c}}(\cdot{|\,}x)
admits a
\nu_{X}
-density
\bar{q}_{\mathrm{c}}(\cdot{|\,}x)
(as is also the case for the discretely distributed covariates). Then inverting (
7
) is equivalent to solving a Fredholm integral equation of the first kind (see e.g.
37
) where
\bar{q}_{\mathrm{c}}
induces a linear integral operator. Such equations are usually ill-posed
(
37
)
, but in a few favourable cases they do admit a unique solution which also retains the nonparametric model class.

One such favourable case occurs when
\mathfrak{Z}=\mathfrak{X}=\real^{K}
and
\bar{q}_{\mathrm{c}}
corresponds to, for example, the Laplace mechanism satisfying
\alpha
-LDP, adding Laplace noise from the Laplace density
p_{\varepsilon}
. Then for the Fourier transform
\mathcal{F}
and its inverse
\mathcal{F}^{-1}
, we have
p_{VX}(v,x)=(\mathcal{F}^{-1}(w\mapsto\frac{(\mathcal{F}p_{VZ}(v,\cdot))(w)}{(\mathcal{F}p_{\varepsilon})(w)}))(x)
by the convolution theorem. Another favourable case occurs when
\mathfrak{Z}=\mathfrak{X}
and
\bar{q}_{\mathrm{c}}
induces a compact integral operator. Then the equation admits a unique and smooth solution (
28
). One class of compact linear operators are Hilbert-Schmidt operators (
40
,
28
). The
\bar{q}_{\mathrm{c}}
induces a Hilbert-Schmidt operator if

\int_{\mathfrak{X}\times\mathfrak{X}}\bar{q}_{\mathrm{c}}(z{|\,}x)^{2}\,\mathrm{d}\nu_{X}(z)\,\mathrm{d}\nu_{X}(x)<\infty

(
40
), or if
(z,x)\mapsto\bar{q}_{\mathrm{c}}(z{|\,}x)
was continuous and the domain
\mathfrak{Z}=\mathfrak{X}\subset\real^{K}
was compact (
28
).

The first, convolution case is specific to privacy achieved by
additive
noise and image space
K
, which is not suitable for a generic covariate under our consideration, for example when
X
also contains coordinates distributed on a finite set. The second, compact case also places restrictions on the domain, or require
\int_{\mathfrak{X}\times\mathfrak{X}}\bar{q}_{\mathrm{c}}(z{|\,}x)^{2}\,\mathrm{d}\nu_{X}(z)\,\mathrm{d}\nu_{X}(x)<\infty
, which we do not expect to hold unless
\mathfrak{X}
is compact (for example, when
\mathfrak{X}=\real
and the mechanism is the additive Laplace noise, then the last integral is infinite). Moreover, these cases assume the existence of a density. In contrast to these, our mechanism (
16
) allows for more generic covariate types and space
\mathfrak{Z}=\mathfrak{X}
handled smoothly by a single mechanism.

Appendix D
Estimation of Nuisance Parameters

This section proves
Proposition

2
and
Proposition

3
of
Section

6
, and details their application to the estimation of the regression and of the Riesz representer.
Section

D.1
contains results on finite-dimensional models, while
Section

D.2
on infinite-dimensional models.

D.1.  Finite-Dimensional Models

In
Section

D.1.1
, we prove
Proposition

2
, and we apply it to the estimation of the regression and the Riesz representer in
Sections

D.1.2
and
D.1.3
, respectively.

D.1.1
Method-of-Moments

Before proving
Proposition

2
on the private method-of-moments, it is helpful to recall the nonprivate version.

Lemma 7

(Nonprivate Generalised method-of-moments (
23
and
33
))
.

Suppose that
\theta_{0}\in\Theta\subset\real^{K}
is the unique minimiser

\theta_{0}=\arg\min_{\theta\in\Theta}P_{VX}\Xi_{\theta},

for a fixed
\Xi_{\theta}:\mathfrak{V}\times\mathfrak{X}\to\real
,
\theta\in\Theta
. Further suppose that the derivative
\mathrm{D}_{\theta}\Xi_{\tilde{\theta}}(v,x)
of
\theta\mapsto\Xi_{\theta}(v,x)
exists at all
\tilde{\theta}\in\mathrm{Nb}(\theta_{0})
, where
\mathrm{Nb}(\theta_{0})
is a neighbourhood of
\theta_{0}
, and for all
(v,x)\in\mathfrak{V}\times\mathfrak{X}
.
Assume that

(i)

The value
\theta_{0}
is in the interior of the compact
\Theta
.

(ii)

Let

\phi_{\tilde{\theta}}(v,x)\coloneqq\mathrm{D}_{\theta}\Xi_{\tilde{\theta}}(v,x)^{\intercal},\quad(v,x)\in\mathfrak{V}\times\mathfrak{X},

be the
K×1
-valued derivative of
\theta\mapsto\Xi_{\theta}(v,x)
at
\tilde{\theta}
. The
\phi_{\theta}
satisfies

\displaystyle\left\lVert{\left\lVert{\phi_{\theta_{0}}}\right\rVert_{2}^{2}}\right\rVert_{L_{1}(P_{VX})}<\infty,

(76)

where, for a fixed
(v,x)\in\mathfrak{V}\times\mathfrak{X}
,
\left\lVert{\phi_{\theta_{0}}}\right\rVert_{2}^{2}(v,x)
is sum of the
K
squared entries of
\phi_{\theta_{0}}(v,x)
.

(iii)

The
K×K
-valued derivative
\dot{\phi}_{\tilde{\theta}}(v,x)\coloneqq\mathrm{D}_{\theta}\phi_{\tilde{\theta}}(v,x)
of
\theta\mapsto\phi_{\theta}(v,x)
at
\tilde{\theta}
exist at all
\tilde{\theta}\in\mathrm{Nb}(\theta_{0})
and all
(v,x)\in\mathfrak{V}\times\mathfrak{X}
. The map
\theta\mapsto\dot{\phi}_{\theta}(v,x)
is continuous at all
\theta\in\mathrm{Nb}(\theta_{0})
for all
(v,x)\in\mathfrak{V}\times\mathfrak{X}
. The expectation of
\dot{\phi}_{\theta_{0}}
exists, and the matrix
P_{VX}\dot{\phi}_{\theta_{0}}
is invertible. Furthermore,

\displaystyle\left\lVert{\sup_{\theta\in\mathrm{Nb}(\theta_{0})}\left\lVert{\dot{\phi}_{\theta}}\right\rVert_{1}}\right\rVert_{L_{1}(P_{VX})}<\infty,

(77)

where, for a fixed
(v,x)\in\mathfrak{V}\times\mathfrak{X}
,
\left\lVert{\dot{\phi}_{\theta}}\right\rVert_{1}(v,x)
is the sum of the absolute values of the
K^{2}
entries of
\dot{\phi}_{\theta}(v,x)
.

Let
A_{n}\in\real^{K\times K}
be an arbitrary sequence of (possibly random and then
\sigma(\mathcal{S}^{\prime})
-measurable) matrices with
A_{n}\overset{P_{VX}}{\to}A_{0}
as
n\to\infty
for a symmetric positive definite
A_{0}
. Then the solution
\hat{\theta}
to

\tilde{\theta}\mapsto\Lambda_{n}(\tilde{\theta})\coloneqq\big(\mathbb{P}_{n}^{\prime}\phi_{\tilde{\theta}}^{\intercal}\big)A_{n}\big(\mathbb{P}_{n}^{\prime}\phi_{\tilde{\theta}}\big)\equiv 0

up to
\Lambda_{n}(\hat{\theta})=o_{P_{VX}}\left(n^{-1/2}\right)
satisfies
\sqrt{n}(\hat{\theta}-\theta_{0})\overset{P_{VX}}{\rightsquigarrow}\mathcal{N}(0,\Sigma)
as
n\to\infty
, where

\displaystyle\Sigma\coloneqq({\dot{\Phi}^{\intercal}}A_{0}{\dot{\Phi}})^{-1}{\dot{\Phi}^{\intercal}}A_{0}\Phi A_{0}{\dot{\Phi}}({\dot{\Phi}^{\intercal}}A_{0}{\dot{\Phi}})^{-1},\quad\dot{\Phi}\coloneqq P_{VX}\dot{\phi}_{\theta_{0}},\quad\Phi\coloneqq P_{VX}\phi_{\theta_{0}}\phi_{\theta_{0}}^{\intercal}.

Further, let
\xi_{\theta}:\mathfrak{V}\times\mathfrak{X}\to\real
,
\theta\in\Theta
, be (possibly random and then
\sigma(\mathcal{S}^{\prime})
-measurable) functions with the derivative of
\theta\mapsto\xi_{\theta}(v,x)
at
\tilde{\theta}
,
\mathrm{D}_{\theta}\xi_{\tilde{\theta}}(v,x)
, existent at all
\tilde{\theta}\in\mathrm{Nb}(\theta_{0})
for all
(v,x)\in\mathfrak{V}\times\mathfrak{X}
. If

\displaystyle\left\lVert{\sup_{\tilde{\theta}\in\mathrm{Nb}(\theta_{0})}\left\lVert{\mathrm{D}_{\theta}\xi_{\tilde{\theta}}}\right\rVert_{2}}\right\rVert_{L_{2}(P_{VX})}=O_{{P_{VX}}}\left(1\right),

(78)

where, for a fixed
(v,x)\in\mathfrak{V}\times\mathfrak{X}
,
\left\lVert{\mathrm{D}_{\theta}\xi_{\tilde{\theta}}}\right\rVert_{2}^{2}(v,x)
is the sum of squared entries of the vector
\mathrm{D}_{\theta}\xi_{\tilde{\theta}}(v,x)
, then
\left\lVert{\xi_{\hat{\theta}}-\xi_{\theta_{0}}}\right\rVert_{L_{2}(P_{VX})}=O_{{P_{VX}}}\left(n^{-1/2}\right)
.

Proof of
Lemma

7
.

We have the identification
P_{VX}\phi_{\theta_{0}}=0_{K}
. The conditions of the lemma are versions of Assumptions 2.1–2.5 and 3.1–3.6 in
23
adapted to our setting, whereby Theorems 2.1 and 3.2
ibid
apply, giving
\sqrt{n}(\hat{\theta}-\theta_{0})\overset{P_{VX}}{\rightsquigarrow}\mathcal{N}(0,\Sigma)
(see also
33
). Then by the mean-value theorem and (
78
),

\left\lVert{\xi_{\hat{\theta}}-\xi_{\theta_{0}}}\right\rVert_{L_{2}(P_{VX})}\leq\left\lVert{\hat{\theta}-\theta_{0}}\right\rVert_{2}\left\lVert{\sup_{\tilde{\theta}\in\mathrm{Nb}(\theta_{0})}\left\lVert{\mathrm{D}_{\theta}\xi_{\tilde{\theta}}}\right\rVert_{2}}\right\rVert_{L_{2}(P_{VX})}=O_{{P_{VX}}}\left(n^{-1/2}\right).

∎

Proposition

2
is modelled after
Lemma

7
but the
\left\lVert{\cdot}\right\rVert_{L_{1}(P_{VX})}
in conditions (
76
) and (
77
) replaced with
\left\lVert{\cdot}\right\rVert_{L_{1}(P_{VZ})}
in
Proposition

2
. The restriction of
\left\lVert{\cdot}\right\rVert_{L_{1}(P_{VZ})}
-bounds in (
76
) and (
77
) instead of the
\left\lVert{\cdot}\right\rVert_{L_{1}(P_{VX})}
-bounds in the nonprivate case of
Lemma

7
appears to be unavoidable in our construction: we need to control
\left\lVert{\cdot}\right\rVert_{L_{1}(P_{VZ})}
, and while
Lemma

3

(iv)
shows that
\left\lVert{h}\right\rVert_{L_{p}(P_{VX})}\lesssim\left\lVert{h}\right\rVert_{L_{p}(P_{VZ})}
when
Q\in\mathcal{Q}_{\delta}
in (
16
), the converse
\left\lVert{h}\right\rVert_{L_{p}(P_{VZ})}\lesssim\left\lVert{h}\right\rVert_{L_{p}(P_{VX})}
may fail. One can replace
\left\lVert{\cdot}\right\rVert_{L_{p}(P_{VZ})}
with
\left\lVert{\cdot}\right\rVert_{\infty}
-bounds, which could be easier to verify. For example, in estimating
\mu_{\mathcal{X}}=\mu_{\theta_{0}}
, if
m
is bounded and
\mu_{\theta}
is a generalised linear model with continuous second derivative, then the
\left\lVert{.}\right\rVert_{\infty}
-bounds hold provided
\mathfrak{V}_{\mathit{1}}\times\mathfrak{X}
is compact.

Proof of
Proposition

2
.

Because
Q_{\mathcal{X}}^{-1}
and differentiation with respect to
\theta
commutes,

\displaystyle\bar{\phi}_{\tilde{\theta}}^{\intercal}=\mathrm{D}_{\theta}\bar{\Xi}_{\tilde{\theta}}=\mathrm{D}_{\theta}Q_{\mathcal{X}}^{-1}\Xi_{\tilde{\theta}}=Q_{\mathcal{X}}^{-1}\mathrm{D}_{\theta}\Xi_{\tilde{\theta}}=Q_{\mathcal{X}}^{-1}\phi_{\tilde{\theta}}^{\intercal},

and then also

\displaystyle\mathrm{D}_{\theta}\bar{\phi}_{\tilde{\theta}}=\mathrm{D}_{\theta}Q_{\mathcal{X}}^{-1}\phi_{\tilde{\theta}}=Q_{\mathcal{X}}^{-1}\mathrm{D}_{\theta}\phi_{\tilde{\theta}}=Q_{\mathcal{X}}^{-1}\dot{\phi}_{\tilde{\theta}}.

(Here, we denote with
Q_{\mathcal{X}}^{-1}\phi_{\tilde{\theta}}^{\intercal}
the vector and with
Q_{\mathcal{X}}^{-1}\dot{\phi}_{\tilde{\theta}}
the matrix where
Q_{\mathcal{X}}^{-1}
is applied element-wise to the coordinate functions of
\phi_{\tilde{\theta}}^{\intercal}
and
\dot{\phi}_{\tilde{\theta}}
, respectively.)

Lemma

3

(iii)
implies the identification

P_{VZ}\bar{\phi}_{\theta_{0}}^{\intercal}=P_{VX}\phi_{\theta_{0}}^{\intercal}=0_{K}
,
as
P_{VX}\phi_{\theta_{0}}^{\intercal}=0_{K}
by the identifiability conditions of
Lemma

7
; and also
P_{VZ}\mathrm{D}_{\theta}\bar{\phi}_{\tilde{\theta}}=P_{VZ}Q_{\mathcal{X}}^{-1}\dot{\phi}_{\tilde{\theta}}=P_{VX}\dot{\phi}_{\tilde{\theta}}
. By this, and the strengthening of
\left\lVert{\cdot}\right\rVert_{L_{p}(P_{VX})}
to
\left\lVert{\cdot}\right\rVert_{L_{p}(P_{VZ})}
in (
78
),(
76
), and (
77
), all assumptions in
Lemma

7
that hold under
(\mathbb{P}_{n}^{\prime},P_{VX})
continue to hold under
(\bar{\mathbb{P}}_{n}^{\prime},P_{VZ})
. Thus, the assertion follows with
\dot{\Phi}
being the same as in
Lemma

7
.
∎

D.1.2
