Regression
\mu_{\mathcal{X}}

Suppose that
\mu_{\mathcal{X}}=\mu_{\theta_{0}}
belonging to the model

\displaystyle\mathcal{M}(\Theta)\coloneqq\left\{\mu_{\theta}:\theta\in\Theta\subset\real^{K}\right\}

(79)

for a fixed
K\geq 1
.

With
Lemma

7
and
Proposition

2
, we can attain the parametric root-
n
rate for the regression in both the nonprivate and the private setting.

Corollary 2

(Regression — Rate of Nonprivate Estimator
\mu_{\hat{\theta}}
)
.

Assume that
\mu_{\mathcal{X}}=\mu_{\theta_{0}}
belonging to the model in (
79
).
For
(v,x,\tilde{\theta})\in\mathfrak{V}\times\mathfrak{X}\times\Theta
, let
\Xi_{\tilde{\theta}}(v,x)\coloneqq\Delta_{\mu_{\tilde{\theta}}}^{2}(v,x)=(m(v,x)-\mu_{\tilde{\theta}}(v_{\mathit{1}},x))^{2}
,

\displaystyle\phi_{\tilde{\theta}}(v,x)\coloneqq\mathrm{D}_{\theta}\Delta_{\mu_{\tilde{\theta}}}^{2}(v,x)^{\intercal}=2(m(v,x)-\mu_{\tilde{\theta}}(v_{\mathit{1}},x))\mathrm{D}_{\theta}\mu_{\tilde{\theta}}(v_{\mathit{1}},x)^{\intercal},\quad\xi_{\tilde{\theta}}\coloneqq\mu_{\tilde{\theta}}.

Take a sequence of (possibly random and then
\sigma(\mathcal{S}^{\prime})
-measurable) matrices
A_{n}\in\real^{K\times K}
, and
let
\hat{\theta}\in\Theta
be the solution to the estimating equation

\tilde{\theta}\mapsto\Lambda_{n}(\tilde{\theta})\coloneqq\big(\mathbb{P}_{n}^{\prime}\phi_{\tilde{\theta}}^{\intercal}\big)A_{n}\big(\mathbb{P}_{n}^{\prime}\phi_{\tilde{\theta}}\big)\equiv 0

up to
\Lambda_{n}(\hat{\theta})=o_{P_{VX}}\left(n^{-1/2}\right)
.

If
(\Xi_{\theta},\phi_{\theta},A_{n},\xi_{\theta})
satisfy the conditions of
Lemma

7
, then
\sqrt{n}(\hat{\theta}-\theta_{0})\overset{P_{VX}}{\rightsquigarrow}\mathcal{N}(0,\Sigma)
as
n\to\infty
for some
\Sigma
, and

\left\lVert{\mu_{\hat{\theta}}-\mu_{\mathcal{X}}}\right\rVert_{L_{2}(P_{VX})}=O_{{P_{VX}}}\left(n^{-1/2}\right)
.

Corollary 3

(Regression — Rate of Private Estimator
\mu_{\check{\theta}}
)
.

Assume that
P_{VZ}\in\mathcal{P}_{VZ}(Q,\mathcal{P}_{VX})
, for a fixed
Q\in\mathcal{Q}_{\psi}
and
P_{VX}\in\mathcal{P}_{VX}
subject to the parametric assumption (
79
). Let
(\Xi_{\theta},\phi_{\theta},\xi_{\theta})
,
\theta\in\Theta
, be as defined in
Corollary

2
.

Take a sequence of (possibly random and then
\sigma(\bar{\mathcal{S}}^{\prime})
-measurable) matrices
\bar{A}_{n}\in\real^{K\times K}
, and let
\check{\theta}\in\Theta
be the solution to the estimating equation

\tilde{\theta}\mapsto\bar{\Lambda}_{n}(\tilde{\theta})\coloneqq\big(\bar{\mathbb{P}}_{n}^{\prime}\bar{\phi}_{\tilde{\theta}}^{\intercal}\big)\bar{A}_{n}\big(\bar{\mathbb{P}}_{n}^{\prime}\bar{\phi}_{\tilde{\theta}}\big)\equiv 0

up to
\bar{\Lambda}_{n}(\check{\theta})=o_{P_{VZ}}\left(n^{-1/2}\right)
, where
\bar{\phi}_{\tilde{\theta}}\coloneqq\mathrm{D}_{\theta}\bar{\Xi}_{\tilde{\theta}}
with
\bar{\Xi}_{\theta}\coloneqq Q_{\mathcal{X}}^{-1}\Xi_{\theta}
for the inverse
Q_{\mathcal{X}}^{-1}
of
Q_{\mathcal{X}}
in (
18
).

If
(\Xi_{\theta},\phi_{\theta},\bar{A}_{n},\xi_{\theta})
satisfy the conditions of
Proposition

2
, then
\sqrt{n}(\check{\theta}-\theta_{0})\overset{P_{VZ}}{\rightsquigarrow}\mathcal{N}(0,\bar{\Sigma})
as
n\to\infty
for some
\bar{\Sigma}
, and
\left\lVert{\mu_{\check{\theta}}-\mu_{\mathcal{X}}}\right\rVert_{L_{2}(P_{VX})}=O_{{P_{VZ}}}\left(n^{-1/2}\right)
.

The proofs of
Corollaries

2
and
3
are omitted.

D.1.3
Riesz Representer
r

In this section, we consider the estimation of the Riesz representer
r
. We begin with an identification result, which is not limited to finite-dimensional models (see also
41
).

Lemma 8

(Identification of
r
)
.

For all
\gamma\in\Gamma
, the Riesz representer
r_{\gamma}
of

L_{2}(P_{V_{\mathit{1}}}X)\ni\mu\mapsto P_{VX}f(V,X,\mu,\gamma)
satisfies (
36
).

Proof of
Lemma

8
.

By the definition of
r_{\gamma}
,

\displaystyle P_{VX}f(V,X,h,\gamma)=P_{VX}r_{\gamma}(V_{\mathit{1}},X)h(V_{\mathit{1}},X)\text{ for all }h\in L_{2}(P_{V_{\mathit{1}}}X).

Then

\displaystyle P_{VX}\Upsilon_{\gamma,h}(V,X)=P_{VX}\left[h^{2}-2f\right]=P_{VX}\left[h^{2}-2hr_{\gamma}\right]=P_{VX}\left[(h-r_{\gamma})^{2}-r_{\gamma}^{2}\right],

whereby
\arg\min_{h\in L_{2}(P_{V_{\mathit{1}}}X)}P_{VX}\Upsilon_{\gamma,h}=\arg\min_{h\in L_{2}(P_{V_{\mathit{1}}}X)}P_{VX}\left[(h-r_{\gamma})^{2}\right]=r_{\gamma}
.
∎

Suppose that the Riesz representer
r_{\gamma}
of
\mu\mapsto P_{VX}f(V,X,\mu,\gamma)
is
r_{\gamma}=r_{\gamma,\theta_{0}}
for each
\gamma\in\Gamma

uniformly
, where
r_{\gamma,\theta_{0}}
belongs to the model

\displaystyle\mathcal{R}_{\gamma}(\Theta)\coloneqq\left\{r_{\gamma,\theta}:\theta\in\Theta\subset\real^{K}\right\},

(80)

with the map
(\gamma,\theta)\mapsto r_{\gamma,\theta}
known. A prototypical example is a parametric model for the propensity score in inferring the average treatment effect on the treated.

Example 10

(name=Average Treatment Effect on the Treated,continues=priv:ex:att_dr)
.

Recall that for
\gamma_{\mathcal{V}}(c)\coloneqq\bar{\gamma}\coloneqq\mathbb{E}D=p_{1}
, the Riesz representer for
\mathbb{E}\left[\left.Y^{0}\,\right|\,D=1\right]
is
r(d,x)=\frac{1-d}{\gamma_{\mathcal{V}}(c)}\frac{\pi_{\mathcal{X}}(1{|\,}x)}{1-\pi_{\mathcal{X}}(1{|\,}x)}
, and for
\mathbb{E}\left[\left.Y^{1}-Y^{0}\,\right|\,D=1\right]
, it is
r(d,x)=\frac{d}{\gamma_{\mathcal{V}}(c)}-\frac{1-d}{\gamma_{\mathcal{V}}(c)}\frac{\pi_{\mathcal{X}}(1{|\,}x)}{1-\pi_{\mathcal{X}}(1{|\,}x)}
. Suppose that the propensity score
\pi_{\mathcal{X}}(1{|\,}x)=\pi_{\theta_{0}}(x)
for some
\pi_{\theta_{0}}
in the presumed model
\left\{\pi_{\theta}:\theta\in\Theta\subset\real^{K}\right\}
, for instance, the logistic model
\pi_{\theta}(x)=(1+\exp(-\theta^{\intercal}x))^{-1}
. Then

\displaystyle r_{\gamma,\theta_{0}}(d,x)=\frac{1-d}{\gamma}\frac{\pi_{\theta_{0}}(x)}{1-\pi_{\theta_{0}}(x)},\quad r_{\gamma,\theta_{0}}(d,x)=\frac{d}{\gamma}-\frac{1-d}{\gamma}\frac{\pi_{\theta_{0}}(x)}{1-\pi_{\theta_{0}}(x)}.

are the Riesz representers for
\mathbb{E}\left[\left.Y^{0}\,\right|\,D=1\right]
and
\mathbb{E}\left[\left.Y^{1}-Y^{0}\,\right|\,D=1\right]
, respectively, for
\gamma=\gamma_{\mathcal{V}}(c)

=\bar{\gamma}=\mathbb{E}D
. Conversely,
r_{\gamma_{\mathcal{V}}(c),\theta}
is not a Riesz representer unless
\theta=\theta_{0}
. Let
\lambda_{0,\theta}(d,x)\coloneqq(1-d)\frac{\pi_{\theta}(x)}{1-\pi_{\theta}(x)}
and
\lambda_{1,\theta}(d,x)\coloneqq d-(1-d)\frac{\pi_{\theta}(x)}{1-\pi_{\theta}(x)}
. Then the models for the Riesz representers are

\displaystyle\mathcal{R}_{\gamma}(\Theta)=\left\{\gamma^{-1}\lambda_{0,\theta}:\theta\in\Theta\right\},\quad\mathcal{R}_{\gamma}(\Theta)=\left\{\gamma^{-1}\lambda_{1,\theta}:\theta\in\Theta\right\}

for
\mathbb{E}\left[\left.Y^{0}\,\right|\,D=1\right]
and
\mathbb{E}\left[\left.Y^{1}-Y^{0}\,\right|\,D=1\right]
, respectively.

In the parametric model (
80
), the Riesz representer
r_{\gamma,\theta_{0}}
is identified by
Lemma

8
as

\displaystyle r_{\gamma,\theta_{0}}

\displaystyle=\arg\min_{\rho\in\mathcal{R}_{\gamma}(\Theta)}P_{VX}\Upsilon_{\gamma,\rho}(V,X)

\displaystyle=\arg\min_{\rho\in\mathcal{R}_{\gamma}(\Theta)}P_{VX}\bigg[\rho(v_{\mathit{1}},x)^{2}-2f(V,X,\rho,\gamma)\bigg].

(81)

Importantly, for all
\gamma\in\Gamma
uniformly,
\theta=\theta_{0}
, and only
\theta=\theta_{0}
, gives the Riesz representer in the model
\mathcal{R}_{\gamma}(\Theta)
. An implication is that for
any

\gamma\in\Gamma
,

\displaystyle\theta_{0}=\arg\min_{\theta\in\Theta}P_{VX}\Upsilon_{\gamma,r_{\gamma,\theta}}(V,X)=\arg\min_{\theta\in\Theta}P_{VX}\bigg[r_{\gamma,\theta}(v_{\mathit{1}},x)^{2}-2f(V,X,r_{\gamma,\theta},\gamma)\bigg].

(82)

This permits us to infer
\theta_{0}
without suffering any bias from the unknown
\gamma_{\mathcal{V}}(c)
. Take an arbitrary
\gamma_{0}\in\Gamma
. In the nonprivate setting, (
82
) can directly be used to construct a method-of-moments estimator
\hat{\theta}_{\gamma_{0}}
of
\theta_{0}
, and next estimate the representer as
\hat{r}\coloneqq r_{\hat{\gamma}_{\mathcal{V}}(c),\hat{\theta}_{\gamma_{0}}}
for
\hat{\gamma}_{\mathcal{V}}(c)
of (
108
). In the private setting,
Proposition

2
can be used to construct a private estimator
\check{\theta}_{\gamma_{0}}
of
\theta_{0}
, and then estimate the representer as
\check{r}\coloneqq r_{\check{\gamma}_{\mathcal{V}}(c),\check{\theta}_{\gamma_{0}}}
for
\check{\gamma}_{\mathcal{V}}(c)
of (
28
). If
(\gamma,\theta)\mapsto r_{\gamma,\theta}
is smooth enough, then analogues to
Corollaries

2
and
3
hold, giving root-
n
rates.

Corollary 4

(Riesz Representer — Rate of Nonprivate Estimator
r_{\hat{\gamma}_{\mathcal{V}}(c),\hat{\theta}_{\gamma_{0}}}
)
.

Suppose that
r_{\gamma}=r_{\gamma,\theta_{0}}
for the model
\mathcal{R}_{\gamma}(\Theta)
of (
80
). Assume that

(i)

The map
(\gamma,\theta)\mapsto r_{\gamma,\theta}(v_{\mathit{1}},x)
is differentiable at all
(\gamma,\theta)\in\mathrm{Nb}(\gamma_{\mathcal{V}}(c))\times\mathrm{Nb}(\theta_{0})
, where the
\mathrm{Nb}(w)
are neighbourhoods of
w
, for all
(v_{\mathit{1}},x)\in\mathfrak{V}_{\mathit{1}}\times\mathfrak{X}
with partial derivatives
\partial_{\gamma}r_{\tilde{\gamma},\tilde{\theta}}(v_{\mathit{1}},x)
with respect to
\gamma
, and
\mathrm{D}_{\theta}r_{\tilde{\gamma},\tilde{\theta}}(v_{\mathit{1}},x)
with respect to
\theta
, at
(\tilde{\gamma},\tilde{\theta})\in\mathrm{Nb}(\gamma_{\mathcal{V}}(c))\times\mathrm{Nb}(\theta_{0})
.

(ii)

The map
\theta\mapsto\Upsilon_{\gamma,r_{\gamma,\theta}}(v,x)=r_{\gamma,\theta}(v_{\mathit{1}},x)^{2}-2f(v,x,r_{\gamma,\theta},\gamma)
is differentiable at all
\theta\in\mathrm{Nb}(\theta_{0})
for all
(v,x,\gamma)\in\mathfrak{V}\times\mathfrak{X}\times\Gamma
with
K×1
-valued derivative
\mathrm{D}_{\theta}\Upsilon_{\gamma,r_{\gamma,\tilde{\theta}}}(v,x)^{\intercal}\eqqcolon\phi_{\gamma,\tilde{\theta}}(v,x)
at
\tilde{\theta}\in\mathrm{Nb}(\theta_{0})
.

Fix an arbitrary
\gamma_{0}\in\Gamma
, and take a sequence of (possibly random and then
\sigma(\mathcal{S}^{\prime})
-measurable) matrices
A_{n}\in\real^{K\times K}
. Let
\hat{\theta}_{\gamma_{0}}\in\Theta
be the solution to the estimating equation

\tilde{\theta}\mapsto\Lambda_{n}(\tilde{\theta})\coloneqq\big(\mathbb{P}_{n}^{\prime}\phi_{\gamma_{0},\tilde{\theta}}^{\intercal}\big)A_{n}\big(\mathbb{P}_{n}^{\prime}\phi_{\gamma_{0},\tilde{\theta}}\big)\equiv 0

up to
\Lambda_{n}(\hat{\theta}_{\gamma_{0}})=o_{P_{VX}}\left(n^{-1/2}\right)
. If
\Xi_{\theta}\coloneqq\Upsilon_{\gamma_{0},r_{\gamma_{0},\theta}}
,
\phi_{\theta}\coloneqq\phi_{\gamma_{0},\theta}
, and
A_{n}
satisfy the conditions of
Lemma

7
pertaining to
(\Xi_{\theta},\phi_{\theta},A_{n})
therein, then
\sqrt{n}(\hat{\theta}_{\gamma_{0}}-\theta_{0})\overset{P_{VX}}{\rightsquigarrow}\mathcal{N}(0,\Sigma_{\gamma_{0}})
as
n\to\infty
for some
\Sigma_{\gamma_{0}}
. If, in addition,

\displaystyle\left\lVert{\partial_{\gamma}r_{\tilde{\gamma}_{\mathcal{V}}(c),\tilde{\theta}_{\gamma_{0}}}}\right\rVert_{L_{2}(P_{VX})}

\displaystyle=O_{{P_{VX}}}\left(1\right),

(83)

\displaystyle\left\lVert{\left\lVert{\mathrm{D}_{\theta}r_{\tilde{\gamma}_{\mathcal{V}}(c),\tilde{\theta}_{\gamma_{0}}}}\right\rVert_{2}}\right\rVert_{L_{2}(P_{VX})}

\displaystyle=O_{{P_{VX}}}\left(1\right)

(84)

for some
(\tilde{\gamma}_{\mathcal{V}}(c),\tilde{\theta}_{\gamma_{0}})
between
(\gamma_{\mathcal{V}}(c),\theta_{0})
and
(\hat{\gamma}_{\mathcal{V}}(c),\hat{\theta}_{\gamma_{0}})
,
then

\left\lVert{\hat{r}-r}\right\rVert_{L_{2}(P_{VX})}=O_{{P_{VX}}}\left(n^{-1/2}\right)
,
where
\hat{r}\coloneqq r_{\hat{\gamma}_{\mathcal{V}}(c),\hat{\theta}_{\gamma_{0}}}
,
r=r_{\gamma_{\mathcal{V}}(c),\theta_{0}}
for the model in (
80
) and
\hat{\gamma}_{\mathcal{V}}(c)
in (
108
).

Proof of
Corollary

4
.

The asymptotic normality of
\hat{\theta}_{\gamma_{0}}
follows directly from
Lemma

7
via the identification (
82
), whereby
P_{VX}\phi_{\gamma,\theta_{0}}=0_{K}
for any fixed
\gamma\in\Gamma
. A mean-value expansion of
r_{\hat{\gamma}_{\mathcal{V}}(c),\hat{\theta}_{\gamma_{0}}}
via
(i)
in combination with (
83
) and (
84
) yields the second assertion as
\hat{\gamma}_{\mathcal{V}}(c)-\gamma_{\mathcal{V}}(c)=O_{{P_{VX}}}\left(n^{-1/2}\right)
, and
\hat{\theta}_{\gamma_{0}}-\theta_{0}=O_{{P_{VX}}}\left(n^{-1/2}\right)
by the first assertion.
∎

Corollary 5

(Riesz Representer — Rate of Private Estimator
r_{\check{\gamma}_{\mathcal{V}}(c),\check{\theta}}
)
.

Assume that
P_{VZ}\in\mathcal{P}_{VZ}(Q,\mathcal{P}_{VX})
, for a fixed
Q\in\mathcal{Q}_{\psi}
and
P_{VX}\in\mathcal{P}_{VX}
subject to the parametric assumption of (
80
). Suppose that
(i)
and
(ii)
of
Corollary

4
hold, and let
\Upsilon_{\gamma,r_{\gamma,\theta}},\phi_{\gamma,\theta}
,
(\gamma,\theta)\in\Gamma\times\Theta
, be as they are defined therein.

Fix an arbitrary
\gamma_{0}\in\Gamma
, and take a (possibly random and then
\sigma(\bar{\mathcal{S}}^{\prime})
-measurable) sequence of matrices
\bar{A}_{n}\in\real^{K\times K}
. Let
\check{\theta}_{\gamma_{0}}\in\Theta
be the solution to the estimating equation
\tilde{\theta}\mapsto\bar{\Lambda}_{n}(\tilde{\theta})\coloneqq\big(\bar{\mathbb{P}}_{n}^{\prime}\bar{\phi}_{\gamma_{0},\tilde{\theta}}^{\intercal}\big)\bar{A}_{n}\big(\bar{\mathbb{P}}_{n}^{\prime}\bar{\phi}_{\gamma_{0},\tilde{\theta}}\big)\equiv 0
,
up to
\bar{\Lambda}_{n}(\check{\theta}_{\gamma_{0}})=o_{P_{VZ}}\left(n^{-1/2}\right)
, with
\bar{\phi}_{\gamma,\tilde{\theta}}\coloneqq\mathrm{D}_{\theta}\bar{\Upsilon}_{\gamma,r_{\gamma,\tilde{\theta}}}
,
\bar{\Upsilon}_{\gamma,r_{\gamma,\tilde{\theta}}}\coloneqq Q_{\mathcal{X}}^{-1}\Upsilon_{\gamma,r_{\gamma,\tilde{\theta}}}
, for the inverse
Q_{\mathcal{X}}^{-1}
of
Q_{\mathcal{X}}
in (
18
).

If
\Xi_{\theta}\coloneqq\Upsilon_{\gamma_{0},r_{\gamma_{0},\theta}}
,
\phi_{\theta}\coloneqq\phi_{\gamma_{0},\theta}
, and
\bar{A}_{n}
satisfy the conditions of
Proposition

2
pertaining to
(\Xi_{\theta},\phi_{\theta},\bar{A}_{n})
therein, then
\sqrt{n}(\check{\theta}_{\gamma_{0}}-\theta_{0})\overset{P_{VZ}}{\rightsquigarrow}\mathcal{N}(0,\bar{\Sigma}_{\gamma_{0}})
as
n\to\infty
for some
\bar{\Sigma}_{\gamma_{0}}
. If, in addition,
\left\lVert{\partial_{\gamma}r_{\tilde{\gamma}_{\mathcal{V}}(c),\tilde{\theta}_{\gamma_{0}}}}\right\rVert_{L_{2}(P_{VX})}=O_{{P_{VZ}}}\left(1\right)
and
\left\lVert{\left\lVert{\mathrm{D}_{\theta}r_{\tilde{\gamma}_{\mathcal{V}}(c),\tilde{\theta}_{\gamma_{0}}}}\right\rVert_{2}}\right\rVert_{L_{2}(P_{VX})}=O_{{P_{VZ}}}\left(1\right)
for some
(\tilde{\gamma}_{\mathcal{V}}(c),\tilde{\theta}_{\gamma_{0}})
between
(\gamma_{\mathcal{V}}(c),\theta_{0})
and
(\check{\gamma}_{\mathcal{V}}(c),\check{\theta}_{\gamma_{0}})
, then

\left\lVert{\check{r}-r}\right\rVert_{L_{2}(P_{VX})}=O_{{P_{VZ}}}\left(n^{-1/2}\right)
,
where
\check{r}\coloneqq r_{\check{\gamma}_{\mathcal{V}}(c),\check{\theta}_{\gamma_{0}}}
,
r=r_{\gamma_{\mathcal{V}}(c),\theta_{0}}
for the model in (
80
) and
\check{\gamma}_{\mathcal{V}}(c)
in (
28
).

Proof of
Corollary

5
.

Follows from
Proposition

2
as
Corollary

4
follows from
Lemma

7
.
∎

D.2.  Infinite-Dimensional Models

Proof of
Proposition

3
.

Write, for
T_{n}
in (
39
),

\displaystyle\check{\theta}(v_{\mathit{1}},x)-\theta(v_{\mathit{1}},x)=

\displaystyle\,T_{n}(v_{\mathit{1}},x)+M_{n}(v_{\mathit{1}},x)+B_{n}(v_{\mathit{1}},x),

\displaystyle M_{n}(v_{\mathit{1}},x)\coloneqq

\displaystyle\,\frac{1}{n}\sum_{i\in[n]}\left\{\bar{w}_{n,i}(v_{\mathit{1}},x,V_{i}^{\prime},Z_{i}^{\prime},\vartheta)-P_{VZ}[\bar{w}_{n,i}(v_{\mathit{1}},x,V,Z,\vartheta)]\right\},

\displaystyle B_{n}(v_{\mathit{1}},x)\coloneqq

\displaystyle\,\frac{1}{n}\sum_{i\in[n]}P_{VZ}[\bar{w}_{n,i}(v_{\mathit{1}},x,V,Z,\vartheta)]-\theta(v_{\mathit{1}},x),

for
(v_{\mathit{1}},x)\in\mathfrak{V}_{\mathit{1}}\times\mathfrak{X}
, so that

\displaystyle P_{VX}(\check{\theta}-\theta)^{2}\leq 2\left\{P_{VX}T_{n}^{2}+2(P_{VX}M_{n}^{2}+P_{VX}B_{n}^{2})\right\},

(85)

since
(x+y)^{2}\leq 2(x^{2}+y^{2})
for all
x,y\in\real
. For
\bar{w}_{ni}
in (
38
), we have by
Lemma

3

(iii)
that
P_{VZ}[\bar{w}_{n,i}(v_{\mathit{1}},x,V,Z,\vartheta)]=P_{VX}[w_{n,i}(v_{\mathit{1}},x,V,X,\vartheta)]
. Then, in the light of (
39
), the bound (
40
) follows once we show

\displaystyle P_{VX}M_{n}^{2}=O_{{P_{VZ}}}\left(\frac{1}{n^{2}}\sum_{i\in[n]}\int\Var\left[\bar{w}_{n,i}(v_{\mathit{1}},x,V,Z,\vartheta)\right]\,\mathrm{d}P_{V_{\mathit{1}}X}(v_{\mathit{1}},x)\right).

(86)

By Markov’s inequality, for all
K>0
,

\displaystyle\mathbb{P}\left(P_{VX}M_{n}^{2}>K\right)\leq\frac{1}{K}\mathbb{E}\left[\int M_{n}(v_{\mathit{1}},x)^{2}\,\mathrm{d}P_{V_{\mathit{1}}X}(v_{\mathit{1}},x)\right]

\displaystyle=\frac{1}{K}\int\mathbb{E}\left[M_{n}(v_{\mathit{1}},x)^{2}\right]\,\mathrm{d}P_{V_{\mathit{1}}X}(v_{\mathit{1}},x).

For any fixed
(v_{\mathit{1}},x)
,
\mathbb{E}\left[M_{n}(v_{\mathit{1}},x)^{2}\right]=\frac{1}{n^{2}}\sum_{i\in[n]}\Var\left[\bar{w}_{n,i}(v_{\mathit{1}},x,V,Z,\vartheta)\right]
, because the
(V_{i}^{\prime},Z_{i})
are i.i.d.. Hence (
86
) holds.

Bound (
41
). If
Q\in\mathcal{Q}_{\delta}
, then, by
Lemma

3
, we have for
h\in L_{2}(P_{VZ})
,

\displaystyle\Var\left[Q_{\mathcal{X}}^{-1}h(V,Z)\right]\leq 2\left\{\frac{1}{\alpha^{2}}\Var\left[h(V,Z)\right]+\left(\frac{1-\alpha}{\alpha}\right)^{2}\Var\left[\int h(V,z)\bar{Q}(\mathrm{d}z)\right]\right\},

since for random variables
X,Y
, we have
\Var\left[X+Y\right]\leq 2(\Var\left[X\right]+\Var\left[Y\right])
. Clearly,
\Var\left[h(V,Z)\right]\leq P_{VZ}h^{2}
, and for the same reason,
\Var\left[\int h(V,z)\bar{Q}(\mathrm{d}z)\right]\leq(P_{V}\otimes\bar{Q})h^{2}
by Jensen’s inequality. But
P_{V}\otimes\bar{Q}\leq\frac{1}{1-\alpha}P_{VZ}
by
Lemma

4
, so (
41
) holds.
∎

In the following, we apply
Proposition

3
to various estimators. The regression
\mu_{\mathcal{X}}
is estimated by kernel and orthogonal series estimators in
Examples

11
and
12
, respectively. The Riesz representer
r
is estimated by orthogonal series in
Example

13
.

Example 11

(name=Regression
\mu_{\mathcal{X}}
— Nadaraya–Watson Kernel Estimator)
.

Suppose that
\mathfrak{V}_{\mathit{1}}\times\mathfrak{X}\subset\real^{d_{\mathit{1}}}\times\real^{d_{\mathcal{X}}}
for fixed positive integers
d_{\mathit{1}},d_{\mathcal{X}}
and that the mechanism
Q\in\mathcal{Q}_{\delta}
in (
16
). To estimate
\mu_{\mathcal{X}}
, consider the nonprivate estimator

\displaystyle{\hat{\mu}_{\mathcal{X}}}(v_{\mathit{1}},x)\coloneqq\frac{1}{n}\sum_{i\in[n]}

\displaystyle\frac{K_{h}(v_{\mathit{1}},x,V_{\mathit{1}i}^{\prime},X_{i}^{\prime})m(V_{i}^{\prime},X_{i}^{\prime})}{\frac{1}{n}\sum_{j\in[n]}K_{h}(v_{\mathit{1}},x,V_{\mathit{1}j^{\prime}},X_{j}^{\prime})},

\displaystyle K_{h}(v_{\mathit{1}},x,V_{\mathit{1}i},X_{i})

\displaystyle\coloneqq K\left(\frac{1}{h}\left((v_{\mathit{1}},x)-(V_{\mathit{1}i},X_{i})\right)\right)

for a bandwidth
h>0
, and a kernel
K:\real^{d}\to\real
with
d\coloneqq d_{\mathit{1}}+d_{\mathcal{X}}
. For the density estimator
\hat{p}_{V_{\mathit{1}}X}(v_{\mathit{1}},x)\coloneqq\frac{1}{nh^{d}}\sum_{i\in[n]}K_{h}(v_{\mathit{1}},x,V_{\mathit{1}i}^{\prime},X_{i}^{\prime})
,
{\hat{\mu}_{\mathcal{X}}}
is of the form (
37
) for
w_{n,i}(v_{\mathit{1}},x,V^{\prime},X^{\prime},\vartheta)=w_{n,i}(v_{\mathit{1}},x,V^{\prime},X^{\prime},p_{V_{\mathit{1}}X})\coloneqq\frac{K_{h}(v_{\mathit{1}},x,V_{\mathit{1}}^{\prime},X^{\prime})m(V^{\prime},X^{\prime})}{h^{d}p_{V_{\mathit{1}}X}(v_{\mathit{1}},x)}
.

The private estimator
{\check{\mu}_{\mathcal{X}}}
is defined as in (
38
), with
\check{p}_{V_{\mathit{1}}X}
an arbitrary estimator of
p_{V_{\mathit{1}}X}
constructed from
\bar{\mathcal{S}}^{\prime}=((V_{i}^{\prime},Z_{i}^{\prime}))_{i\in[n]}
. For instance, if
\bar{Q}
of
Q
in (
16
) admits a density
\bar{q}
with respect to the same dominating measure as
P_{X}
does, then
Lemma

4

(iii)
motivates the choice

\displaystyle\check{p}_{V_{\mathit{1}}X}(v_{\mathit{1}},x)\coloneqq\frac{1}{\alpha}\check{p}_{V_{\mathit{1}}Z}(v_{\mathit{1}},x)-\frac{1-\alpha}{\alpha}\check{p}_{V_{\mathit{1}}}(v)\bar{q}(x),

(87)

where
(\check{p}_{V_{\mathit{1}}Z},\check{p}_{V_{\mathit{1}}})
is an arbitrary estimator of

(p_{V_{\mathit{1}}Z},p_{V_{\mathit{1}}})
constructed from
\bar{\mathcal{S}}^{\prime}
; for example

\displaystyle\begin{aligned} \check{p}_{V_{\mathit{1}}Z}(v_{\mathit{1}},z)\coloneqq\frac{1}{nh^{d}}\sum_{i\in[n]}K_{h}(v_{\mathit{1}},x,V_{\mathit{1}i}^{\prime},Z_{i}^{\prime}),\\
\check{p}_{V_{\mathit{1}}}(v_{\mathit{1}})\coloneqq\frac{1}{nh_{\mathit{1}}^{d_{\mathit{1}}}}\sum_{i\in[n]}K_{\mathit{1},h_{\mathit{1}}}(v_{\mathit{1}},V_{\mathit{1}i}^{\prime}),\end{aligned}

(88)

where
K_{\mathit{1},h}(v_{\mathit{1}},V_{\mathit{1}})\coloneqq K_{\mathit{1}}((v_{\mathit{1}}-V_{\mathit{1}})/h_{\mathit{1}})
for a kernel
K_{\mathit{1}}:\real^{d_{\mathit{1}}}\to\real
and bandwidth
h_{\mathit{1}}>0
. Next, we study
{\check{\mu}_{\mathcal{X}}}
in relation to
Proposition

3
.

Term
T_{n}
in (
39
). Let
g_{h}(v_{\mathit{1}},x,v^{\prime},x^{\prime})\coloneqq K_{h}(v_{\mathit{1}},x,v_{\mathit{1}}^{\prime},x^{\prime})m(v^{\prime},x^{\prime})
and
\bar{g}_{h}(v_{\mathit{1}},x,v^{\prime},z^{\prime})\coloneqq(Q_{\mathcal{X}}^{-1}(v^{\prime},x^{\prime})\mapsto g_{h}(v_{\mathit{1}},x,v^{\prime},x^{\prime}))(v^{\prime},z^{\prime})
. Then

\displaystyle T_{n}(v_{\mathit{1}},x)=\frac{p_{V_{\mathit{1}}X}(v_{\mathit{1}},x)-\check{p}_{V_{\mathit{1}}X}(v_{\mathit{1}},x)}{p_{V_{\mathit{1}}X}(v_{\mathit{1}},x)}\left(\frac{1}{\check{p}_{V_{\mathit{1}}X}(v_{\mathit{1}},x)}\frac{1}{nh^{d}}\sum_{i\in[n]}\bar{g}_{h}(v_{\mathit{1}},x,V_{i}^{\prime},Z_{i}^{\prime})\right).

Assume that the
(v_{\mathit{1}},x)
-supremum of the bracketed factor is
O_{{P_{VZ}}}\left(1\right)
independent of
h
, and
\left\lVert{\frac{1}{p_{V_{\mathit{1}}X}}}\right\rVert_{\infty}<\infty
. Then
P_{VX}T_{n}^{2}
is dominated by
P_{VX}(p_{V_{\mathit{1}}X}-\check{p}_{V_{\mathit{1}}X})^{2}
. Suppose that
\check{p}_{V_{\mathit{1}}X}(v_{\mathit{1}},x)
is defined as (
87
) and (
88
), and that
p_{V_{\mathit{1}}Z}(v_{\mathit{1}},z)=\alpha p_{V_{\mathit{1}}X}(v_{\mathit{1}},z)+(1-\alpha)p_{V_{\mathit{1}}}(v_{\mathit{1}})\bar{q}(z)
has
\beta
continuous derivatives which are uniformly bounded. Further suppose that the kernel
K
is chosen suitably, so that
K
is of order
\beta
, satisfying
\int K(u)\,\mathrm{d}u=1
,
\int u^{j}K(u)\,\mathrm{d}u=0
if
|j|<\beta
and
\int u^{j}K(u)\,\mathrm{d}u\neq 0
if
|j|=\beta
, where
u^{j}
is the multi-index notation
u^{j}\coloneqq u_{1}^{j_{1}}u_{2}^{j_{1}}\cdots u_{d}^{j_{d}}
for nonnegative integers
j_{1},j_{2},\ldots,j_{d}
with
|j|\coloneqq\sum_{k=1}^{d}j_{k}
. Then we expect
P_{VX}(p_{V_{\mathit{1}}Z}-\check{p}_{V_{\mathit{1}}Z})^{2}=O_{{P_{VZ}}}\left(\frac{1}{nh^{d}}+h^{2\beta}\right)
, and, consequently,

P_{VX}(p_{V_{\mathit{1}}X}-\check{p}_{V_{\mathit{1}}X})^{2}=O_{{P_{VZ}}}\left(\frac{1}{\alpha^{2}}\left(\frac{1}{nh^{d}}+h^{2\beta}\right)\right)

under suitable kernel
K_{\mathit{1}}
and bandwidth
h_{\mathit{1}}
, because the error of
\check{p}_{V_{\mathit{1}}X}
is dominated by the error of
\check{p}_{V_{\mathit{1}}Z}
as opposed to that of
\check{p}_{V_{\mathit{1}}}
, since, clearly,
V_{\mathit{1}}
is lower dimensional than
(V_{\mathit{1}},Z)
and
\bar{q}
is known.

Turning to the variance term in (
40
),
\Var\left[\bar{w}_{n,i}(v_{\mathit{1}},x,V,Z)\right]=\frac{1}{h^{2d}p_{V_{\mathit{1}}X}^{2}(v_{\mathit{1}},x)}\Var\left[\bar{g}_{h}(v_{\mathit{1}},x,V,Z)\right]
. By (
41
),

\displaystyle(\alpha^{2}/4)\Var\left[\bar{g}_{h}(v_{\mathit{1}},x,V,Z)\right]\leq\int\mathbb{E}\left[K_{h}^{2}(v_{\mathit{1}},x,V_{\mathit{1}},Z)m^{2}(V,Z)\right]\,\mathrm{d}P_{V_{\mathit{1}}X}(v_{\mathit{1}},x)

\displaystyle=\mathbb{E}\left[m^{2}(V,Z)\int{K_{h}^{2}(v_{\mathit{1}},x,V_{\mathit{1}},Z)}\,\mathrm{d}P_{V_{\mathit{1}}X}(v_{\mathit{1}},x)\right]=h^{d}\mathbb{E}m^{2}(V,Z)K^{2}(V_{\mathit{1}},Z),

where the last step is by a change of variables and using that
p_{V_{\mathit{1}}X}
integrates to one.
Hence, if
\left\lVert{\frac{1}{p_{V_{\mathit{1}}X}}}\right\rVert_{\infty}<\infty
and
P_{VZ}(mK)^{2}<\infty
, then
\frac{1}{n^{2}}\sum_{i\in[n]}\int\bar{\sigma}_{i}^{2}(v_{\mathit{1}},x)\,\mathrm{d}P_{V_{\mathit{1}}X}(v_{\mathit{1}},x)\lesssim\frac{1}{\alpha^{2}nh^{d}}
.

The bias term in (
40
) satisfies

\displaystyle\frac{1}{n}\sum_{i\in[n]}P_{VX}[w_{n,i}(v_{\mathit{1}},x,V,X,\vartheta)]-\theta(v_{\mathit{1}},x)=\frac{P_{VX}K_{h}(v_{\mathit{1}},x,V_{\mathit{1}},X)m(V,X)}{h^{d}p_{V_{\mathit{1}}X}(v_{\mathit{1}},x)}-\mu_{\mathcal{X}}(v_{\mathit{1}},x)

\displaystyle=\frac{P_{VX}K_{h}(v_{\mathit{1}},x,V_{\mathit{1}},X)\mu_{\mathcal{X}}(V_{\mathit{1}},X)-h^{d}p_{V_{\mathit{1}}X}(v_{\mathit{1}},x)\mu_{\mathcal{X}}(v_{\mathit{1}},x)}{h^{d}p_{V_{\mathit{1}}X}(v_{\mathit{1}},x)},

by the tower property of expectations and the definition of
\mu_{\mathcal{X}}
. Let
g(w)\coloneqq p_{V_{\mathit{1}}X}(w)\mu_{\mathcal{X}}(w)
for
w\coloneqq(v_{\mathit{1}},x)
. If
g
has
\beta^{\prime}
continuous derivatives which are uniformly bounded, then a change-of-variables and a Taylor-expansion argument show that, if
\left\lVert{\frac{1}{p_{V_{\mathit{1}}X}}}\right\rVert_{\infty}<\infty
, then the
P_{VX}
-integrated square of the last display is of the order
h^{2\beta^{\prime}}
. Then the bias term in (
40
) is
O_{{P_{VZ}}}\left(h^{2\beta^{\prime}}\right)
.

Conclude that

\displaystyle P_{VX}({\check{\mu}_{\mathcal{X}}}-\mu_{\mathcal{X}})^{2}=O_{{P_{VZ}}}\left(\frac{1}{\alpha^{2}}\left(\frac{1}{nh^{d}}+h^{2\beta}\right)\right)+O_{{P_{VZ}}}\left(\frac{1}{\alpha^{2}nh^{d}}\right)+O_{{P_{VZ}}}\left(h^{2\beta^{\prime}}\right),

which, apart from the
\alpha^{-2}
factor deriving from privacy, is the usual nonprivate error rate of kernel estimators for
\beta=\beta^{\prime}
. The privacy mechanism does introduce a bias through the estimation of the secondary-nuisance parameter
p_{V_{\mathit{1}}X}
because of the
\alpha^{-1}
factors in (
87
).

Example 12

(name=Regression
\mu_{\mathcal{X}}
— Orthogonal Series Estimator)
.

Let
(\varphi_{j})
,
j=1,2,\ldots
, be an orthonormal basis in
L_{2}(P_{V_{\mathit{1}}X})
, that is,
P_{V_{\mathit{1}}X}(\varphi_{j}\varphi_{k})=\mathbbm{1}_{k=j}
for all
j,k\geq 1
and
\mu=\sum_{j=1}^{\infty}c_{j}\varphi_{j}
for projection coefficients
c_{j}\coloneqq P_{V_{\mathit{1}}X}(\mu\varphi_{j})
for all
\mu\in L_{2}(P_{V_{\mathit{1}}X})
. If
(\varphi_{j})
were known, one could estimate
\mu_{\mathcal{X}}
in the nonprivate setting by
\sum_{j=1}^{J}\hat{c}_{j}\varphi_{j}
with
\hat{c}_{j}\coloneqq\frac{1}{n}\sum_{i\in[n]}m(V_{i}^{\prime},X_{i}^{\prime})\varphi_{j}(V_{\mathit{1}i}^{\prime},X_{i}^{\prime})
and a positive integer
J=J_{n}
tending to infinity. However, we cannot construct a basis
(\varphi_{j})
in
L_{2}(P_{V_{\mathit{1}}X})
, because
P_{V_{\mathit{1}}X}
itself is unknown. A remedy to this is to assume that
\mu_{\mathcal{X}}
is in the smaller space
L_{2}(\nu_{V_{\mathit{1}}X})\subseteq L_{2}(P_{V_{\mathit{1}}X})
, where
\nu_{V_{\mathit{1}}X}
is the dominating measure of
P_{V_{\mathit{1}}X}
. For instance, with
(V_{\mathit{1}},X)
taking values in a subspace of
d
and
\nu_{V_{\mathit{1}}X}
the Lebesgue measure, this assumption necessitates that
|\mu_{\mathcal{X}}(v_{\mathit{1}},x)|
decay to zero as
v_{\mathit{1}}
or
x
tends to infinity. Under this assumption, we can write
\mu_{\mathcal{X}}=\sum_{j=1}^{\infty}a_{j}\phi_{j}
for
a_{j}\coloneqq\nu_{V_{\mathit{1}}X}(\mu\phi_{j})
and an orthonormal basis
(\phi_{j})
in
L_{2}(\nu_{V_{\mathit{1}}X})
. Correspondingly, if
{\mathcal{S}}^{\prime}=((V_{i}^{\prime},X_{i}^{\prime}))_{i\in[n]}
were observed, we could estimate
\mu_{\mathcal{X}}
by

\displaystyle{\hat{\mu}_{\mathcal{X}}}(v_{\mathit{1}},x)\coloneqq\sum_{j=1}^{J}\hat{a}_{j}\phi_{j}(v_{\mathit{1}},x),\quad\hat{a}_{j}\coloneqq\frac{1}{n}\sum_{i\in[n]}\frac{m(V_{i}^{\prime},X_{i}^{\prime})\phi_{j}(V_{\mathit{1}i}^{\prime},X_{i}^{\prime})}{\hat{p}_{V_{\mathit{1}}X}(V_{\mathit{1}i}^{\prime},X_{i}^{\prime})},

(89)

for
J=J_{n}
tending to infinity with
n
. We inversely weight with the estimated density
\hat{p}_{V_{\mathit{1}}X}
to correct for having the basis in
L_{2}(\nu_{V_{\mathit{1}}X})
but using the empirical version of
P_{V_{\mathit{1}}X}
to compute the projection coefficients, since
P_{V_{\mathit{1}}X}\left(\frac{\mu\phi_{j}}{p_{V_{\mathit{1}}X}}\right)=\nu_{V_{\mathit{1}}X}(\mu\phi_{j})=a_{j}
. We can rewrite
{\hat{\mu}_{\mathcal{X}}}
in the form (
37
) as

\displaystyle{\hat{\mu}_{\mathcal{X}}}(v_{\mathit{1}},x)

\displaystyle=\frac{1}{n}\sum_{i\in[n]}w_{n,i}(v_{\mathit{1}},x,V_{i}^{\prime},X_{i}^{\prime},\hat{p}_{V_{\mathit{1}}X}),

\displaystyle w_{n,i}(v_{\mathit{1}},x,v^{\prime},x^{\prime},p_{V_{\mathit{1}}X})

\displaystyle\coloneqq\sum_{j=1}^{J}\frac{m(v^{\prime},x^{\prime})\phi_{j}(v_{\mathit{1}}^{\prime},x^{\prime})}{p_{V_{\mathit{1}}X}(v_{\mathit{1}}^{\prime},x^{\prime})}\phi_{j}(v_{\mathit{1}},x),

and modify it according to (
38
) for private estimation from
\bar{\mathcal{S}}^{\prime}=((V_{i}^{\prime},Z_{i}^{\prime}))_{i\in[n]}
as

\displaystyle{\check{\mu}_{\mathcal{X}}}(v_{\mathit{1}},x)

\displaystyle\coloneqq\frac{1}{n}\sum_{i\in[n]}\bar{w}_{n,i}(v_{\mathit{1}},x,V_{i}^{\prime},Z_{i}^{\prime},\check{p}_{V_{\mathit{1}}X}),

\displaystyle\bar{w}_{n,i}(v_{\mathit{1}},x,v^{\prime},z^{\prime},\check{p}_{V_{\mathit{1}}X})

\displaystyle=\left(Q_{\mathcal{X}}^{-1}(v^{\prime},x^{\prime})\mapsto\sum_{j=1}^{J}\frac{m(v^{\prime},x^{\prime})\phi_{j}(v_{\mathit{1}}^{\prime},x^{\prime})}{\check{p}_{V_{\mathit{1}}X}(v_{\mathit{1}}^{\prime},x^{\prime})}\phi_{j}(v_{\mathit{1}},x)\right)(v^{\prime},z^{\prime})

\displaystyle=\sum_{j=1}^{J}\left(Q_{\mathcal{X}}^{-1}(v^{\prime},x^{\prime})\mapsto\frac{m(v^{\prime},x^{\prime})\phi_{j}(v_{\mathit{1}}^{\prime},x^{\prime})}{\check{p}_{V_{\mathit{1}}X}(v_{\mathit{1}}^{\prime},x^{\prime})}\right)(v^{\prime},z^{\prime})\phi_{j}(v_{\mathit{1}},x),

\displaystyle\eqqcolon\sum_{j=1}^{J}(Q_{\mathcal{X}}^{-1}\check{\varrho}_{j})(v^{\prime},z^{\prime})\phi_{j}(v_{\mathit{1}},x),

by the linearity of
Q_{\mathcal{X}}^{-1}
, where
\check{p}_{V_{\mathit{1}}X}
is an arbitrary estimator of
p_{V_{\mathit{1}}X}
computed from
\bar{\mathcal{S}}^{\prime}
. Next, we study
{\check{\mu}_{\mathcal{X}}}
in relation to
Proposition

3
, assuming that
Q\in\mathcal{Q}_{\delta}
.

The term
T_{n}
in (
39
) is

\displaystyle T_{n}(v_{\mathit{1}},x)

\displaystyle=\frac{1}{n}\sum_{i\in[n]}\bar{w}_{n,i}(v_{\mathit{1}},x,V_{i}^{\prime},Z_{i}^{\prime},\check{p}_{V_{\mathit{1}}X})-\bar{w}_{n,i}(v_{\mathit{1}},x,V_{i}^{\prime},Z_{i}^{\prime},p_{V_{\mathit{1}}X})

\displaystyle=\sum_{j=1}^{J}\left(\frac{1}{n}\sum_{i\in[n]}(Q_{\mathcal{X}}^{-1}(\check{\varrho}_{j}-\varrho_{j}))(V_{i}^{\prime},Z_{i}^{\prime})\right)\phi_{j}(v_{\mathit{1}},x)\eqqcolon\sum_{j=1}^{J}\Delta_{n,j}\phi_{j}(v_{\mathit{1}},x),

where
\varrho_{j}(v^{\prime},x^{\prime})\coloneqq\frac{m(v^{\prime},x^{\prime})\phi_{j}(v_{\mathit{1}}^{\prime},x^{\prime})}{p_{V_{\mathit{1}}X}(v_{\mathit{1}}^{\prime},x^{\prime})}
. The
\Delta_{n,j}
do not depend on
(v_{\mathit{1}},x)
. Because
T_{n}^{2}
is nonnegative,

\displaystyle P_{VX}T_{n}^{2}\leq\left\lVert{p_{V_{\mathit{1}}X}}\right\rVert_{\infty}\sum_{j=1}^{J}\sum_{k=1}^{J}\Delta_{n,j}\Delta_{k,n}P_{VX}\left(\frac{\phi_{j}\phi_{k}}{p_{V_{\mathit{1}}X}}\right)=\left\lVert{p_{V_{\mathit{1}}X}}\right\rVert_{\infty}\sum_{j=1}^{J}\Delta_{n,j}^{2}

as
P_{VX}\left(\frac{\phi_{j}\phi_{k}}{p_{V_{\mathit{1}}X}}\right)=\nu_{V_{\mathit{1}}X}(\phi_{j}\phi_{k})=\mathbbm{1}_{j=k}
by the orthonormality of the
(\phi_{j})
. Assume that

\displaystyle\left\lVert{\frac{m}{p_{V_{\mathit{1}}X}}}\right\rVert_{\infty}\left\lVert{p_{V_{\mathit{1}}X}}\right\rVert_{\infty}<\infty,\quad\sum_{j=1}^{J}\left\lVert{\phi_{j}}\right\rVert_{\infty}^{2}=O\left(J\right).

As
\left\lVert{Q_{\mathcal{X}}^{-1}h}\right\rVert_{\infty}\leq\frac{2}{\alpha}\left\lVert{h}\right\rVert_{\infty}
,
P_{VX}T_{n}^{2}=O\left(\frac{J}{\alpha^{2}}\left\lVert{\frac{\check{p}_{V_{\mathit{1}}X}-p_{V_{\mathit{1}}X}}{\check{p}_{V_{\mathit{1}}X}}}\right\rVert_{\infty}^{2}\right)
.

To bound the variance term in (
40
), we use (
41
) under
Q\in\mathcal{Q}_{\delta}
:

\displaystyle\Var\left[\bar{w}_{n,i}(v_{\mathit{1}},x,V,Z,p_{V_{\mathit{1}}X})\right]\leq\mathbb{E}w_{n,i}^{2}(v_{\mathit{1}},x,V,Z,p_{V_{\mathit{1}}X})

\displaystyle=\mathbb{E}\left[\frac{m^{2}(V,Z)p_{V_{\mathit{1}}Z}(V_{\mathit{1}},Z)p_{V_{\mathit{1}}X}(v_{\mathit{1}},x)}{p_{V_{\mathit{1}}X}^{2}(V_{\mathit{1}},Z)}\frac{\left(\sum_{j=1}^{J}\phi_{j}(V_{\mathit{1}},Z)\phi_{j}(v_{\mathit{1}},x)\right)^{2}}{p_{V_{\mathit{1}}Z}(V_{\mathit{1}},Z)p_{V_{\mathit{1}}X}(v_{\mathit{1}},x)}\right]

\displaystyle\leq\left\lVert{\frac{m^{2}p_{V_{\mathit{1}}Z}}{p_{V_{\mathit{1}}X}^{2}}}\right\rVert_{\infty}\left\lVert{p_{V_{\mathit{1}}X}}\right\rVert_{\infty}\sum_{j=1}^{J}\sum_{k=1}^{J}\mathbb{E}\left[\frac{\phi_{j}\phi_{k}}{p_{V_{\mathit{1}}Z}}(V,Z)\right]\frac{\phi_{j}\phi_{k}}{p_{V_{\mathit{1}}X}}(v_{\mathit{1}},x).

Here the expectation equals
\mathbbm{1}_{j=k}
, and likewise
P_{V_{\mathit{1}}X}\left(\frac{\phi_{j}\phi_{k}}{p_{V_{\mathit{1}}X}}\right)=\mathbbm{1}_{j=k}
. Hence, if
\left\lVert{\frac{m^{2}p_{V_{\mathit{1}}Z}}{p_{V_{\mathit{1}}X}^{2}}}\right\rVert_{\infty}\left\lVert{p_{V_{\mathit{1}}X}}\right\rVert_{\infty}<\infty
, then
\int\Var\left[\bar{w}_{n,i}(v_{\mathit{1}},x,V,Z,p_{V_{\mathit{1}}X})\right]\,\mathrm{d}P_{V_{\mathit{1}}X}(v_{\mathit{1}},x)\lesssim J
.

To bound the bias in (
40
), first write by the tower property of expectations, expanding
\mu_{\mathcal{X}}=\sum_{j=1}^{\infty}a_{j}\phi_{j}
for
a_{j}=P_{VX}(\mu_{\mathcal{X}}\phi_{j})
,

\displaystyle\frac{1}{n}\sum_{i\in[n]}P_{VX}[w_{n,i}(v_{\mathit{1}},x,V,X,\vartheta)]

\displaystyle=P_{VX}\left[\sum_{j=1}^{J}\frac{m(V,X)\phi_{j}(V_{\mathit{1}},X)}{p_{V_{\mathit{1}}X}(V_{\mathit{1}}X)}\phi_{j}(v_{\mathit{1}},x)\right]

\displaystyle=P_{VX}\left[\left(\sum_{j=1}^{J}\frac{\phi_{j}(V_{\mathit{1}},X)}{p_{V_{\mathit{1}}X}(V_{\mathit{1}}X)}\phi_{j}(v_{\mathit{1}},x)\right)\sum_{j=1}^{\infty}a_{j}\phi_{j}(V_{\mathit{1}},X)\right]

\displaystyle=\sum_{j=1}^{J}a_{j}\phi_{j}(v_{\mathit{1}},x),

where we used the orthonormality of
(\phi_{j})
. Hence,

\left(\sum_{j=1}^{J}a_{j}\phi_{j}(v_{\mathit{1}},x)-\mu_{\mathcal{X}}(v_{\mathit{1}},x)\right)^{2}=\left(\sum_{j=J+1}^{\infty}a_{j}\phi_{j}(v_{\mathit{1}},x)\right)^{2}.

A nonnegative function, its integral is bounded by

\displaystyle P_{VX}\left(\sum_{j=J+1}^{\infty}a_{j}\phi_{j}(V_{\mathit{1}},X)\right)^{2}\leq\left\lVert{p_{V_{\mathit{1}}X}}\right\rVert_{\infty}\nu_{V_{\mathit{1}}X}\left(\sum_{j=J+1}^{\infty}a_{j}\phi_{j}(V_{\mathit{1}},X)\right)^{2}\leq\left\lVert{p_{V_{\mathit{1}}X}}\right\rVert_{\infty}\sum_{j=J+1}^{\infty}a_{j}^{2},

again by the orthonormality of
(\phi_{j})
. Thus, the bias in (
40
) is bounded by
O\left(\sum_{j=J+1}^{\infty}a_{j}^{2}\right)
.

Conclude that

\displaystyle P_{VX}({\check{\mu}_{\mathcal{X}}}-\mu_{\mathcal{X}})^{2}=O\left(\frac{J}{\alpha^{2}}\left\lVert{\frac{\check{p}_{V_{\mathit{1}}X}-p_{V_{\mathit{1}}X}}{\check{p}_{V_{\mathit{1}}X}}}\right\rVert_{\infty}^{2}\right)+O_{{P_{VZ}}}\left(\frac{J}{\alpha^{2}n}\right)+O\left(\sum_{j=J+1}^{\infty}a_{j}^{2}\right),

(90)

where the first term dominates the second one. Apart from the
\alpha^{-2}
privacy factor in the second term, the last two term correspond to the usual bound for orthogonal series estimates under equidistant or uniformly distributed design
(V_{\mathit{1}},X)
. The first term derives from of the weighting correction to accommodate the unknown density
p_{V_{\mathit{1}}X}
. This first term usually results in a suboptimal rate. Indeed, suppose that
\mu_{\mathcal{X}}
only depends on
X
and that
X_{i}=\frac{i}{n}
. If
\mu_{\mathcal{X}}
belongs to a Sobolev smoothness class
S(\beta,L)
for an
L>0
and integer
\beta>0
, so that the
(\beta-1)
-th derivative of
\mu_{\mathcal{X}}
is absolutely continuous and its integrated squared
\beta
-th derivative is bounded by
L
, then we expect
P_{VX}({\hat{\mu}_{\mathcal{X}}}-\mu_{\mathcal{X}})^{2}=O\left(\frac{J}{n}\right)+O\left(J^{-2\beta}\right)
for a trigonometric basis (see
47
). If
\beta
is known, a suitable choice of
J\sim n^{\frac{1}{2\beta+1}}
gives a rate
n^{\frac{-2\beta}{2\beta+1}}
. In contrast, if
\left\lVert{\hat{p}_{X}-p_{X}}\right\rVert_{\infty}=\left(\frac{\log n}{n}\right)^{a}
for some
0<a\leq\frac{1}{2}
— a common case for nonparametric density estimation under regularity conditions — and
\left\lVert{\frac{1}{\hat{p}_{X}}}\right\rVert_{\infty}<\infty
, then the choice of
J
balancing the terms in (
90
) in the nonprivate setting is
J\sim\left[\left(\frac{\log n}{n}\right)^{2a}+\frac{1}{n}\right]^{\frac{-1}{2\beta+1}}
, yielding a rate of
\left[\left(\frac{\log n}{n}\right)^{2a}+\frac{1}{n}\right]^{\frac{2\beta}{2\beta+1}}
for
{\hat{\mu}_{\mathcal{X}}}
. This is of the order
\left(\frac{\log n}{n}\right)^{2a\frac{2\beta}{2\beta+1}}
, so a loss of
n^{2a}
is incurred.

To prevent such a loss,
27
constructs an orthonormal basis in
L_{2}(\mathbb{P}_{n}^{\prime})
for the empirical distribution
\mathbb{P}_{n}^{\prime}
of
((V_{i}^{\prime},X_{i}^{\prime}))_{i\in[n]}
. Such a basis is constructed using the whole sample
((V_{\mathit{1}i}^{\prime},X_{i}^{\prime}))_{i\in[n]}
and for that reason it is unclear how it would lend itself for transformation to the private setting and analysis by
Proposition

3
.

Example 13

(name=Riesz Representer
r
— Orthogonal Series Estimator)
.

The Riesz representer
r=r_{\gamma_{\mathcal{V}}(c)}
can be estimated by orthogonal series similarly to the regression in
Example

12
. Assume that
r\in L_{2}(\nu_{V_{\mathit{1}}X})\subseteq L_{2}(P_{V_{\mathit{1}}X})
, and let
(\phi_{j})
,
j=1,2,\ldots
, be an orthonormal basis in
L_{2}(\nu_{V_{\mathit{1}}X})
. Then we can write
r=\sum_{j=1}^{\infty}a_{j}\phi_{j}
, where the projection coefficients
a_{j}\coloneqq\nu_{V_{\mathit{1}}X}(r_{\gamma_{\mathcal{V}}(c)}\phi_{j})
depend on
\gamma_{\mathcal{V}}(c)
. Assume that
\phi_{j}/p_{V_{\mathit{1}}X}\in L_{2}(P_{V_{\mathit{1}}X})
for all
j\geq 1
. Then, using the Riesz property “backwards,” we have

a_{j}=\nu_{V_{\mathit{1}}X}(r_{\gamma_{\mathcal{V}}(c)}\phi_{j})=P_{V_{\mathit{1}}X}\left(r_{\gamma_{\mathcal{V}}(c)}\frac{\phi_{j}}{p_{V_{\mathit{1}}X}}\right)=P_{VX}\left(f\left(V,X,\frac{\phi_{j}}{p_{V_{\mathit{1}}X}},\gamma_{\mathcal{V}}(c)\right)\right).

This is useful, because the form of
r_{\gamma_{\mathcal{V}}(c)}
may be unknown, so constructing estimates
\hat{a}_{j}
from the empirical version of
P_{VX}\left(r_{\gamma_{\mathcal{V}}(c)}\frac{\phi_{j}}{p_{V_{\mathit{1}}X}}\right)

may not be feasible; but
f
is known, so estimates can be derived from the rightmost side of the display. Indeed, we can estimate
r
in the nonprivate setting in the form (
37
) as

\displaystyle\hat{r}(v_{\mathit{1}},x)

\displaystyle\coloneqq\frac{1}{n}\sum_{i\in[n]}w_{n,i}(v_{\mathit{1}},x,V_{i}^{\prime},X_{i}^{\prime},\hat{p}_{V_{\mathit{1}}X},\hat{\gamma}_{\mathcal{V}}(c)),

\displaystyle w_{n,i}(v_{\mathit{1}},x,v^{\prime},x^{\prime},p_{V_{\mathit{1}}X},\gamma)

\displaystyle\coloneqq\sum_{j=1}^{J}f\left(v^{\prime},x^{\prime},\frac{\phi_{j}}{p_{V_{\mathit{1}}X}},\gamma\right)\phi_{j}(v_{\mathit{1}},x),

for a positive integer
J=J_{n}
tending to infinity with
n
; some estimator
\hat{\gamma}_{\mathcal{V}}(c)
of
\gamma_{\mathcal{V}}(c)
, such as (
108
), and
\hat{p}_{V_{\mathit{1}}X}
, an estimator of
p_{V_{\mathit{1}}X}
, both computed from
\mathcal{S}^{\prime}=((V_{i}^{\prime},X_{i}^{\prime}))_{i\in[n]}
.

Transferring
\hat{r}
to the private setting according to (
38
), we obtain

\displaystyle\check{r}(v_{\mathit{1}},x)

\displaystyle\coloneqq\frac{1}{n}\sum_{i\in[n]}\bar{w}_{n,i}(v_{\mathit{1}},x,V_{i}^{\prime},Z_{i}^{\prime},\check{p}_{V_{\mathit{1}}X},\check{\gamma}_{\mathcal{V}}(c)),

\displaystyle\bar{w}_{n,i}(v_{\mathit{1}},x,v^{\prime},z^{\prime},p_{V_{\mathit{1}}X},\gamma)

\displaystyle=\sum_{j=1}^{J}\bar{f}\left(v^{\prime},z^{\prime},\frac{\phi_{j}}{p_{V_{\mathit{1}}X}},\gamma\right)\phi_{j}(v_{\mathit{1}},x),

where
\bar{f}(\cdot,\mu,\gamma)=(Q_{\mathcal{X}}^{-1}(v,x)\mapsto f(v,x,\mu,\gamma))(\cdot)
, and
\check{\gamma}_{\mathcal{V}}(c)
is an estimator of
\gamma_{\mathcal{V}}(c)
, such as (
28
), and
\check{p}_{V_{\mathit{1}}X}
of
p_{V_{\mathit{1}}X}
; all computed from
\bar{\mathcal{S}}^{\prime}=((V_{i}^{\prime},Z_{i}^{\prime}))_{i\in[n]}
. Let us turn to the implications of
Proposition

3
, which are similar to those in
Example

12
, except now we have two secondary-nuisance parameters
\vartheta=(\gamma_{\mathcal{V}}(c),p_{V_{\mathit{1}}X})
. We assume that
Q\in\mathcal{Q}_{\delta}
in (
16
).

The term
T_{n}
in (
39
) is

\displaystyle T_{n}(v_{\mathit{1}},x)=

\displaystyle\,\left\{\frac{1}{n}\sum_{i\in[n]}\bar{w}_{n,i}(v_{\mathit{1}},x,V_{i}^{\prime},Z_{i}^{\prime},\check{p}_{V_{\mathit{1}}X},\check{\gamma}_{\mathcal{V}}(c))-\frac{1}{n}\sum_{i\in[n]}\bar{w}_{n,i}(v_{\mathit{1}},x,V_{i}^{\prime},Z_{i}^{\prime},\check{p}_{V_{\mathit{1}}X},\gamma_{\mathcal{V}}(c))\right\}

\displaystyle+\left\{\frac{1}{n}\sum_{i\in[n]}\bar{w}_{n,i}(v_{\mathit{1}},x,V_{i}^{\prime},Z_{i}^{\prime},\check{p}_{V_{\mathit{1}}X},\gamma_{\mathcal{V}}(c))-\frac{1}{n}\sum_{i\in[n]}\bar{w}_{n,i}(v_{\mathit{1}},x,V_{i}^{\prime},Z_{i}^{\prime},p_{V_{\mathit{1}}X},\gamma_{\mathcal{V}}(c))\right\}

\displaystyle\eqqcolon

\displaystyle\,T_{n1}(v_{\mathit{1}},x)+T_{n2}(v_{\mathit{1}},x).

By the mean-value theorem,
T_{n1}(v_{\mathit{1}},x)=(\check{\gamma}_{\mathcal{V}}(c)-\gamma_{\mathcal{V}}(c))\sum_{j=1}^{J}D_{n,j}\phi_{j}(v_{\mathit{1}},x)
, where the
D_{n,j}\coloneqq\frac{1}{n}\sum_{i\in[n]}\partial_{\gamma}\bar{f}\left(V_{i}^{\prime},Z_{i}^{\prime},\frac{\phi_{j}}{\check{p}_{V_{\mathit{1}}}X},\tilde{\gamma}_{\mathcal{V}}(c)\right)
do not depend on
(v_{\mathit{1}},x)
, and
\tilde{\gamma}_{\mathcal{V}}(c)
is some value between
\check{\gamma}_{\mathcal{V}}(c)
and
\gamma_{\mathcal{V}}(c)
. Because
V_{\mathit{2}}
is discretely distributed,
\check{\gamma}_{\mathcal{V}}(c)-\gamma_{\mathcal{V}}(c)=O_{{P_{VZ}}}\left(n^{-1/2}\right)
for any reasonable estimator such as (
28
). Then, along arguments in
Example

12
,

P_{VX}T_{n1}^{2}\leq O_{{P_{VZ}}}\left(1/n\right)\left\lVert{p_{V_{\mathit{1}}X}}\right\rVert_{\infty}\sum_{j=1}^{J}D_{n,j}^{2}

by the orthonormality of
(\phi_{j})
. Assume
\sum_{j=1}^{J}D_{n,j}^{2}=O_{{P_{VZ}}}\left(\frac{J}{\alpha^{2}}\right)
, which is reasonable for
\partial_{\gamma}f
bounded in all its arguments and privacy mechanism (
16
). Then, by arguments in
Example

12
,
P_{VX}T_{n1}^{2}=O_{{P_{VZ}}}\left(\frac{J}{\alpha^{2}n}\right)
provided
\left\lVert{p_{V_{\mathit{1}}X}}\right\rVert_{\infty}<\infty
.

By the linearity of
f
and thus
\bar{f}
in the regression argument,
T_{2n}(v_{\mathit{1}},x)=\sum_{j=1}^{J}\Delta_{n,j}\phi_{j}(v_{\mathit{1}},x)
for
\Delta_{n,j}\coloneqq\frac{1}{n}\sum_{i\in[n]}\bar{f}\left(V_{i}^{\prime},Z_{i}^{\prime},\phi_{j}\cdot\left(\frac{1}{\check{p}_{V_{\mathit{1}}X}}-\frac{1}{p_{V_{\mathit{1}}X}}\right),\gamma_{\mathcal{V}}(c)\right)
. Assuming
\left\lVert{p_{V_{\mathit{1}}X}}\right\rVert_{\infty}<\infty
and

\sum_{j=1}^{J}\Delta_{n,j}^{2}=O_{{P_{VZ}}}\left(\frac{J}{\alpha^{2}}\left\lVert{\frac{\check{p}_{V_{\mathit{1}}X}-p_{V_{\mathit{1}}X}}{\check{p}_{V_{\mathit{1}}X}}}\right\rVert_{\infty}^{2}\right),

we obtain

P_{VX}T_{n}^{2}=O_{{P_{VZ}}}\left(\frac{J}{\alpha^{2}n}\right)+O_{{P_{VZ}}}\left(\frac{J}{\alpha^{2}}\left\lVert{\check{p}_{V_{\mathit{1}}X}-p_{V_{\mathit{1}}X}}\right\rVert_{\infty}^{2}\right)=O_{{P_{VZ}}}\left(\frac{J}{\alpha^{2}}\left\lVert{\check{p}_{V_{\mathit{1}}X}-p_{V_{\mathit{1}}X}}\right\rVert_{\infty}^{2}\right).

We bound the variance term in (
40
) by (
41
) under
Q\in\mathcal{Q}_{\delta}
. Assume that
\left\lVert{p_{V_{\mathit{1}}X}}\right\rVert_{\infty}<\infty
and

\displaystyle\sum_{j=1}^{J}\mathbb{E}f^{2}\left(V,Z,\frac{\phi_{j}}{p_{V_{\mathit{1}}X}},\gamma_{\mathcal{V}}(c)\right)=O\left(J\right).

Then the arguments in
Example

12
give
\int\Var\left[\bar{w}_{n,i}(v_{\mathit{1}},x,V,Z,p_{V_{\mathit{1}}X},\gamma_{\mathcal{V}}(c))\right]\,\mathrm{d}P_{V_{\mathit{1}}X}(v_{\mathit{1}},x)\lesssim J
.

The bias in (
40
) can be bounded as in
Example

12
, yielding the bound
O\left(\sum_{j=J+1}^{\infty}a_{j}^{2}\right)
for
a_{j}=\nu_{V_{\mathit{1}}X}(r_{\gamma_{\mathcal{V}}(c)}\phi_{j})
.

Conclude that

\displaystyle P_{VZ}(\check{r}-r)^{2}=O_{{P_{VZ}}}\left(\frac{J}{\alpha^{2}}\left\lVert{\frac{\check{p}_{V_{\mathit{1}}X}-p_{V_{\mathit{1}}X}}{\check{p}_{V_{\mathit{1}}X}}}\right\rVert_{\infty}^{2}\right)+O_{{P_{VZ}}}\left(\frac{J}{\alpha^{2}n}\right)+O\left(\sum_{j=J+1}^{\infty}a_{j}^{2}\right),

where the first term dominates the second one. See
Example

12
for a discussion of these terms; in particular, on how the estimated secondary-nuisance
p_{V_{\mathit{1}}X}
of the first term leads to a rate slower than what only the last two terms would imply.

Appendix E
Nonprivate Estimation

Our aim is private inference, but given that our parameter class is novel, we also present results on nonprivate inference in
Section

E.1
, proven in
Section

E.2
.

E.1.  Results

In this section, we estimate
\chi(P_{VX})
of (
T
) in the nonprivate setting, from random samples from
(V,X)\sim P_{VX}
in the nonparametric model (
M
). For estimation, we further require the approximability conditions

\displaystyle\begin{aligned} \mathbb{E}\left[\{f(V,X,\mu,\gamma)-f(V,X,\mu_{\mathcal{X}},\gamma_{\mathcal{V}}(c))\}^{2}\right]&\to 0\\
\mathbb{E}\left[\{\partial_{\gamma}f(V,X,\mu,\gamma)-\partial_{\gamma}f(V,X,\mu_{\mathcal{X}},\gamma_{\mathcal{V}}(c))\}^{2}\right]&\to 0\end{aligned}

(C.SC)

as
\rho((\mu,\gamma),(\mu_{\mathcal{X}},\gamma_{\mathcal{V}}(c)))\to 0
. Also note that the linearity (
C.L
) implies the
P_{VX}
-a.s. linearity of
\mu\mapsto\frac{\partial^{j}f}{\partial\gamma^{j}}(V,X,\mu,\bar{\gamma})
for any integer
j\geq 1
for which the derivative exists. This follows directly from the definition of the derivative.

Recall the one-step estimator

\displaystyle\hat{\chi}_{n}=\chi(\hat{P}_{VX})+\mathbb{P}_{n}\hat{\tilde{\chi}}=\chi(\hat{P}_{VX})+\frac{1}{n}\sum_{i\in[n]}\hat{\tilde{\chi}}(V_{i},X_{i}).

from (
1
). Here, the estimator of the influence function (
4
) is

\displaystyle\begin{aligned} \hat{\tilde{\chi}}(v,x)\coloneqq&\,\hat{r}(v_{\mathit{1}},x)(m(v,x)-{\hat{\mu}_{\mathcal{X}}}(v_{\mathit{1}},x))+\frac{\mathbbm{1}_{v_{\mathit{2}}=c}}{\hat{p}_{V_{\mathit{2}}}(c)}(g(v,x)-\hat{\gamma}_{\mathcal{V}}(c))\hat{e}\\
&+f(v,x,{\hat{\mu}_{\mathcal{X}}},\hat{\gamma}_{\mathcal{V}}(c))-\chi(\hat{P}_{VX}),\end{aligned}

(91)

where
\hat{r},{\hat{\mu}_{\mathcal{X}}}
, taking values in
L_{2}(P_{V_{\mathit{1}}X})
, are some estimators of
r,\mu_{\mathcal{X}}
, respectively;
\hat{p}_{V_{\mathit{2}}}(c)
, taking values in , is some estimator of
p_{V_{\mathit{2}}}(c)
; and

\hat{e}
, taking values in , is some estimator of

\displaystyle e\coloneqq\mathbb{E}\partial_{\gamma}f(V,X,\mu_{\mathcal{X}},\gamma_{\mathcal{V}}(c)).

(92)

Note that we use the same initial estimator
\chi(\hat{P}_{VX})
in
\hat{\tilde{\chi}}
, which we may set to

\displaystyle\chi(\hat{P}_{VX})=\mathbb{P}_{n}f(V,X,{\hat{\mu}_{\mathcal{X}}},\hat{\gamma}_{\mathcal{V}}(c))=\frac{1}{n}\sum_{i\in[n]}f(V_{i},X_{i},{\hat{\mu}_{\mathcal{X}}},\hat{\gamma}_{\mathcal{V}}(c)).

(93)

Therefore,

\displaystyle\hat{\chi}_{n}=

\displaystyle\,\chi(\hat{P}_{VX})+\mathbb{P}_{n}\hat{\tilde{\chi}}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\,\frac{1}{n}\sum_{i\in[n]}\left\{\hat{r}(v_{\mathit{1}i},X_{i})(m(V_{i},X_{i})-{\hat{\mu}_{\mathcal{X}}}(v_{\mathit{1}i},X_{i}))+\frac{\mathbbm{1}_{V_{\mathit{2}i}=c}}{\hat{p}_{V_{\mathit{2}}}(c)}(g(V_{i},X_{i})-\hat{\gamma}_{\mathcal{V}}(c))\hat{e}\right.

\displaystyle+\left.\vphantom{\frac{\mathbbm{1}_{V_{\mathit{2}i}=c}}{\hat{p}_{V_{\mathit{2}}}(c)}}f(V_{i},X_{i},{\hat{\mu}_{\mathcal{X}}},\hat{\gamma}_{\mathcal{V}}(c))\right\}.

We assume that the estimators

\displaystyle\begin{aligned} \hat{\eta}&\coloneqq(\hat{r},{\hat{\mu}_{\mathcal{X}}},\hat{\gamma}_{\mathcal{V}}(c),\hat{p}_{V_{\mathit{2}}}(c),\hat{e})\in L_{2}(P_{V_{\mathit{1}}X})\times L_{2}(P_{V_{\mathit{1}}X})\times\Gamma\times\real\times\real\,\text{ of }\\
\eta&\coloneqq(r,\mu_{\mathcal{X}},\gamma_{\mathcal{V}}(c),p_{V_{\mathit{2}}}(c),e)\end{aligned}

(94)

are computed from random samples from
P_{VX}
which are independentof

\mathcal{S}\coloneqq\mathcal{(}(V_{i},X_{i}))_{i\in[n]}.

Specifically, we assume that there are two more random samples

\displaystyle\mathcal{S}^{\prime}\coloneqq((V_{i}^{\prime},X_{i}^{\prime}))_{i\in[n]}\,\text{ and }\,\mathcal{S}^{\prime\prime}\coloneqq((V_{i}^{\prime\prime},X_{i}^{\prime\prime}))_{i\in[n]}

from
P_{VX}
, with
\mathcal{S},\mathcal{S}^{\prime},\mathcal{S}^{\prime\prime}
mutually independent, where
\mathcal{S^{\prime}}
is used for the estimation of
(r,\mu_{\mathcal{X}},\gamma_{\mathcal{V}}(c),p_{V_{\mathit{2}}}(c))
, and
\mathcal{S}^{\prime},\mathcal{S}^{\prime\prime}
are used for the estimation of
e
in (
92
) as

\displaystyle\hat{e}\coloneqq\mathbb{P}_{n}^{\prime\prime}\partial_{\gamma}f(V,X,{\hat{\mu}_{\mathcal{X}}},\hat{\gamma}_{\mathcal{V}}(c))\coloneqq\frac{1}{n}\sum_{i\in[n]}\partial_{\gamma}f(V_{i}^{\prime\prime},X_{i}^{\prime\prime},{\hat{\mu}_{\mathcal{X}}},\hat{\gamma}_{\mathcal{V}}(c)).

(95)

With this estimation strategy, we can establish the consistency of
\hat{e}
, and hence of
\hat{\tilde{\chi}}
and
\hat{\chi}_{n}
in turn, without additional regularity conditions. Decompose

\displaystyle\sqrt{n}(\hat{\chi}_{n}-\chi(P_{VX}))

\displaystyle=\sqrt{n}\mathbb{P}_{n}\tilde{\chi}+\sqrt{n}(\mathbb{P}_{n}-P_{VX})(\hat{\tilde{\chi}}-\tilde{\chi})+\sqrt{n}R_{n},

(96)

\displaystyle R_{n}

\displaystyle\coloneqq\chi(\hat{P}_{VX})-\chi(P_{VX})+P_{VX}\hat{\tilde{\chi}},

(97)

where we used that
P_{VX}\tilde{\chi}=0
by
\tilde{\chi}
being the influence function. The term
\sqrt{n}\mathbb{P}_{n}\tilde{\chi}\overset{P_{VX}}{\rightsquigarrow}\mathcal{N}(0,P_{VX}\tilde{\chi}^{2})
by the standard central limit theorem. The second term in (
96
) is called the empirical process term and is vanishing as
o_{P_{VX}}\left(1\right)
under consistent estimators
\hat{\eta}
and stochastic boundedness conditions.

Assumption 4

(Consistent Estimators)
.

It holds that

\displaystyle\left\lVert{\hat{r}-r}\right\rVert_{L_{2}(P_{VX})}

\displaystyle=o_{P_{VX}}\left(1\right),

(98)

\displaystyle\hat{\gamma}_{\mathcal{V}}(c)-\gamma_{\mathcal{V}}(c)

\displaystyle=o_{P_{VX}}\left(1\right),

(99)

\displaystyle\hat{p}_{V_{2}}(c)-p_{V_{2}}(c)

\displaystyle=o_{P_{VX}}\left(1\right).

(100)

Further, it either holds that

\displaystyle\left\lVert{m-\mu_{\mathcal{X}}}\right\rVert_{\infty}

\displaystyle=O\left(1\right),

(101)

\displaystyle\left\lVert{\mu_{\mathcal{X}}-{\hat{\mu}_{\mathcal{X}}}}\right\rVert_{\infty}

\displaystyle=o_{P_{VX}}\left(1\right),

(102)

or that

\displaystyle\left\lVert{m-{\hat{\mu}_{\mathcal{X}}}}\right\rVert_{\infty}

\displaystyle=O_{{P_{VX}}}\left(1\right),

(103)

\displaystyle\left\lVert{\mu_{\mathcal{X}}-{\hat{\mu}_{\mathcal{X}}}}\right\rVert_{L_{2}(P_{VX})}

\displaystyle=o_{P_{VX}}\left(1\right),

(104)

\displaystyle P_{VX}\left(\left\{(V_{\mathit{1}},X)\in\mathfrak{V}_{\mathit{1}}\times\mathfrak{X}:|r(V_{\mathit{1}},X)|>\bar{R}\right\}\right)

\displaystyle=0

(105)

for some constant
\bar{R}<\infty
, where (
105
) may be replaced by
\left\lVert{r}\right\rVert_{\infty}<\infty
.

Lemma 9

(Vanishing Empirical Process Term)
.

Assume that
4
holds for
\hat{\eta}
in (
94
).
Then
\hat{e}-e=o_{P_{VX}}\left(1\right)
and
(\mathbb{P}_{n}-P_{VX})(\hat{\tilde{\chi}}-\tilde{\chi})=o_{P_{VX}}\left(n^{-1/2}\right)
.

Hence, if the nuisance parameters in
\eta
are consistently estimated and bound conditions apply, the behaviour of
\sqrt{n}(\hat{\chi}_{n}-\chi(P_{VX}))
is governed by the second-order bias term
R_{n}
in (
97
). We show that our class of parameters (
T
) enjoys a rate-double-robustness property, therefore
R_{n}
exhibits a product-structure of estimation errors.

Theorem

1
implies that the bias in (
97
) is

\displaystyle\begin{aligned} R_{n}=&\ \chi(\hat{P}_{VX})-\chi(P_{VX})+P_{VX}\hat{\tilde{\chi}}\\
=&\ -P_{VX}(r-\hat{r})(\mu_{\mathcal{X}}-{\hat{\mu}_{\mathcal{X}}})+(\gamma_{\mathcal{V}}(c)-\hat{\gamma}_{\mathcal{V}}(c))\left(\frac{p_{V_{\mathit{2}}}(c)}{\hat{p}_{V_{\mathit{2}}}(c)}\hat{e}-e^{\prime}\right)\\
&-(\gamma_{\mathcal{V}}(c)-\hat{\gamma}_{\mathcal{V}}(c))^{2}\frac{P_{VX}\partial_{\gamma}^{2}f(V,X,{\hat{\mu}_{\mathcal{X}}},\tilde{\gamma}_{\mathcal{V}}(c))}{2},\end{aligned}

(106)

for some
\tilde{\gamma}_{\mathcal{V}}(c)
between
\gamma_{\mathcal{V}}(c)
and
\hat{\gamma}_{\mathcal{V}}(c)
, and

\displaystyle e^{\prime}\coloneqq

\displaystyle\,P_{VX}\partial_{\gamma}f(V,X,{\hat{\mu}_{\mathcal{X}}},\hat{\gamma}_{\mathcal{V}}(c)).

(107)

Because
V_{\mathit{2}}
is distributed on a finite set, any reasonable estimators of
\gamma_{\mathcal{V}}(c)
,
p_{V_{\mathit{2}}}(c)
are
\sqrt{n}
-consistent; for instance

\displaystyle\hat{\gamma}_{\mathcal{V}}(c)\coloneqq\frac{1}{N_{c}}\sum_{i\in[n]}\mathbbm{1}_{V_{\mathit{2}i}^{\prime}=c}g(V_{i}^{\prime},X_{i}^{\prime}),\quad\hat{p}_{V_{\mathit{2}}}(c)\coloneqq N_{c}/n,\quad N_{c}\coloneqq\sum_{i\in[n]}\mathbbm{1}_{V_{\mathit{2}i}^{\prime}=c}

(108)

satisfy
\hat{\gamma}_{\mathcal{V}}(c)-\gamma_{\mathcal{V}}(c)=O_{{P_{VX}}}\left(n^{-1/2}\right)
,
\hat{p}_{V_{\mathit{2}}}(c)-p_{V_{\mathit{2}}}(c)=O_{{P_{VX}}}\left(n^{-1/2}\right)
by the standard central limit theorem. Suppose that
\hat{e}-e^{\prime}=o_{P_{VX}}\left(1\right)
and that
P_{VX}\partial_{\gamma}^{2}f(V,X,{\hat{\mu}_{\mathcal{X}}},\tilde{\gamma}_{\mathcal{V}}(c))=O_{{P_{VX}}}\left(1\right)
. It follows from (
106
) that the bias is then

\displaystyle R_{n}=-P_{VX}(r-\hat{r})(\mu_{\mathcal{X}}-{\hat{\mu}_{\mathcal{X}}})+o_{P_{VX}}\left(n^{-1/2}\right).

(109)

Hence,
R_{n}
is ultimately determined by the product of the estimation errors of the Riesz representer
r
and the regression function
\mu_{\mathcal{X}}
.
For example, we find that the average treatment effect on the treated is double robust. This is aligned with
13
, and is an improvement on
41
, who too, establish asymptotic normality, but not efficiency, as this parameter is not natively included in their class.

Our results amounts to the asymptotic efficiency of
\hat{\chi}_{n}
under fast enough estimation rates, boundedness conditions, and the estimation strategy using independent samples.

Assumption 5

(Rates of Estimators)
.

For
\hat{\eta}
in (
94
),

\displaystyle P_{VX}(r-\hat{r})(\mu_{\mathcal{X}}-{\hat{\mu}_{\mathcal{X}}})

\displaystyle=o_{P_{VX}}\left(n^{-1/2}\right),

\displaystyle\hat{\gamma}_{\mathcal{V}}(c)-\gamma_{\mathcal{V}}(c)

\displaystyle=O_{{P_{VX}}}\left(n^{-1/2}\right),

\displaystyle\hat{p}_{V_{\mathit{2}}}(c)-p_{V_{\mathit{2}}}(c)

\displaystyle=O_{{P_{VX}}}\left(n^{-1/2}\right),

\displaystyle P_{VX}\partial_{\gamma}^{2}f(V,X,{\hat{\mu}_{\mathcal{X}}},\tilde{\gamma}_{\mathcal{V}}(c))

\displaystyle=O_{{P_{VX}}}\left(1\right).

Corollary 6

(Asymptotic Efficiency of
\hat{\chi}_{n}
)
.

If
4
and
5
hold, then

\sqrt{n}(\hat{\chi}_{n}-\chi(P_{VX}))\overset{P_{VX}}{\rightsquigarrow}\mathcal{N}(0,P_{VX}\tilde{\chi}^{2})
as
n\to\infty
.

Conveniently and expectedly, when
(X,V_{\mathit{1}})
is discretely distributed, the same limit is achievable without the use of independent samples
\mathcal{S},\mathcal{S}^{\prime},\mathcal{S}^{\prime\prime}
. Indeed, the plug-in estimator
\chi(\hat{P}_{VX})
is asymptotically efficient when we compute the estimators
({\hat{\mu}_{\mathcal{X}}},\hat{\gamma}_{\mathcal{V}})
and
\chi(\hat{P}_{VX})
on the same sample
\mathcal{S}
, provided some boundedness conditions hold.

Proposition 4

(Asymptotic Efficiency of the Plug-in Estimator for Discrete
(X,V_{\mathit{1}})
)
.

Suppose that
\mathfrak{X}
and
\mathfrak{V}_{\mathit{1}}
are finite, and define

\displaystyle\chi(\hat{P}_{VX})\coloneqq

\displaystyle\,\frac{1}{n}\sum_{i\in[n]}f(V_{i},X_{i},{\hat{\mu}_{\mathcal{X}}},\hat{\gamma}_{\mathcal{V}}(c)),

\displaystyle{\hat{\mu}_{\mathcal{X}}}(v_{\mathit{1}},x)\coloneqq

\displaystyle\,\frac{1}{N_{v_{\mathit{1}}x}}\sum_{i\in[n]}\mathbbm{1}_{V_{\mathit{1}i}=v_{\mathit{1}},X_{i}=x}m(V_{i},X_{i}),\quad N_{v_{\mathit{1}}x}\coloneqq\sum_{i\in[n]}\mathbbm{1}_{V_{\mathit{1}i}=v_{\mathit{1}},X_{i}=x},

\displaystyle\hat{\gamma}_{\mathcal{V}}(c)\coloneqq

\displaystyle\,\frac{1}{N_{c}}\sum_{i\in[n]}\mathbbm{1}_{V_{\mathit{2}i}=c}g(V_{i},X_{i}),\quad N_{c}\coloneqq\sum_{i\in[n]}\mathbbm{1}_{V_{\mathit{2}i}=c}.

Assume that
\left\lVert{\mu_{\mathcal{X}}}\right\rVert_{\infty}<\infty
and that there exists an
\hat{r}:\mathfrak{V}_{1}\times\mathfrak{X}\to\real
such that
\left\lVert{\hat{r}-r}\right\rVert_{\infty}=o_{P_{VX}}\left(1\right)
, where
r
is the Riesz representer of
\mu\mapsto\mathbb{E}f(V,X,\mu,\gamma_{\mathcal{V}}(c))

=\chi(P_{VX})
as before. Further assume that for every fixed
\mu\in L_{2}(P_{V_{\mathit{1}}X})
and for every
\tilde{\gamma}_{\mathcal{V}}(c)
between
\gamma_{\mathcal{V}}(c)
and
\hat{\gamma}_{\mathcal{V}}(c)
, the bound conditions

\displaystyle(\mathbb{P}_{n}-P_{VX})\partial_{\gamma}f(V,X,\mu,\tilde{\gamma}_{\mathcal{V}}(c))

\displaystyle=O_{{P_{VX}}}\left(1\right),

(110)

\displaystyle(\mathbb{P}_{n}-P_{VX})\partial_{\gamma}^{2}f(V,X,\mu,\tilde{\gamma}_{\mathcal{V}}(c))

\displaystyle=O_{{P_{VX}}}\left(1\right),

(111)

\displaystyle P_{VX}\partial_{\gamma}f(V,X,\mu_{\mathcal{X}},\tilde{\gamma}_{\mathcal{V}}(c))

\displaystyle=O_{{P_{VX}}}\left(1\right),

(112)

\displaystyle P_{VX}\partial_{\gamma}^{2}f(V,X,\mu_{\mathcal{X}},\tilde{\gamma}_{\mathcal{V}}(c))

\displaystyle=O_{{P_{VX}}}\left(1\right),

(113)

\displaystyle P_{VX}\partial_{\gamma}^{2}f(V,X,{\hat{\mu}_{\mathcal{X}}},\tilde{\gamma}_{\mathcal{V}}(c))

\displaystyle=O_{{P_{VX}}}\left(1\right)

(114)

hold. Then
\sqrt{n}(\chi(\hat{P}_{VX})-\chi(P_{VX}))\overset{P_{VX}}{\rightsquigarrow}\mathcal{N}(0,P_{VX}\tilde{\chi}^{2})
as
n\to\infty
.

Note that for particular forms of
f
, the conditions of
Proposition

4
are easier to verify. Namely, if
f(v,x,\mu,\gamma)
factors as

f(v,x,\mu,\gamma)=f_{1}(v,x,\mu)f_{2}(\gamma)

where
\frac{\partial^{2}f_{2}}{\partial\gamma^{2}}
exists and is continuous and
(v,x)\mapsto f_{1}(v,x,\mu)
belongs to
L_{2}(P_{VX})
for any
\mu\in L_{2}(P_{V_{\mathit{1}}X})
, then the boundedness conditions (
110
),(
111
),(
112
), (
113
) are easily met by the standard central limit theorem and the continuous mapping theorem. For instance, this includes the average treatment effect on the treated in
Example

3
.

E.2.  Proofs

In this section, we prove the results in
Section

E.1
:
Lemmas

9
,
6
and
4
.

Proof of
Lemma

9
.

Throughout, we apply that if a
P_{VX}
-random function
\hat{q}\in L_{2}(P_{VX})
is independent of the random sample generating the process
\mathbb{P}_{n}
, then

\displaystyle\begin{aligned} \int(\hat{q}(v,x)-q(v,x))^{2}\,\mathrm{d}P_{VX}(v,x)=o_{P_{VX}}\left(1\right)\text{ implies }\\
\sqrt{n}(\mathbb{P}_{n}-P_{VX})(\hat{q}-q)=o_{P_{VX}}\left(1\right);\end{aligned}

(115)

which follows from Markov’s inequality and the dominated convergence theorem.

By the definitions (
4
) and (
91
),

\displaystyle\hat{\tilde{\chi}}(v,x)-\tilde{\chi}(v,x)=

\displaystyle\,T_{1}(v,x)+T_{2}(v,x)+T_{3}(v,x)+T_{4},

(116)

\displaystyle T_{1}(v,x)\coloneqq

\displaystyle\,\hat{r}(v_{\mathit{1}},x)(m(v,x)-{\hat{\mu}_{\mathcal{X}}}(v_{\mathit{1}},x))

\displaystyle-r(v_{\mathit{1}},x)(m(v,x)-\mu_{\mathcal{X}}(v_{\mathit{1}},x)),

\displaystyle T_{2}(v,x)\coloneqq

\displaystyle\,\frac{\mathbbm{1}_{v_{\mathit{2}}=c}}{\hat{p}_{V_{\mathit{2}}}(c)}(g(v,x)-\hat{\gamma}_{\mathcal{V}}(c))\hat{e}-\frac{\mathbbm{1}_{v_{\mathit{2}}=c}}{p_{V_{\mathit{2}}}(c)}(g(v,x)-\gamma_{\mathcal{V}}(c))e,

\displaystyle T_{3}(v,x)\coloneqq

\displaystyle\,f(v,x,{\hat{\mu}_{\mathcal{X}}},\hat{\gamma}_{\mathcal{V}}(c))-f(v,x,\mu_{\mathcal{X}},\gamma_{\mathcal{V}}(c)),

\displaystyle T_{4}\coloneqq

\displaystyle\,-\chi(\hat{P}_{VX})+\chi(P_{VX}).

As
T_{4}
is constant, not depending on
(v,x)
,
(\mathbb{P}_{n}-P_{VX})T_{4}=0
. It remains to show
(\mathbb{P}_{n}-P_{VX})T_{j}=o_{P_{VX}}\left(n^{-1/2}\right)
for
j=1,2,3
by the linearity of the process
\mathbb{P}_{n}-P_{VX}
.

Term
T_{1}.\,\,
Suppressing the arguments, write

\displaystyle T_{1}=\hat{r}(m-{\hat{\mu}_{\mathcal{X}}})-r(m-\mu_{\mathcal{X}})

\displaystyle=(\hat{r}-r+r)(m-{\hat{\mu}_{\mathcal{X}}})-r(m-\mu_{\mathcal{X}})

(117)

\displaystyle=(\hat{r}-r)(m-{\hat{\mu}_{\mathcal{X}}})+r(\mu_{\mathcal{X}}-{\hat{\mu}_{\mathcal{X}}}).

(118)

By
4
,
\left\lVert{m-{\hat{\mu}_{\mathcal{X}}}}\right\rVert_{\infty}=O_{{P_{VX}}}\left(1\right)
; either directly by
(
103
), or by (
101
) and (
102
), noting that
\left\lVert{m-{\hat{\mu}_{\mathcal{X}}}}\right\rVert_{\infty}\leq\left\lVert{m-\mu_{\mathcal{X}}}\right\rVert_{\infty}+\left\lVert{\mu_{\mathcal{X}}-{\hat{\mu}_{\mathcal{X}}}}\right\rVert_{\infty}=O_{{P_{VX}}}\left(1\right)+o_{P_{VX}}\left(1\right)=O_{{P_{VX}}}\left(1\right)
.
Then the
L_{2}(P_{V_{\mathit{1}}X})
-convergence (
98
) of
r
implies that
(\mathbb{P}_{n}-P_{VX})((\hat{r}-r)(m-{\hat{\mu}_{\mathcal{X}}}))=o_{P_{VX}}\left(n^{-1/2}\right)
by (
115
) as

\displaystyle P_{VX}((\hat{r}-r)^{2}(m-{\hat{\mu}_{\mathcal{X}}})^{2})

\displaystyle\leq\left\lVert{m-{\hat{\mu}_{\mathcal{X}}}}\right\rVert_{\infty}^{2}P_{VX}(\hat{r}-r)^{2}=O_{{P_{VX}}}\left(1\right)o_{P_{VX}}\left(1\right)=o_{P_{VX}}\left(1\right)

since
\left\lVert{|q|^{2}}\right\rVert_{\infty}=\left\lVert{q}\right\rVert_{\infty}^{2}
.

By
4
, either (
102
), or (
104
) and (
105
) hold. In the former case,

\displaystyle P_{VX}(r^{2}(\mu_{\mathcal{X}}-{\hat{\mu}_{\mathcal{X}}})^{2})\leq\left\lVert{\mu_{\mathcal{X}}-{\hat{\mu}_{\mathcal{X}}}}\right\rVert_{\infty}^{2}P_{VX}r^{2}=o_{P_{VX}}\left(1\right),

by (
115
) because
r\in L_{2}(P_{V_{\mathit{1}}X})
. In the latter case, letting

B\coloneqq\left\{(V_{\mathit{1}},X)\in\mathfrak{V}_{\mathit{1}}\times\mathfrak{X}:|r(V_{\mathit{1}},X)|>\bar{R}\right\}

with complement
B^{C}
, we have

\displaystyle P_{VX}(r^{2}(\mu_{\mathcal{X}}-{\hat{\mu}_{\mathcal{X}}})^{2})

\displaystyle=\int_{B^{C}}r^{2}(\mu_{\mathcal{X}}-{\hat{\mu}_{\mathcal{X}}})^{2}\,\mathrm{d}P_{VX}+\int_{B}r^{2}(\mu_{\mathcal{X}}-{\hat{\mu}_{\mathcal{X}}})^{2}\,\mathrm{d}P_{VX}

\displaystyle\leq\bar{R}^{2}\left\lVert{\mu_{\mathcal{X}}-{\hat{\mu}_{\mathcal{X}}}}\right\rVert_{L_{2}(P_{V_{\mathit{1}X}})}^{2}+0=o_{P_{VX}}\left(1\right),

since by (
105
),
P_{VX}(B)=0
and
{\hat{\mu}_{\mathcal{X}}}
is
L_{2}(P_{V_{\mathit{1}X}})
-convergent by (
104
). Thus,
(\mathbb{P}_{n}-P_{VX})(r(\mu_{\mathcal{X}}-{\hat{\mu}_{\mathcal{X}}}))=o_{P_{VX}}\left(n^{-1/2}\right)
by (
115
). Conclude that
(\mathbb{P}_{n}-P_{VX})T_{1}=o_{P_{VX}}\left(n^{-1/2}\right)
.

Term
T_{2}.\,\,
By the mean-value theorem there exists
(\tilde{\gamma}_{\mathcal{V}}(c),\tilde{p}_{V_{\mathit{2}}}(c),\tilde{e})
between

\displaystyle(\gamma_{\mathcal{V}}(c),p_{V_{\mathit{2}}}(c),e)\text{ and }(\hat{\gamma}_{\mathcal{V}}(c),\hat{p}_{V_{\mathit{2}}}(c),\hat{e})

such that

\displaystyle T_{2}(v,x)=

\displaystyle\,\frac{\mathbbm{1}_{v_{\mathit{2}}=c}}{\hat{p}_{V_{\mathit{2}}}(c)}(g(v,x)-\hat{\gamma}_{\mathcal{V}}(c))\hat{e}-\frac{\mathbbm{1}_{v_{\mathit{2}}=c}}{p_{V_{\mathit{2}}}(c)}(g(v,x)-\gamma_{\mathcal{V}}(c))e

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\,-\frac{\mathbbm{1}_{v_{\mathit{2}}=c}}{\tilde{p}_{V_{\mathit{2}}}(c)}\tilde{e}(\hat{\gamma}_{\mathcal{V}}(c)-\gamma_{\mathcal{V}}(c))

\displaystyle-\frac{\mathbbm{1}_{v_{\mathit{2}}=c}}{\tilde{p}_{V_{\mathit{2}}}(c)^{2}}(g(v,x)-\tilde{\gamma}_{\mathcal{V}}(c))\tilde{e}(\hat{p}_{V_{\mathit{2}}}(c)-p_{V_{\mathit{2}}}(c))

\displaystyle+\frac{\mathbbm{1}_{v_{\mathit{2}}=c}}{\tilde{p}_{V_{\mathit{2}}}(c)}(g(v,x)-\tilde{\gamma}_{\mathcal{V}}(c))(\hat{e}-e).

By the standard central limit theorem,
\sqrt{n}(\mathbb{P}_{n}-P_{VX})\mathbbm{1}_{V_{\mathit{2}}=c}=O_{{P_{VX}}}\left(1\right)
. By the linearity of the process
\mathbb{P}_{n}-P_{VX}
,

\displaystyle\sqrt{n}(\mathbb{P}_{n}-P_{VX})\big[\mathbbm{1}_{V_{\mathit{2}}=c}g(V,X)-\mathbbm{1}_{V_{\mathit{2}}=c}\tilde{\gamma}_{\mathcal{V}}(c)\big]

\displaystyle=\sqrt{n}(\mathbb{P}_{n}-P_{VX})\mathbbm{1}_{V_{\mathit{2}}=c}g(V,X)-\tilde{\gamma}_{\mathcal{V}}(c)\sqrt{n}(\mathbb{P}_{n}-P_{VX})\mathbbm{1}_{V_{\mathit{2}}=c}

\displaystyle=(1-\tilde{\gamma}_{\mathcal{V}}(c))O_{{P_{VX}}}\left(1\right)=O_{{P_{VX}}}\left(1\right)

again by the standard central limit theorem and (
99
). Suppose that
\hat{e}-e=o_{P_{VX}}\left(1\right)
, which we show later. Then by (
99
) and (
100
),
(\mathbb{P}_{n}-P_{VX})T_{2}=o_{P_{VX}}\left(n^{-1/2}\right)
.

Term
T_{3}.\,\,
Recall that
T_{3}(v,x)=f(v,x,{\hat{\mu}_{\mathcal{X}}},\hat{\gamma}_{\mathcal{V}}(c))-f(v,x,\mu_{\mathcal{X}},\gamma_{\mathcal{V}}(c))
. The continuity (
C.SC
), together with the consistency
of
\hat{\gamma}_{\mathcal{V}}
and
{\hat{\mu}_{\mathcal{X}}}
((
99
) and (
102
) or (
104
)) imply that
\int T_{3}(v,x)^{2}\,\mathrm{d}P_{VX}(v,x)=o_{P_{VX}}\left(1\right)
by the continuous mapping theorem. Conclude by (
115
) that
(\mathbb{P}_{n}-P_{VX})T_{3}=o_{P_{VX}}\left(n^{-1/2}\right)
.

Consistency of

\hat{e}.\,\,
By the definition of
e
and
\hat{e}
,

\displaystyle\hat{e}-e=

\displaystyle\,\mathbb{P}_{n}^{\prime\prime}\partial_{\gamma}f(V,X,{\hat{\mu}_{\mathcal{X}}},\hat{\gamma}_{\mathcal{V}}(c))-P_{VX}\partial_{\gamma}f(V,X,\mu_{\mathcal{X}},\gamma_{\mathcal{V}}(c))

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\,\mathbb{P}_{n}^{\prime\prime}\partial_{\gamma}f(V,X,{\hat{\mu}_{\mathcal{X}}},\hat{\gamma}_{\mathcal{V}}(c))-P_{VX}\partial_{\gamma}f(V,X,{\hat{\mu}_{\mathcal{X}}},\hat{\gamma}_{\mathcal{V}}(c))

\displaystyle+P_{VX}\partial_{\gamma}f(V,X,{\hat{\mu}_{\mathcal{X}}},\hat{\gamma}_{\mathcal{V}}(c))-P_{VX}\partial_{\gamma}f(V,X,\mu_{\mathcal{X}},\gamma_{\mathcal{V}}(c))

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\,(\mathbb{P}_{n}^{\prime\prime}-P_{VX})\partial_{\gamma}f(V,X,{\hat{\mu}_{\mathcal{X}}},\hat{\gamma}_{\mathcal{V}}(c))

\displaystyle+P_{VX}\big[\partial_{\gamma}f(V,X,{\hat{\mu}_{\mathcal{X}}},\hat{\gamma}_{\mathcal{V}}(c))-\partial_{\gamma}f(V,X,\mu_{\mathcal{X}},\gamma_{\mathcal{V}}(c))\big]

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\,(\mathbb{P}_{n}^{\prime\prime}-P_{VX})\partial_{\gamma}f(V,X,\mu_{\mathcal{X}},\gamma_{\mathcal{V}}(c))

\displaystyle+(\mathbb{P}_{n}^{\prime\prime}-P_{VX})\big[\partial_{\gamma}f(V,X,{\hat{\mu}_{\mathcal{X}}},\hat{\gamma}_{\mathcal{V}}(c))-\partial_{\gamma}f(V,X,\mu_{\mathcal{X}},\gamma_{\mathcal{V}}(c))\big]

\displaystyle+P_{VX}\big[\partial_{\gamma}f(V,X,{\hat{\mu}_{\mathcal{X}}},\hat{\gamma}_{\mathcal{V}}(c))-\partial_{\gamma}f(V,X,\mu_{\mathcal{X}},\gamma_{\mathcal{V}}(c))\big].

Here, the first term is
O_{{P_{VX}}}\left(n^{-1/2}\right)=o_{P_{VX}}\left(1\right)
by the standard central limit theorem, and the second and third term are
o_{P_{VX}}\left(1\right)
by the continuity (
C.SC
) along the same arguments concerning
T_{3}
above.
∎

Proof of
Corollary

6
.

Follows from
Lemmas

9
and
1
, noting that, for
e^{\prime}
in (
107
),

\hat{e}-e^{\prime}=(\mathbb{P}_{n}^{\prime\prime}-P_{VX})\partial_{\gamma}f(V,X,{\hat{\mu}_{\mathcal{X}}},\hat{\gamma}_{\mathcal{V}}(c))

is
o_{P_{VX}}\left(1\right)
by the consistency proof of
\hat{e}
in
Lemma

9
.
∎

Proof of
Proposition

4
.

We start with an auxiliary result. Define

\displaystyle\hat{\tilde{\chi}}(v,x)\coloneqq

\displaystyle\,\hat{r}(v_{\mathit{1}},x)(m(v,x)-{\hat{\mu}_{\mathcal{X}}}(v_{\mathit{1}},x))+\frac{\mathbbm{1}_{v_{\mathit{2}}=c}}{\hat{p}_{V_{\mathit{2}}}(c)}(g(v,x)-\hat{\gamma}_{\mathcal{V}}(c))\hat{e}

\displaystyle+f(v,x,{\hat{\mu}_{\mathcal{X}}},\hat{\gamma}_{\mathcal{V}}(c))-\chi(\hat{P}_{VX})

\displaystyle\hat{p}_{V_{\mathit{2}}}(c)\coloneqq

\displaystyle\,N_{c}/n.

First we show that

\displaystyle\mathbb{P}_{n}\hat{\tilde{\chi}}=\mathbb{P}_{n}\left[\hat{r}(V_{\mathit{1}},X)(m(V,X)-{\hat{\mu}_{\mathcal{X}}}(V_{\mathit{1}},X)\right]

\displaystyle+\frac{\hat{e}}{\hat{p}_{V_{\mathit{2}}}(c)}\mathbb{P}_{n}\left[\mathbbm{1}_{V_{\mathit{2}}=c}g(V,X)-\hat{\gamma}_{\mathcal{V}}(c)\mathbbm{1}_{V_{\mathit{2}}=c}\right]+\mathbb{P}_{n}\left[f(V,X,{\hat{\mu}_{\mathcal{X}}},\hat{\gamma}_{\mathcal{V}}(c))-\chi(\hat{P}_{VX}))\right]

is zero. We apply an ‘empirical tower property’ to the first term to get

\displaystyle\mathbb{P}_{n}\hat{r}(V_{\mathit{1}},X)(m(V,X)-{\hat{\mu}_{\mathcal{X}}}(V_{\mathit{1}},X))=\frac{1}{n}\sum_{i\in[n]}\hat{r}(V_{\mathit{1}i},X_{i})(m(V_{i},X_{i})-{\hat{\mu}_{\mathcal{X}}}(V_{\mathit{1}i},X_{i}))

\displaystyle=\frac{1}{n}\sum_{(v_{\mathit{1}},x)\in\mathfrak{V}_{\mathit{1}}\times\mathfrak{X}}\sum_{i:(V_{\mathit{1}i},X_{i})=(v_{\mathit{1}},x)}\hat{r}(V_{\mathit{1}i},X_{i})(m(V_{i},X_{i})-{\hat{\mu}_{\mathcal{X}}}(V_{\mathit{1}i},X_{i}))

\displaystyle=\frac{1}{n}\sum_{(v_{\mathit{1}},x)\in\mathfrak{V}_{\mathit{1}}\times\mathfrak{X}}\left\{\hat{r}(v_{\mathit{1}},x)\vphantom{\left[\left(\sum_{i:(V_{\mathit{1}i},X_{i})=(v_{\mathit{1}},x)}m(V_{i},X_{i})\right)-\left(\sum_{i:(V_{\mathit{1}i},X_{i})=(v_{\mathit{1}},x)}{\hat{\mu}_{\mathcal{X}}}(v_{\mathit{1}},x)\right)\right]}\right.

\displaystyle\left.\times\left[\left(\sum_{i:(V_{\mathit{1}i},X_{i})=(v_{\mathit{1}},x)}m(V_{i},X_{i})\right)-\left(\sum_{i:(V_{\mathit{1}i},X_{i})=(v_{\mathit{1}},x)}{\hat{\mu}_{\mathcal{X}}}(v_{\mathit{1}},x)\right)\right]\right\}

\displaystyle=\frac{1}{n}\sum_{(v_{\mathit{1}},x)\in\mathfrak{V}_{\mathit{1}}\times\mathfrak{X}}\hat{r}(v_{\mathit{1}},x)\left[N_{v_{\mathit{1}}x}{\hat{\mu}_{\mathcal{X}}}(v_{\mathit{1}},x)-N_{v_{\mathit{1}}x}{\hat{\mu}_{\mathcal{X}}}(v_{\mathit{1}},x)\right]=0.

The second term satisfies

\displaystyle\mathbb{P}_{n}\left[\mathbbm{1}_{V_{\mathit{2}}=c}g(V,X)-\hat{\gamma}_{\mathcal{V}}(c)\mathbbm{1}_{V_{\mathit{2}}=c}\right]

\displaystyle=\mathbb{P}_{n}\mathbbm{1}_{V_{\mathit{2}}=c}g(V,X)-\hat{\gamma}_{\mathcal{V}}(c)\mathbb{P}_{n}\mathbbm{1}_{V_{\mathit{2}}=c}

\displaystyle=N_{c}\hat{\gamma}_{\mathcal{V}}(c)-\hat{\gamma}_{\mathcal{V}}(c)N_{c}=0

by the definition of
\hat{\gamma}_{\mathcal{V}}(c),N_{c}
. The last term satisfies

\mathbb{P}_{n}\left[f(V,X,{\hat{\mu}_{\mathcal{X}}},\hat{\gamma}_{\mathcal{V}}(c))-\chi(\hat{P}_{VX}))\right]=\mathbb{P}_{n}f(V,X,{\hat{\mu}_{\mathcal{X}}},\hat{\gamma}_{\mathcal{V}}(c))-\chi(\hat{P}_{VX})=0

by the definition of
\chi(\hat{P}_{VX})
as
\chi(\hat{P}_{VX})
is constant with respect to the empirical measure
\mathbb{P}_{n}
. Conclude that
\mathbb{P}_{n}\hat{\tilde{\chi}}=0
.

Now we show asymptotic efficiency. Using that
\mathbb{P}_{n}\hat{\tilde{\chi}}=0
, write

\displaystyle\sqrt{n}(\chi(\hat{P}_{VX})-\chi(P_{VX}))

\displaystyle=\sqrt{n}\mathbb{P}_{n}\tilde{\chi}+\sqrt{n}(\mathbb{P}_{n}-P_{VX})(\hat{\tilde{\chi}}-\tilde{\chi})+\sqrt{n}R_{n},

\displaystyle R_{n}

\displaystyle=\chi(\hat{P}_{VX})-\chi(P_{VX})+P_{VX}\hat{\tilde{\chi}},

for
\tilde{\chi}
in (
4
). The first term satisfies
\sqrt{n}\mathbb{P}_{n}\tilde{\chi}\overset{P_{VX}}{\rightsquigarrow}\mathcal{N}(0,P_{VX}\tilde{\chi}^{2})
. Because the arguments in
Theorem

1
also apply when
({\hat{\mu}_{\mathcal{X}}},\hat{\gamma}_{\mathcal{V}})
and
\chi(\hat{P}_{VX})
are computed from the same sample
\mathcal{S}
, we have
\sqrt{n}R_{n}=o_{P_{VX}}\left(1\right)
. This follows from (
6
) of
Theorem

1
, because
{\hat{\mu}_{\mathcal{X}}}
,
\hat{\gamma}_{\mathcal{V}}(c)
and
\hat{p}_{V_{\mathit{2}}}(c)
are asymptotically normal,
\hat{r}
is consistent ,
P_{VX}\partial_{\gamma}^{2}f(V,X,{\hat{\mu}_{\mathcal{X}}},\tilde{\gamma}_{\mathcal{V}}(c)=O_{{P_{VX}}}\left(1\right)
by (
114
), and, as we show at the end of this proof,
\hat{e}-e^{\prime}=o_{P_{VX}}\left(1\right)
for
e^{\prime}=P_{VX}\partial_{\gamma}f(V,X,{\hat{\mu}_{\mathcal{X}}},\hat{\gamma}_{\mathcal{V}}(c))
.

It remains to show
\sqrt{n}(\mathbb{P}_{n}-P_{VX})(\hat{\tilde{\chi}}-\tilde{\chi})=o_{P_{VX}}\left(1\right)
. Because
Lemma

9
assumes that
({\hat{\mu}_{\mathcal{X}}},\hat{\gamma}_{\mathcal{V}})
are computed from a sample independent from what generates
\mathbb{P}_{n}
, we need to adapt the arguments therein. As in (
116
), write

\displaystyle\hat{\tilde{\chi}}(v,x)-\tilde{\chi}(v,x)=

\displaystyle\,T_{1}(v,x)+T_{2}(v,x)+T_{3}(v,x)+T_{4},

(119)

\displaystyle T_{1}(v,x)\coloneqq

\displaystyle\,\hat{r}(v_{\mathit{1}},x)(m(v,x)-{\hat{\mu}_{\mathcal{X}}}(v_{\mathit{1}},x))

\displaystyle-r(v_{\mathit{1}},x)(m(v,x)-\mu_{\mathcal{X}}(v_{\mathit{1}},x)),

\displaystyle T_{2}(v,x)\coloneqq

\displaystyle\,\frac{\mathbbm{1}_{v_{\mathit{2}}=c}}{\hat{p}_{V_{\mathit{2}}}(c)}(g(v,x)-\hat{\gamma}_{\mathcal{V}}(c))\hat{e}-\frac{\mathbbm{1}_{v_{\mathit{2}}=c}}{p_{V_{\mathit{2}}}(c)}(g(v,x)-\gamma_{\mathcal{V}}(c))e,

\displaystyle T_{3}(v,x)\coloneqq

\displaystyle\,f(v,x,{\hat{\mu}_{\mathcal{X}}},\hat{\gamma}_{\mathcal{V}}(c))-f(v,x,\mu_{\mathcal{X}},\gamma_{\mathcal{V}}(c)),

\displaystyle T_{4}\coloneqq

\displaystyle\,-\chi(\hat{P}_{VX})+\chi(P_{VX}).

Again,
T_{4}
being a constant under
\mathbb{P}_{n}-P_{VX}
,
\sqrt{n}(\mathbb{P}_{n}-P_{VX})T_{4}=0
, so we need show
(\mathbb{P}_{n}-P_{VX})T_{j}=o_{P_{VX}}\left(n^{-1/2}\right)
for
j=1,2,3
.

Term
T_{1}.\,\,
Write

\displaystyle\sqrt{n}(\mathbb{P}_{n}-P_{VX})T_{1}(V,X)=\sqrt{n}(\mathbb{P}_{n}-P_{VX})\left[m(V,X)(\hat{r}(V_{\mathit{1}},X)-r(V_{\mathit{1}},X))\right]

\displaystyle+\sqrt{n}(\mathbb{P}_{n}-P_{VX})\left[{\hat{\mu}_{\mathcal{X}}}(V_{\mathit{1}},X)(\hat{r}(V_{\mathit{1}},X)-r(V_{\mathit{1}},X))\right]

(120)

\displaystyle+\sqrt{n}(\mathbb{P}_{n}-P_{VX})\left[r(V_{\mathit{1}},X)(\mu_{\mathcal{X}}(V_{\mathit{1}},X)-{\hat{\mu}_{\mathcal{X}}}(V_{\mathit{1}},X))\right].

(121)

Because
\mathfrak{V}_{\mathit{1}}\times\mathfrak{X}
is finite, we can write

\displaystyle r(v_{\mathit{1}},x)

\displaystyle=\sum_{(\bar{v}_{\mathit{1}},\bar{x})\in\mathfrak{V}_{\mathit{1}}\times\mathfrak{X}}\varrho_{\bar{v}_{\mathit{1}},\bar{x}}\mathbbm{1}_{(\bar{v}_{\mathit{1}},\bar{x})}(v_{\mathit{1}},x),\quad\varrho_{\bar{v}_{\mathit{1}},\bar{x}}\coloneqq r(\bar{v}_{\mathit{1}},\bar{x}),

\displaystyle\hat{r}(v_{\mathit{1}},x)

\displaystyle=\sum_{(\bar{v}_{\mathit{1}},\bar{x})\in\mathfrak{V}_{\mathit{1}}\times\mathfrak{X}}\hat{\varrho}_{\bar{v}_{\mathit{1}},\bar{x}}\mathbbm{1}_{(\bar{v}_{\mathit{1}},\bar{x})}(v_{\mathit{1}},x),\quad\hat{\varrho}_{\bar{v}_{\mathit{1}},\bar{x}}\coloneqq\hat{r}(\bar{v}_{\mathit{1}},\bar{x}).

But then

\displaystyle\sqrt{n}(\mathbb{P}_{n}-P_{VX})\left[m(V,X)(\hat{r}(V_{\mathit{1}},X)-r(V_{\mathit{1}},X))\right]

\displaystyle=\sqrt{n}(\mathbb{P}_{n}-P_{VX})\left[m(V,X)\left(\sum_{(\bar{v}_{\mathit{1}},\bar{x})\in\mathfrak{V}_{\mathit{1}}\times\mathfrak{X}}(\hat{\varrho}_{\bar{v}_{\mathit{1}},\bar{x}}-\varrho_{\bar{v}_{\mathit{1}},\bar{x}})\mathbbm{1}_{(\bar{v}_{\mathit{1}},\bar{x})}(V_{\mathit{1}},X)\right)\right]

\displaystyle=\sum_{(\bar{v}_{\mathit{1}},\bar{x})\in\mathfrak{V}_{\mathit{1}}\times\mathfrak{X}}(\hat{\varrho}_{\bar{v}_{\mathit{1}},\bar{x}}-\varrho_{\bar{v}_{\mathit{1}},\bar{x}})\sqrt{n}(\mathbb{P}_{n}-P_{VX})\left[m(V,X)\mathbbm{1}_{(\bar{v}_{\mathit{1}},\bar{x})}(V_{\mathit{1}},X)\right],

which is
o_{P_{VX}}\left(1\right)
, because
\hat{r}
is consistent,

\sqrt{n}(\mathbb{P}_{n}-P_{VX})\left[m(V,X)\mathbbm{1}_{(\bar{v}_{\mathit{1}},\bar{x})}(V_{\mathit{1}},X)\right]=O_{{P_{VX}}}\left(1\right)

by the standard central limit theorem, and
|\mathfrak{V}_{\mathit{1}}\times\mathfrak{X}|
is finite. The terms (
120
), (
121
) can be handled similarly because
\left\lVert{{\hat{\mu}_{\mathcal{X}}}}\right\rVert_{\infty}\leq\left\lVert{{\hat{\mu}_{\mathcal{X}}}-\mu_{\mathcal{X}}}\right\rVert_{\infty}+\left\lVert{\mu_{\mathcal{X}}}\right\rVert_{\infty}=o_{P_{VX}}\left(1\right)+O\left(1\right)=O_{{P_{VX}}}\left(1\right)
by assumption. Thus
\sqrt{n}(\mathbb{P}_{n}-P_{VX})T_{1}=o_{P_{VX}}\left(1\right)
.

Term
T_{2}.\,\,
Same arguments as in
Lemma

9
apply, yielding
\sqrt{n}(\mathbb{P}_{n}-P_{VX})T_{2}

=o_{P_{VX}}\left(1\right)
, because
\hat{e}-e=o_{P_{VX}}\left(1\right)
— which we show at the end of this proof —, and
\hat{\gamma}_{\mathcal{V}}(c),\hat{p}_{V_{\mathit{2}}}(c)
are consistent.

Term
T_{3}.\,\,
Write

\displaystyle T_{3}(v,x)=

\displaystyle\,f(v,x,{\hat{\mu}_{\mathcal{X}}},\hat{\gamma}_{\mathcal{V}}(c))-f(v,x,\mu_{\mathcal{X}},\gamma_{\mathcal{V}}(c))

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\,f(v,x,{\hat{\mu}_{\mathcal{X}}},\hat{\gamma}_{\mathcal{V}}(c))-f(v,x,\mu_{\mathcal{X}},\hat{\gamma}_{\mathcal{V}}(c))

\displaystyle+f(v,x,\mu_{\mathcal{X}},\hat{\gamma}_{\mathcal{V}}(c))-f(v,x,\mu_{\mathcal{X}},\gamma_{\mathcal{V}}(c)).

(122)

As with
r
before, represent
\mu_{\mathcal{X}}(v_{\mathit{1}},x)=\sum_{(\bar{v}_{\mathit{1}},\bar{x})\in\mathfrak{V}_{\mathit{1}}\times\mathfrak{X}}\lambda_{\bar{v}_{\mathit{1}},\bar{x}}\mathbbm{1}_{(\bar{v}_{\mathit{1}},\bar{x})}(v_{\mathit{1}},x)
for some parameter
\lambda\in\real^{|\mathfrak{V}_{\mathit{1}}\times\mathfrak{X}|}
with
\lambda_{\bar{v}_{\mathit{1}},\bar{x}}\coloneqq\mu_{\mathcal{X}}(\bar{v}_{\mathit{1}},\bar{x})
; similarly, write

\displaystyle{\hat{\mu}_{\mathcal{X}}}(v_{\mathit{1}},x)

\displaystyle=\sum_{(\bar{v}_{\mathit{1}},\bar{x})\in\mathfrak{V}_{\mathit{1}}\times\mathfrak{X}}\hat{\lambda}_{\bar{v}_{\mathit{1}},\bar{x}}\mathbbm{1}_{(\bar{v}_{\mathit{1}},\bar{x})}(v_{\mathit{1}},x),

\displaystyle\hat{\lambda}_{\bar{v}_{\mathit{1}},\bar{x}}

\displaystyle\coloneqq{\hat{\mu}_{\mathcal{X}}}(\bar{v}_{\mathit{1}},\bar{x}).

By the linearity (
C.L
) of
f
and the mean value theorem,

\displaystyle\sqrt{n}(\mathbb{P}_{n}-P_{VX})\left[f(V,X,{\hat{\mu}_{\mathcal{X}}},\hat{\gamma}_{\mathcal{V}}(c))-f(V,X,\mu_{\mathcal{X}},\gamma_{\mathcal{V}}(c))\right]

\displaystyle=\sqrt{n}(\mathbb{P}_{n}-P_{VX})\left[f(V,X,{\hat{\mu}_{\mathcal{X}}}-\mu_{\mathcal{X}},\hat{\gamma}_{\mathcal{V}}(c))\right]

\displaystyle=\sqrt{n}(\mathbb{P}_{n}-P_{VX})\left[\sum_{(\bar{v}_{\mathit{1}},\bar{x})\in\mathfrak{V}_{\mathit{1}}\times\mathfrak{X}}(\hat{\lambda}_{\bar{v}_{\mathit{1}},\bar{x}}-\lambda_{\bar{v}_{\mathit{1}},\bar{x}})f(V,X,\mathbbm{1}_{\bar{v}_{\mathit{1}},\bar{x}},\hat{\gamma}_{\mathcal{V}}(c))\right]

\displaystyle=\sum_{(\bar{v}_{\mathit{1}},\bar{x})\in\mathfrak{V}_{\mathit{1}}\times\mathfrak{X}}(\hat{\lambda}_{\bar{v}_{\mathit{1}},\bar{x}}-\lambda_{\bar{v}_{\mathit{1}},\bar{x}})\sqrt{n}(\mathbb{P}_{n}-P_{VX})\left[f(V,X,\mathbbm{1}_{\bar{v}_{\mathit{1}},\bar{x}},\hat{\gamma}_{\mathcal{V}}(c))\right]

\displaystyle=\sum_{(\bar{v}_{\mathit{1}},\bar{x})\in\mathfrak{V}_{\mathit{1}}\times\mathfrak{X}}(\hat{\lambda}_{\bar{v}_{\mathit{1}},\bar{x}}-\lambda_{\bar{v}_{\mathit{1}},\bar{x}})\sqrt{n}(\mathbb{P}_{n}-P_{VX})\left[f(V,X,\mathbbm{1}_{\bar{v}_{\mathit{1}},\bar{x}},\gamma_{\mathcal{V}}(c))\right]

\displaystyle+\sum_{(\bar{v}_{\mathit{1}},\bar{x})\in\mathfrak{V}_{\mathit{1}}\times\mathfrak{X}}(\hat{\lambda}_{\bar{v}_{\mathit{1}},\bar{x}}-\lambda_{\bar{v}_{\mathit{1}},\bar{x}})(\mathbb{P}_{n}-P_{VX})\left[\partial_{\gamma}f(V,X,\mathbbm{1}_{\bar{v}_{\mathit{1}},\bar{x}},\tilde{\gamma}_{\mathcal{V}}(c))\right]

\displaystyle\times\sqrt{n}(\hat{\gamma}_{\mathcal{V}}(c)-\gamma_{\mathcal{V}}(c))

for some
\tilde{\gamma}_{\mathcal{V}}(c)
between
\gamma_{\mathcal{V}}(c)
and
\hat{\gamma}_{\mathcal{V}}(c)
. But this is
o_{P_{VX}}\left(1\right)
by the standard central limit theorem, and because
\sqrt{n}(\hat{\gamma}_{\mathcal{V}}(c)-\gamma_{\mathcal{V}}(c))=O_{{P_{VX}}}\left(1\right)
,
{\hat{\mu}_{\mathcal{X}}}
being consistent and
(\mathbb{P}_{n}-P_{VX})\left[\partial_{\gamma}f(V,X,\mathbbm{1}_{\bar{v}_{\mathit{1}},\bar{x}},\tilde{\gamma}_{\mathcal{V}}(c))\right]=O_{{P_{VX}}}\left(1\right)
by (
110
). For the term (
122
), apply the mean-value theorem twice to get

\displaystyle T_{3,1}(v,x)\coloneqq f(v,x,\mu_{\mathcal{X}},\hat{\gamma}_{\mathcal{V}}(c))-f(v,x,\mu_{\mathcal{X}},\gamma_{\mathcal{V}}(c))

\displaystyle=(\hat{\gamma}_{\mathcal{V}}(c)-\gamma_{\mathcal{V}}(c))\left\{\partial_{\gamma}f(v,x,\mu_{\mathcal{X}},\gamma_{\mathcal{V}}(c))+(\tilde{\gamma}_{\mathcal{V}}(c)-\gamma_{\mathcal{V}}(c))\partial_{\gamma}^{2}f(v,x,\mu_{\mathcal{X}},\tilde{\gamma}_{\mathcal{V}}^{\prime}(c))\right\}

for some
\tilde{\gamma}_{\mathcal{V}}^{\prime}(c)
between
\gamma_{\mathcal{V}}(c)
and
\hat{\gamma}_{\mathcal{V}}(c)
. Thus
\sqrt{n}(\mathbb{P}_{n}-P_{VX})T_{3,1}=o_{P_{VX}}\left(1\right)
by the standard central limit theorem,
\sqrt{n}
-consistency of
\hat{\gamma}_{\mathcal{V}}(c)
, and stochastic boundedness (
111
) of the second derivative. Hence,
\sqrt{n}(\mathbb{P}_{n}-P_{VX})T_{3}=o_{P_{VX}}\left(1\right)
.

Consistency of

\hat{e}.\,\,
Write

\displaystyle\hat{e}-e=

\displaystyle\,\mathbb{P}_{n}\partial_{\gamma}f(V,X,{\hat{\mu}_{\mathcal{X}}},\hat{\gamma}_{\mathcal{V}}(c))-P_{VX}\partial_{\gamma}f(V,X,{\hat{\mu}_{\mathcal{X}}},\hat{\gamma}_{\mathcal{V}}(c))

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\,(\mathbb{P}_{n}-P_{VX})\partial_{\gamma}f(V,X,{\hat{\mu}_{\mathcal{X}}},\hat{\gamma}_{\mathcal{V}}(c))

(123)

\displaystyle+P_{VX}\left[\partial_{\gamma}f(V,X,{\hat{\mu}_{\mathcal{X}}},\hat{\gamma}_{\mathcal{V}}(c))-\partial_{\gamma}f(V,X,\mu_{\mathcal{X}},\gamma_{\mathcal{V}}(c))\right].

(124)

As
\mu\mapsto\partial_{\gamma}f(v,x,\mu,\bar{\gamma})
is linear, (
123
) is
o_{P_{VX}}\left(1\right)
along similar arguments as
T_{3}
above; because we only need consistency, we only need the existence and stochastic boundedness (
111
) of the second derivative; no need for higher order derivatives. For the term (
124
), write it as

\displaystyle P_{VX}\left[\partial_{\gamma}f(V,X,{\hat{\mu}_{\mathcal{X}}},\hat{\gamma}_{\mathcal{V}}(c))-\partial_{\gamma}f(V,X,\mu_{\mathcal{X}},\hat{\gamma}_{\mathcal{V}}(c))\right]

\displaystyle+P_{VX}\left[\partial_{\gamma}f(V,X,\mu_{\mathcal{X}},\hat{\gamma}_{\mathcal{V}}(c))-\partial_{\gamma}f(V,X,\mu_{\mathcal{X}},\gamma_{\mathcal{V}}(c))\right].

By the linearity of
\mu\mapsto\partial_{\gamma}f(v,x,\mu,\hat{\gamma}_{\mathcal{V}}(c))
, the first term here is
o_{P_{VX}}\left(1\right)
, following a mean-value expansion, by the consistency of
{\hat{\mu}_{\mathcal{X}}}
and the assumed boundedness (
113
) of
P_{VX}\partial_{\gamma}f(V,X,\mu_{\mathcal{X}},\hat{\gamma}_{\mathcal{V}}(c))
. The second term is
o_{P_{VX}}\left(1\right)
by the same arguments under assumption (
113
) for
P_{VX}\partial_{\gamma}^{2}f(V,X,\mu_{\mathcal{X}},\hat{\gamma}_{\mathcal{V}}(c))
. Hence,
\hat{e}-e=o_{P_{VX}}\left(1\right)
. Finally, note that
\hat{e}-e^{\prime}=o_{P_{VX}}\left(1\right)
too as we claimed, because it is equal to (
123
).
∎
